## 2026-11-12, desktop surfaces (CW-12, CW-15 and CW-19)

- **Selected project.** The Connected tools view uses the directory-scoped authenticated SDK for the selected session directory, falling back to its repository path. This requires an additive directory prop through `dialog-settings-v2.tsx` and `subagents.tsx`, beyond the original two-line mount estimate. Without a selected project the view asks the user to open one; it never substitutes the engine process's working directory. Events invalidate only that directory's view. Obsolete requests are aborted, and an obsolete owner read cannot begin desktop discovery.
- **Saved results and actions.** Timeline metadata provides only a run pointer. The card loads the owned result, and updates trigger another owner read rather than displaying event payloads. An action captures its original run, session and engine before awaiting that read; changing conversations cannot move the action into another composer. Fix messages carry identifiers only and retain the engine's approval requirement. Dismiss is an explicit person action through the owner endpoint, without a model turn; that endpoint remains unavailable until CW-17.
- **Connected view actions and the import port.** Fix and worktree actions in Connected tools also reload the existing owned run and require its conversation to remain current; they preserve that run's provenance instead of registering it again. The desktop findings import helper remains a tested port. Its production VectorScope-panel caller belongs to the VectorScope owner and the later branch integration described in section 13; this branch does not edit that protected panel or claim the caller is wired.
- **Drafts.** Refreshing connected runs or unrelated global settings preserves an unsaved review draft. A change to the selected engine/project or the saved review values reloads those settings. Failed saves, cancellation and external-agent discovery are presented explicitly.
- **Validation limits.** The pre-change production measurement job passed nine cases at `578ecb02d` (run `43154405177`, job `130970869743`). Focused model, transport and action tests and app types have passed their recorded checkpoints. Browser regression sources cover selected-directory requests, draft focus, background owner reads and guest fallback; their runtime CI and the post-change measurement remain required. No performance improvement or complete-workflow success is claimed.

## 2026-11-12, CW-09 owner transport and nonstarting inventory

- **Inventory does not start MCP.** The owner approved an additive status-only method. `MCP.snapshot()` checks `InstanceState.has` and reads only an already-successful cache entry through `ScopedCache.getSuccess`; it never starts or waits for a lookup. Enabled configured servers without completed state are listed as unavailable, explicitly not yet connected, while configured disabled servers remain off. The method reads no stored credentials and makes no auth or connection probe. The existing `MCP.status()` behavior is unchanged. Two real-stdio-child tests cover a cold spawn count of zero and reuse of an existing live connection without another child or credential read.
- **HTTP transport must carry cancellation.** The production listener (`server/server.ts`, `serverLayer`) uses `node:http` and `NodeHttpServer`; the core wrapper re-exports that Node adapter without a Bun-specific listener. A separate in-process path, `Server.Default().app.fetch`, uses a Web `Request` and its `AbortSignal`. A disposable raw-TCP probe found that Node 24 emits response and socket `close` when a peer disconnects after sending a POST body, while Bun 1.3.14's `node:http` adapter emits neither. An ordinary request `close` only marks the consumed body and is never treated as cancellation.
- **The unsupported path refuses before work.** Owner review starts from an `IncomingMessage` on Bun's Node HTTP adapter return a typed disabled response before a transcript row, approval or review is started. This applies during admission even for a requested background review, because it may still be waiting for approval before detaching. Web `Request` starts remain supported on either runtime; native Node HTTP starts bind unfinished-response close, socket close and body abort to the hub's caller signal. Unknown source types refuse instead of lending a signal that cannot observe cancellation. Listing, reading, cancelling and importing findings remain available. The feature flag stays off by default.
- **Native Node acceptance is separate from Bun's HTTP compatibility.** `script/smoke-node-bundle.ts --connect-only` exercises the actual desktop engine bundle under Node, using a disposable project and fake provider. It requires explicit approval to stay pending after an ordinary POST body, rejects a concurrent start without an extra transcript message, and destroys a real TCP socket to require a settled cancelled run and no remaining approval. The normal Node bundle CI smoke includes the same checks. The Bun integration tests separately exercise actual Web `Request` cancellation and successful approval, and require the unsupported Node-adapter path to refuse before work. No cancelled-row assertion is removed or replaced by a claimed Bun socket-cancellation pass.

## 2026-11-09, CW-02 (core connect/policy.ts, findings.ts, frame.ts)

The contract was right about the code for CW-02. These are choices where it is silent, all on the fail-closed side, recorded so CW-03 to CW-08 build on the same reading:

- **`connect_model` for an unreadable price.** `reviewConsents` asks `connect_model` for a model whose cost kind is `unknown`, as for a priced one, because Vector cannot show it is free (I10). `plan` (Codium), `free` and `local` do not ask unless the repository picked the model.
- **Any spend-guard stop refuses.** `effectiveCap` refuses a review whenever `run.stopped` is set, whatever the stop reason, and when a run with a dollar limit has nothing left. A local model still has no dollar cap.
- **The check-command list is read, not copied.** `checkCommands` and `checkCommandRules` read VectorScope's list from the exported `reviewPermissionRules({ checks: true })`, since `CHECK_COMMANDS` is not exported, so both stay one list. The fix child's rules are VectorScope's bash rules with `allow` turned into `ask`, after a leading `bash * deny`.
- **Read-pattern selection is sound, not literal.** `readPatterns` drops a pattern only when a later requester allow covers it, or when a VectorScope deny covers it and no later VectorScope rule could reopen one of its files without a later requester allow. Under the build agent's defaults this gives `.docker/config.json`, `**/.docker/config.json`, `.kube/*` and `**/.kube/*`, as section 3.2 says; a user's own deny on `*.env.*` is kept, because VectorScope reads `.env.example`. `readBlock` is the exact per-file check the hub runs on what it finds.
- **Check 1 ignores letter case** and also fires when the `.vector` directory itself changed, because on a case-insensitive disk `.vector/Review.json` is the file the review reads.
- **Comparing working-tree reviews.** An earlier finding nothing matched is labelled `fixed` only while its file is still among the reviewed changes (when the caller passes them); otherwise it is left out with a note. A match with an earlier `dismissed` finding stays `dismissed`. Fixed findings are appended with an empty body. For a branch, a fixed finding keeps the bucket the earlier run gave it, or reads as `still-open` (from VectorScope's state) when that run does not name it.
- **The model-facing findings block is bounded.** `frame` lists every finding, but past about 40,000 characters later findings lose their bodies, and past half again as much they are counted; the text then points at `connect findings` with the run id. Results themselves still carry up to 200 whole findings.

## 2026-11-09, CW-03 (engine connect/adapter.ts, hub.ts, members.ts, adapters/index.ts)

The contract was right about the code for CW-03. These are the readings where it is silent or only sketches a shape, recorded so CW-04 to CW-09 build on them:

- **Names.** Engine modules re-export themselves as a namespace, so the adapter interface is `ConnectAdapter.Adapter` (with `ConnectAdapter.HelperHandle`, `ConnectAdapter.HandoffContext` and `ConnectAdapter.Factory`), not a bare `ConnectAdapter` interface. `adapters/index.ts` exports `ADAPTERS`, typed `Factory<never>[]`; CW-04 widens that type to the services its adapter needs and adds them to the hub's `deps`, where the layer graph checks them.
- **The context carries more.** Besides the contract's fields, `HandoffContext` has `spend` (the requester's run as `ConnectPolicy.RunSpend`, from `guard.running`, `guard.snapshot` and `guard.stopped`, for `effectiveCap`), `previous` (the `since` run) and `source` (the review a fix works from). `progress` takes an update without `at` and stamps it.
- **The hub asks `connect` itself.** Before it hands a run to the adapter, the hub asks `connect` with the pattern `<member>.<action>` (`vectorscope.review` for a review), once per run, so a user's `connect: deny` holds whatever an adapter does. The adapter asks `connect_model` and `connect_write` through `context.ask`; the hub asks `connect_model` once per request for each model, and `connect_write` every time with no "Always" patterns.
- **Read rules come first.** The hub runs the read-rule check before the adapter, so for a review it comes before section 4.2's checks 1 and 2: nothing is asked about a model for a review the read rules would refuse anyway, and no model runs in either order. The check walks every file and directory under the review directory except `.git`, enters a directory only when one of `ConnectPolicy.readPatterns` could match beneath it, follows a link to a directory inside the review directory under the link's own name (a link that leaves it is an external read, which review sessions are denied), confirms each match with `ConnectPolicy.readBlock`, and refuses (fail closed) on an error or past 500,000 entries.
- **What the hub refuses on its own.** Everything while `experimentalConnect` is off; `background: true` until CW-08; a requester session that is a hidden helper (`internal` set: no chains); a run whose directory does not exist; a member whose `describe()` fails, names another id, lacks the action, or is not ready; a `since` that is not a review of a comparable target in the requester's session; a fix whose run or finding ids are not there. The requester's rules are its agent's (`requester.agent`, else the default agent) merged with its session's. A run with no session asks through the caller's `ask` or is refused at its first question.
- **Request id and the cap.** The hub derives the request id itself (the last message the user wrote, newest first, through `Session.findMessage`), never from the caller; a run with no session is its own request. The per-request cap counts review runs saved in the session since that message plus the ledger's, each once; a refused run did no work and does not count.
- **Results.** A tool part or note stores the whole encoded run under `metadata.connect` (`ConnectHub.metadata`); `fromPart` reads it back only from a `connect` tool part or a synthetic text note. An adapter result the schema refuses fails the run; a refused, failed or cancelled review reads `not-reviewed`.
- **The helper handle** also refuses a session whose `internal` name is not the member's own (`vectorscope.…`), any call once the run is over, and more than 200 helper sessions. On a cancel the hub cancels every helper session the run prompted, through the caller's prompt calls.
- **Registry.** `members.ts` lists agents (not hidden ones), the named helpers, Parallel Workspaces, skills, saved workflows, MCP servers and the GitHub agent (from `.github/workflows/vector.yml`); members reached through a tool (`tasks`, `browser`, `computer`, `vector-cloud`, `tool:<id>`) only when the caller passes `ToolRegistry.ids()`, as the HTTP view will. Only adapters report `sendsTo`: an agent's or tool's destination follows the session's model, which the registry does not resolve (no model call, no catalog fetch). Vector Local turns a ready member off when it runs in a cloud or sends code past this computer and the listed model servers; MCP servers keep MCP's own "Off in Vector Local" status.

## 2026-11-09, review repairs for CW-01 to CW-03

A review of phase A1 found places where the contract reads the code wrongly or where CW-02 and CW-03 chose the open side. These readings refine, and where named supersede, the two entries above:

- **The read check covers the repository, not the folder.** Section 3.2 says the hub globs "the review directory". A reviewer reads the whole worktree with no external-directory question, VectorScope reviews from git's top level, and the read tool names files relative to the instance's worktree. So the hub checks from the instance worktree when it holds the folder, naming files relative to it; a folder of an instance outside git from the folder itself, also naming files relative to "/" as the read tool does there; a folder outside the instance (a task's worktree) from the nearest folder at or above it that holds `.git`. `ConnectPolicy.readBlock`'s doc now says this.
- **Links in the read check.** A link to a directory inside that root is walked again under its own name only when some rule could tell a file under it from the same file under its real name (`ConnectPolicy.sameBeneath`, over every pattern of the requester's read rules, the ask when none matches, and VectorScope's review rules); otherwise its files are checked where they lie. A package store linked from many workspaces is walked once. `MAX_SCAN` rises from 500,000 to 2,000,000 entries.
- **Read errors fail closed.** Only a folder that vanished during the walk (ENOENT, ENOTDIR) is skipped. Any other error, and any error on the top folder, refuses with `read-rule`, as the CW-03 entry already said.
- **Vector Local at admission.** The hub applies `ConnectMembers.gate` when it admits a run, so a member that reports ready but runs in a cloud or sends code past this computer and the listed model servers is refused with `unavailable` before anything is asked.
- **Requests.** With the flag off, `run` returns a refused run and reads, records and publishes nothing. A request the schema refuses ends refused with `input`, unrecorded, unpublished and with no adapter call; `run` never dies on it. A requester of kind `agent` whose session has a parent (a subagent) is refused with `not-allowed`; a workflow stage (`kind: workflow`) is not.
- **The request a run belongs to.** An automatic compaction after an overflow copies the user's last message into a new user message after its marker and summary. The hub skips that copy and walks on to the message it copied, so the per-request cap and the once-per-request `connect_model` answer hold across it. Text parts marked `ignored` are not the user's words, as for `SessionTaskSize.real`.
- **Saved runs.** Only a `connect` tool part in an assistant message carries a run. The synthetic-note branch is gone until CW-08 defines a note a client cannot write, because a client can send a synthetic text part with any metadata. A saved run that never ended (its tool part was cut off) reads as cancelled, "Vector stopped before this hand-off ended.". `runs({ sessionID })` merges that session's saved runs with the ledger's. `since` compares only with a review that completed or was partial. Signing saved runs, or checking their helper sessions, was not added: the first needs a key the engine keeps across restarts, and the second accepts reused ids. The guest-writable part route (`PATCH /session/:id/message/:mid/part/:pid`) is the server owner's to close.
- **Dismissed findings.** This supersedes the CW-02 bullet "A match with an earlier `dismissed` finding stays `dismissed`". `compareWorktree` no longer carries `dismissed` over from a saved run: the match reads `still-open`. Only CW-17's owner click dismisses.
- **`connect_write` before CW-07.** The hub refuses `connect_write` with `permission` while `PermissionPolicy.requiresApproval` does not cover it, because until CW-07 adds that line nothing keeps an allow rule, a saved approval or an auto-accept from answering. The hub test checks both states, so CW-07's line turns it into "asked every time" with no test change.
- **The helper handle** also refuses the requester's child sessions that existed when the run was admitted (an earlier run's helpers), a session another live run holds, and a prompt that sets `tools`, `budget` or `noReply` or runs as any agent but the member's own (`review` or `security` for VectorScope).
- **Remote MCP servers** are listed with place `your-cloud`, since their code runs on another host. MCP members keep MCP's own status and skip the registry's Vector Local gate, because MCP applies Vector Local to its servers itself.
- **Framing.** `escapeUntrusted` escapes every "<" and the characters that read like one (fullwidth, small, angle brackets and quotes) after dropping format characters (`\p{Cf}`), instead of only "<untrusted". A ">" is left as it is: without a "<" nothing reads as a tag.
- **Check 1 through links.** `editedSettings(changed, links)` also takes the settings paths whose real path differs from their own (CW-04 resolves `.vector` and each `SETTINGS_PATHS` entry with realpath, relative to the repository root). It fires when a change edits a link's target or a file beneath a linked directory, and when a link could not be resolved (fail closed). A target outside the repository cannot be edited by the changes and does not fire.
- **Not done: MCP status without starting MCP.** `MCP.status()`, the registry's MCP source in section 3.1, sets up MCP's per-instance state on first use, which starts every configured server. A status read that does not start MCP needs a change in engine mcp/index.ts, outside connect's files (sections 7.2 and 7.3), so `members()` keeps calling `MCP.status()` until the owner allows an additive read there.

## 2026-11-09, CW-04 (engine connect/adapters/vectorscope.ts, connect/review-prompt.ts)

The contract was right about the code for CW-04. These are readings where it is silent or where the code made one choice necessary, all on the fail-closed side:

- **Check 1 reads settings changes from the working tree, whatever the target.** `executeLocalReview` reads review.json, review.md, REVIEW.md and .gitattributes from the working tree for every non-PR target, so check 1 looks at every path that differs between the target's base (HEAD, or the merge base for a branch) and the working tree or the index, at untracked files, at the branch's own commits, and at untracked files git ignores under `.vector` and `.gitattributes` (an agent can write an ignored settings file as easily as a tracked one). A staged review therefore also stops when an unstaged edit touches a settings file. `changed`, used to compare with an earlier review, stays the target's own list. A git error, a missing base or a repository with no commits refuses with `input`, so check 1 never passes on what it could not read. Settings links are resolved as the A1 entry says; a settings path that resolves to the repository's top level itself counts as unresolved.
- **Readiness comes before check 1.** The hub calls `describe()` when it admits a run, and `describe()` reads review.json from the instance's worktree and resolves the review and triage models through `resolveReviewModel`, `MIN_CONTEXT` and `capabilities.toolcall`. A model that cannot run refuses the run with `unavailable` before check 1. So does an edit under `.vector` that voids workspace trust, when the review model's provider comes from the project's own config. No model runs either way, and the result is never clean. "Off" is reported when Vector Local is on and the resolution error carries the provider's own "off in Vector Local" wording; every other failure is `unavailable`.
- **One network note.** For a Vector Codium review model, the provider's `getModel` may wait for vectordev.ai's Codium catalog when the account has none yet. That is an existing destination, reached through `resolveReviewModel` as section 3.1 names it; connect adds none.
- **The model the review uses is the one connect checked.** `executeLocalReview` resolves its models again. Connect wraps `resolveModel` so that a review or triage model other than the one check 2 resolved (and asked `connect_model` about) fails the review with a plain message instead of running.
- **Cap and consent over two models.** The dollar cap is worked out as local (no cap) only when every model is local; otherwise for the priced, unknown or other non-local kind. Personal Brain documents go only when every model sends to this computer or a model server, never in a shared review. A result's `sendsTo` is the review model's, or the triage model's when that one sends the diff further, with a note naming both when they differ.
- **Results.** Check 1 firing gives state `completed`, verdict `incomplete`, the settings note and no `sendsTo`. Nothing to review gives `completed` and `not-reviewed`. A review that could not start or whose every reviewer failed gives `failed` and `not-reviewed`. A partial outcome, a pass that failed, timed out or stopped, or an unreviewed file gives state `partial`. A cached outcome carries no sessions and no cost, since this run made and spent nothing. Any Codium involvement shows as plan usage with no dollars. Stdout is read only for `cached`; every note is Vector's own.
- **What the adapter refuses itself.** Through CW-06 it lists only the `review` action (`fix` arrives with CW-07). It refuses with `unavailable` a run with no session to parent the reviewer sessions, and a run with no prompt calls to build the helper handle from. The bridge also refuses prompt options, as well as every member but `prompt` and `cancel`.
- **Layer graph.** The adapter's services join the hub's dependencies through `connect/adapters` `DEPS`. `ConnectHub.node` depends on both; `ConnectHub.nodeWith` (tests with fake adapters) keeps the hub's own `ConnectHub.DEPS`. None of the added nodes reaches the prompt loop or the tool registry.
- **Owner.** VectorScope's member carries owner `vectorscope`: the code is on main, but it is VectorScope's.

## 2026-11-11, CW-05 (engine tool/connect.ts and connect.txt; registry.ts, agent.ts, session/memory.ts, connect/members.ts)

The contract was right about the code for CW-05. These are readings where it is silent, or where the shared-file line counts came out slightly different:

- **Shared-file lines.** registry.ts has 7 added lines, not 6: the import anchor holds two imports (`ConnectTool` and `ConnectHub`, the second for the `ConnectHub.node` deps line). session/memory.ts changes the two `taint` call sites as the contract says, but at 120 columns the formatter splits the first condition, so that hunk is 1 line out and 4 in. agent.ts has exactly the 2 lines. Every hunk sits at its named anchor, more than two unchanged lines from the hands and vector-at-mention hunks in the same files.
- **Who the tool refuses, before any hand-off.** Any agent but `build`; a session with a parent (from `ctx.parentID` or the stored session) or with `internal` set; a session `FreeModelGuests.marked` reports; and, for `review` only, a Light or chat request (with the escalate hint) or a request the engine did not size (fail closed: the build agent's root requests are always sized, so only an unexpected caller gets here). These refusals are not runs: nothing is recorded, published or counted.
- **The size gate covers `review` only.** `findings`, `status` and `cancel` read or stop what this session already holds and spend nothing, so they also work on a Light request (the user asking "what did it find?" is usually Light). Subagents, helpers and guests are refused for every action.
- **Only a review saves a run.** The tool saves `ConnectHub.metadata(run)` in its part for `review` alone. `findings`, `status` and `cancel` save `{ action, run }` under other keys, because the hub reads a run from any `connect` tool part, and a copy of an earlier review in a later part would count toward the later request's cap.
- **Arguments the action does not take are refused**, not ignored: `run` or `findings` on a review, and `base` with a target other than `branch`. `max_cost` is held above 0 by the parameter schema (an invalid-arguments error) and checked again with `ConnectPolicy.maxCostInput`. A `since` that is not a run id goes to the hub, which refuses the request with `input`.
- **Output.** A review's output is `ConnectFrame.frame(result)`; the run id line ("Pass it as `since` ...") is added only for a completed or partial review. `findings` puts the selected findings, with bodies and suggestions, in the same untrusted block under the same preamble; `status` uses only Vector's words (summary, verdict, where the diff went, cost), never finding text.
- **Connect's own tool is not a member.** members.ts adds `connect` to the tools that are not listed as `tool:<id>` members, now that `ToolRegistry.ids()` includes it while the flag is on.
- **The hub is built with the registry whatever the flag.** `ConnectHub.node` is in the registry's deps, so its layer (and the VectorScope adapter's, which only captures services) is built with the flag off too. It reads, records and publishes nothing then, and the tool is not offered.
- **Known edge of the name rule.** `taint` reads a tool part named `connect` as a subagent's report by its name, as the contract says. A custom or plugin tool a user names exactly `connect` is therefore held as `subagent` rather than `mcp`, flag on or off. Both hold memory writes for the user; only the stated reason differs.

## 2026-11-11, CW-06 (engine command/index.ts and command/template/vectorscope.txt)

The contract was right about the code for CW-06. These are readings where it is silent:

- **Shared-file lines.** command/index.ts has 16 added lines, not about 14: two imports (`RuntimeFlags` and the template), `VECTORSCOPE` in `Default`, the `flags` line, and the 12-line block after `/review` with its comment. Adding `RuntimeFlags.node` made the `node` line longer than 120 columns, so the formatter splits it over five lines. No other branch has a hunk in this file.
- **Never a subtask, no agent or model of its own.** The command sets `subtask: false`, so it never moves into a child session, where the connect tool refuses every caller. It pins no agent and no model, as `/review` pins none: it runs on the agent and model the user has chosen, and only the build agent is offered the tool. The template tells any other agent to say that reviews run from the build agent, and not to review the changes itself. `size: "standard"` comes before what the arguments say, so the request is Work whatever the user typed; a sender's own size (a Quick mode pick sends `chat`) still comes first, as for every command, and the tool then refuses with its Light message.
- **The template does not guess.** The user's words reach it only where `$ARGUMENTS` stands. Anything other than nothing, `uncommitted`, `staged`, `branch` or `branch <base>` makes the agent name the accepted forms instead of calling the tool. The template carries no shell snippet, `@` reference or numbered placeholder, since a built-in `source: "command"` template runs its snippets without asking; the test pins that.
- **Names.** A config command named `vectorscope` keeps the name, as one named `review` does. With connected tools on, a skill named `vectorscope` is hidden by the built-in command, as skills named `init` or `review` already are; with them off nothing changes.
- **Testing `/command`.** The test serves the production instance handler group over the real command service, with the other instance services mocked, instead of the whole HTTP app. The full app, the HttpApi exerciser and the other suites that build the command layer stay in CI.

## 2026-11-11, review repairs for phase A2 (CW-04 to CW-06)

A review of phase A2 found eight things; all eight held up against the code (the two on the registry's name check are one finding). These readings refine, and where named supersede, the CW-04 and CW-05 entries above:

- **A stored outcome sent nothing.** This supersedes the CW-04 bullet's "A cached outcome carries no sessions and no cost": it also carries no `sendsTo`, since section 3.2's `sendsTo` is where this run sent the code, and the triage-model note is left out. It gets the note "Connect sent no code this time: these findings are from that earlier review." (`VectorScopeAdapter.CACHED_NOTE`).
- **Check 1 runs three times.** Once before anything else, again right before `executeLocalReview` (after the `connect_model` question, which can wait on the user for any length of time, and after the lazy load), and once more after it returns. An edit found the second time gives the plain check-1 result with no model run. One found the third time gives `completed`, `incomplete`, no findings, the settings note plus "They changed after connect checked them, while the review ran, so what it found is not shown." (`SETTINGS_CHANGED_NOTE`), and keeps the run's sessions, cost and `sendsTo`, since they were made, spent and sent. A git error in the third check counts as an edit. What is left: an edit made and undone between VectorScope's own read of its settings and the third check. Those reads are in cli/cmd/review.ts (VectorScope's, section 7.1), so connect cannot see the bytes the review read.
- **Check 1 reads raw bytes too (`drifted`).** git's view of the working tree can be told to look away: an assume-unchanged or skip-worktree entry (sparse checkout sets skip-worktree), `core.ignoreStat`, `core.trustctime` with `core.checkStat`, or a clean filter defined in `.git/config` with an attribute in `.git/info/attributes`. (The engine's git already runs with `core.fsmonitor=false`.) So check 1 also compares every settings file's bytes in the working tree, read as the review reads them, with the same file at the target's base, through `git cat-file --batch --follow-symlinks`. A file that is there is looked up under its real path, so links and a case-insensitive disk read the same file on both sides. One that is not there is looked up under its own name, with git following links inside the base tree. Carriage returns before a line feed are ignored, so a checkout's line-ending conversion is not an edit. A file on one side only, or one git cannot answer for, counts as edited. A base link that leaves the repository counts only once the file no longer leads out of it, so replacing such a link with a folder in the repository edits every settings path beneath it. Settings files under a smudge filter, `ident` or `working-tree-encoding` now always fire check 1 (fail closed; none of them is a usual setup for these four files). On a case-insensitive disk an edit to `.vector/review.md` is listed under both `review.md` and `REVIEW.md`, since both names read that file.
- **VectorScope's branch state file is trusted only when connect saw VectorScope write it.** The state file (`<git dir>/vector/review/<branch>.json`) is plain JSON, and a shell can write it. The adapter keeps, in memory, a hash of each state file as VectorScope left it after one of connect's branch reviews, and passes `full: true` to `executeLocalReview` whenever the file differs, or connect has no hash for it. A stored outcome (`cached`) or an earlier head (an incremental range) then always comes from what VectorScope wrote. The cost: the first connect branch review in an engine process, and the first after a restart or after a `vector review` of that branch, reviews the whole branch, and VectorScope's state starts over from that review, so findings an earlier `vector review` left open do not come back as still open or fixed in it. Re-reviews (`since`) in the same process keep VectorScope's labels. What is left: a write in the instant between VectorScope's own write and connect's read of the hash.
- **The registry knows its own connect tool.** This supersedes the CW-05 "Known edge of the name rule" bullet. The offer filter in `tools()` runs only while `experimentalConnect` is on, and then offers the built-in tool to build alone and a custom or plugin tool whose id is `connect` to no agent, so it can neither replace the built-in nor write `connect` tool parts the hub would read as runs. With the flag off a custom tool named `connect` is offered as it was before CW-05. registry.ts has 3 lines at that anchor (a comment and a two-line condition), not 1, so 9 added lines in all; the hunk is still at the anchor named in 7.2. Left as it is: a `connect` tool part a custom tool wrote earlier, while the flag was off, is read as a run only if its metadata decodes as a whole run under `ConnectHub.METADATA_KEY`.
- **Memory holds an unknown connect tool as an outside tool.** `taint` counts a `connect` tool part as a connected tool's report only when it carries the built-in tool's metadata (a run under `ConnectHub.METADATA_KEY`, or `action` `review`, `findings`, `status` or `cancel`). Any other part named `connect`, and a built-in part still pending, is held as an outside tool (`mcp`, the stronger hold), so `carried()` passes it up from a subagent again. The memory.ts hunk is one block at the two call sites: a five-line helper with a one-line comment between them, the first condition over parts instead of names, and the second call site's line back as it is on main with the check on the line after it. A custom tool that writes the built-in's metadata shape is still held as `subagent`; both hold memory writes.
- **What the `connect_model` question shows.** The hub puts the model, where the diff goes and the cap in the ask's `metadata.description`, but no client read it: the desktop dock showed only the patterns (the model id) and the TUI only "Call tool connect_model". The desktop now shows it, for `connect`, `connect_model` and `connect_write` asks that carry a connect run id: new app features/connect/consent.ts (`connectConsentDetails`, with its test) and two additive hunks in app pages/session/composer/session-permission-dock.tsx, a file neither 7.1 nor 7.2 lists. One hunk is an import after the `Icon` import, the other a row after the tool-description row, each at least three unchanged lines from the hunks Hands has in that file. No i18n key was added, since app i18n has a parity test across every language; the sentence is Vector's English, as the hub writes it. **The TUI is not done:** tui/src/routes/session/permission.tsx is outside 7.3's safe zone and Hands edits it. The request to its owner is a `connect`/`connect_model` branch that shows `metadata.description` (or it joins CW-11). Until then `VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL_CONNECT` stays off by default, and CW-25 must not turn it on first.
- **Dead field.** `Checked.resolved` is gone; `Checked` is `ConnectPolicy.ReviewModel`, and the `resolveModel` wrapper keeps comparing model names.

## 2026-11-11, CW-07 (connect fix and re-review; agent.ts, permission-policy.ts, task/isolation.ts)

The contract was right about the code, with two test files it did not expect to change. These are the readings where it is silent or where the code made a choice necessary, all on the fail-closed side:

- **Two tests changed with the behaviour.** The phase A1 note said the hub's `connect_write` test would flip "with no test edit". It could not: once `requiresApproval` covers `connect_write`, a run that asks through `Permission` waits for an answer, so the test now answers it ("always", then "once": the second question is asked again) instead of awaiting a run that would never end. engine test/agent/agent.test.ts lists every native subagent; `connect_fix` is one, so the list gains it (one import, one comment line, the list line).
- **agent.ts has 36 added lines, not about 16.** Two imports, a `connectFix` helper before the agents (it needs `defaults` and `readDefaults`), the 12-line agent block after `general`, and a 14-line rebuild after the config loop, beside the research agents' rebuild. The config loop merges an agent's own config rules after its permission and may set `hidden`, `mode` and `name`: a rule after the fixed part, an unhidden agent the task tool would take from a model, or a renamed one the merge check would miss. The rebuild keeps the name, `native`, `hidden` and `subagent`, and puts the fixed part last again; the agent's own rules and the user ceiling only narrow it. Every hunk is at least 20 unchanged lines from Hands' hunks.
- **The fixed part keeps the requester's reads.** Section 4.3 says the fixed part "denies everything, then allows read, …". A plain `read: allow` last would let the child read `.env` and the other files its requester is asked about, and would override the user's own read denies. So `ConnectPolicy.fixAgentRules` denies everything, allows the fix tools, re-applies the agent defaults' read rules, asks for the check commands (every other command denied), then applies the user's rules only where they narrow (a deny anywhere; an ask or allow only for a fix tool, never for the shell or the network), and last denies `network_command`, `webfetch`, `websearch`, `task` and MCP resources (`read mcp:*`). With a user's own `"*": "allow"` the child reads `.env` as the build agent then does, and no more. Not covered: a requester's session-level ask rules reach no subagent (`deriveSubagentSessionPermission` passes only denies and external-directory rules), the fix child included.
- **Where the merge asks.** `TaskIsolation.merge` (task/isolation.ts, a 7.3 safe zone) asks `connect_write` with the diff, before its `edit` ask, for every agent merge of a `connect_fix` child: a model's `task_manage merge` included, since tool/task-manage.ts (7.1) passes `ctx.ask` through as `By.ask`, whose type now admits `connect_write`. The owner's merge from the Tasks pane asks nothing, as section 4.3 says. Only the owner may include stacked changes, so a task stacked on a fix child cannot carry its changes past the question. The question names the review run (from connect's own title, "Fix 2 findings from cr_…") as `runID`, so the desktop dock shows its sentence.
- **The tool holds the task tool.** `worktree` calls `TaskTool.execute` from tool/connect.ts, as the research tool does, through a launcher the call lends the hub (`Call.launch`, passed on as `HandoffContext.launch` to the vectorscope adapter only, for a fix only, and refused once the run is over). The task tool gets the call's context without `callID` and with its metadata writes dropped, so the child's metadata never lands in the connect part and task recovery never settles that part; the child's card is in the Tasks pane. The child's checked answer is read from its last message (`TaskStructured.latest`) and decoded as data (`ConnectPolicy.fixAnswer`: only ids it was given, each once).
- **Owned paths.** They are the owned-path rule's, named relative to the requester's directory, as the task tool reads `owned_paths`; a finding whose file lies outside that folder is left out with a reason. The hub also checks the requester's rules over the chosen findings' files (`ConnectPolicy.fixTouches`) once the review is found, so an edit deny on an owned file refuses before any question; the blanket check before the lookup is unchanged.
- **What a fix takes on.** `ConnectPolicy.fixable` leaves out, each with a reason in the notes, findings already fixed or dismissed, outside-diff and still-open ones, files under `.github` or `.vector`, and files no longer among the target's changes (read again with git, since a saved run keeps no changed-file list). Nothing fixable refuses with `input`, and a worktree fix with no launcher refuses with `unavailable`, both before any question.
- **The tool.** `fix` needs a Work or Thorough request, as `review` does (`findings`, `status` and `cancel` keep working on a Light one). `mode` defaults to `here`, and a review refuses it. A fix's run is saved in its tool part like a review's; it never counts toward the per-request review cap. The result has no verdict; its findings read `fixing` (here: every one taken on; worktree: those the child says it fixed) or `open`. The child's own report reaches the model only in this call's output, as the task tool renders it, never in the saved run. The output says to review again with `since` when the fixed review was of uncommitted changes, and without it otherwise, since `since` compares only reviews of the same kind.
- **Memory.** A fix refused before the hub saves `{ action: "fix", refused }`; memory.ts names only `review`, `findings`, `status` and `cancel` as the built-in tool's actions, so that part is held as an outside tool (`mcp`, the stronger hold). Left as it is, to keep memory.ts's shared hunk unchanged; both hold memory writes for the user.
- **Left open.** (1) A 1.99.111 client attached to a newer engine has its own copy of `requiresApproval` without `connect_write`, so its auto-accept could answer it; the same holds today for `agent_marker`, `prompt_source` and `research_keep`, and the desktop app and the TUI ship with the engine. (2) `task_manage resume` of a stopped or failed fix child runs it again with no new `connect_write` question (task-manage.ts is on 7.1); its changes still reach the checkout only through a merge that asks. (3) Naming the agent in `invokedAgents` skips the user's `task` rules for it, as the contract's mechanism does; `connect`, `connect_write` and the child's fixed rules still apply. (4) `connect_fix` is in the agent list, hidden, whatever the flag: clients and the registry leave hidden agents out, and nothing launches it with the flag off. (5) The TUI still shows `connect_write` as a plain tool call (the A2 entry's open item).

## 2026-11-11, CW-08 (background runs; engine connect/hub.ts, connect/note.ts, connect/adapter.ts, connect/adapters/vectorscope.ts, tool/connect.ts; task/notify.ts)

The contract was right about the code. These are the readings where it is silent, or where an earlier entry left CW-08 to decide:

- **Where a background run's result is kept.** Section 3.2 keeps a run's result in its `connect` tool part, "or in the connect note for a run with no tool call", and the A1 entry left notes without runs until CW-08 gave them a form a client cannot write. A background review started by the tool has a tool part, so the hub writes the ended run into that part when the run ends. It uses `SubagentLifecycle.patchPart`, the exported helper the task tool uses, which waits until the processor has completed the part. The `connect` note only names its run (`connectNotification: { runID, state, quiet }`) and never carries it, and `fromPart` still reads runs only from connect tool parts, so a forged note gives a client nothing. Runs with no tool call (CW-09's owner service, CW-18) have no part. Whoever adds them needs a store a client cannot write.
- **Every connect note is quiet in CW-08.** Section 3.2 makes a clean result quiet, and lets blocking findings start a turn only with repair on. The after-run table makes `report` "a quiet note with the result". Repair arrives with CW-09 and CW-13, so every note is recorded with `noReply` and `taskQuiet`: findings, incomplete, failed and cancelled ones too. No note starts a turn or spends anything on its own. A busy parent reads the note at its next step, because the window delivers it at once and the loop reads its history each step. An idle parent reads it with the user's next message. `ConnectNote.wakes(run, { repair })` is the hook CW-13 turns on. session/task-size.ts needs no change, because quiet notes belong to no request (`groups()` skips them). CW-13's waking repair note will need `reported()` there to know `connectNotification`, so a Chat request the note joins is sized as work. That is the point to coordinate with Hands, and it is not touched here.
- **The caller waits for `working`.** `HandoffContext.working` is new and optional. The VectorScope adapter calls it right before `executeLocalReview`, after both check-1 passes, check 2 and the `connect_model` question. So every question attaches to the tool call, every refusal comes back in the call's output, and only the review itself goes on in the background. A run that ends before `working` is reported by the caller, and no note follows. Once the caller stops waiting, the hub stops streaming into the call, and any later ask goes through Permission in the requester's session with its merged rules. The caller's abort before `working` cancels the run. After it, the abort does not: the run is handed off, as background subagents are spared when a turn stops. Which side reports the end is decided in one step, so the end is reported exactly once.
- **Per action, not per member.** The review action declares `background: true`. Fix stays foreground: `here` returns at once, and `worktree` waits on the task tool and its merge question. The hub refuses `background` with `input` for an action that declares false. It refuses with `unavailable` a background run that has no session or no prompt calls to report through. The tool refuses `background` on a fix with its usual "A fix takes run, findings and mode."
- **Spend.** For a background run the hub reads the requester's spend as outside the run (`running: false`), so `ConnectPolicy.effectiveCap` caps the review at what the run has left from `guard.snapshot`, or $0.50 with no dollar limit. That holds even while the turn is still going, since the review may outlive it. The hub also refuses a background run whose requester's run stopped on its budget, with `budget`, before any question. The adapter's own check still refuses it too.
- **The job.** Its id is the run id, its type `connect`, its metadata `{ parentSessionId, background: true, runID, member, action }`, and it is not promotable. Existing readers of that metadata then do this: deleting the session or a workspace warp cancels the run; a settings reload waits for it (`disposeIdleInstances`); stopping the session's turn spares it, as it spares background subagents; and TaskNotify counts it as a running sibling, so while it runs an idle parent's other notes wait up to the hold (5 s) rather than the quiet time. A job stopped because its instance is going away (`interrupted`) sends no note and writes nothing into its part. The part then reads as cut off (`STOPPED_SUMMARY`), as after a restart.
- **Shared-file lines.** task/notify.ts has 12 added lines and 1 changed line: the `connect` kind, the `runID` field with its comment, one early-return line in `metadata()`, and a new `connectNotification` function after it. The function exists so the existing nested ternary is not re-indented. No other branch has hunks in notify.ts. memory.ts is unchanged. A turn that reads a connect note already has the connect tool part (with `metadata.connect`) in the history `taint` reads, so memory is held as `subagent`.
- **Hub dependencies.** `BackgroundJob.node`, `TaskNotify.node` and `Database.node` join `ConnectHub.DEPS`. All three were already in the tool registry's graph, through the task tool.
- **Tests.** The busy-parent case is proven at TaskNotify's level, as test/task/notify.test.ts proves it. With a 20-second window, the note reaches a busy parent at once, with `noReply`, into the running turn's history. The cap is proven twice: once with the real VectorScope adapter and fake `runReview`/`resolveModel` (the `maxCost` the review receives), and once with a fake member (the spend the hub passes). No test runs the real prompt loop.
- **Left open.** (1) `vector run` does not wait for a background connect review. Its tracker knows only task and shell notes (cli/cmd/run.ts and run/background-wait.ts, outside connect's files), so a headless run may exit first, and the run is then lost as after a restart. (2) Reverting the message that started a background review drops its note (`launchID`) but does not stop the review, because session/revert.ts cancels only task and shell jobs. (3) The tool part's title stays "VectorScope review: in the background" after the run ends, because `patchPart` writes metadata only. The run in that metadata is the ended one.

## 2026-11-11, review repairs for phase A3 (CW-07 and CW-08)

A review of phase A3 found ten things; all ten held up against the code. These readings refine, and where named supersede, the CW-02, CW-07 and CW-08 entries above:

- **No command runs in a fix child.** This supersedes the CW-02 bullet "The fix child's rules are VectorScope's bash rules with `allow` turned into `ask`", the contract's "bash outside the check list is denied" (CW-07) and I3's "Commands in fix children ask per command". A test or typecheck runs code the child may have just written in an owned file (a `fetch` a finding asked for, say), the shell keeps the network unless Vector Local's Web access is None, and auto-accept or a saved approval such as `bun test *` can answer a `bash` ask in a child session. Nothing in connect's files can run a child's commands without the network (that is tool/shell.ts, section 7.1), so `ConnectPolicy.fixAgentRules` denies every command and the child is not offered the shell at all. The requester runs the checks after `here` or after the merge. The fix prompt (agent/prompt/connect-fix.txt) and `ConnectFix.brief` say so. `checkCommands` and `checkCommandRules` stay exported, read from VectorScope's list, but no fix child gets them.
- **The fix child reads as its requester does.** This supersedes the CW-07 bullet "The fixed part keeps the requester's reads". The fixed part now takes the fix tools exactly as the build agent has them once its own config and the ceiling are applied (`agent.build.permission`, `ceiling.agent.build`), each of its rules that covers a fix tool in its order, instead of the agent defaults' read rules. The child's own rules (the user's, `agent.connect_fix`'s and the ceiling's) only narrow: an ask for a fix tool, after which the requester's denies come again so no ask reopens one, then a deny anywhere. An allow written for `connect_fix` no longer opens anything. Still not covered: a requester's session-level ask rules (`deriveSubagentSessionPermission`, task.ts on 7.1, passes only denies to any subagent).
- **No config picks the fix child's model, prompt, steps or options.** The rebuild after the config loop replaces the whole agent, so a trusted project's `agent.connect_fix` or `.vector/agent/connect_fix.md` sets neither model, variant, prompt, description, steps, temperature, top-p nor options. The child runs on its parent's model, as the task tool picks it, under the prompt that frames the findings as data. Turning it off (`disable`) still removes it.
- **No agent's task call starts or lists the fix child.** This supersedes CW-07's left-open item (4). Every agent whose rules let `task` launch `connect_fix` gets `task: { connect_fix: deny }` after the rebuild, as the research rebuild does for the judge, so the task tool's "Set subagent_type to one of" list no longer names it and a model's call is refused before any question. Connect's own launch names it in `invokedAgents`, which passes that deny. Left open: `vector agent list` prints every agent with its rules, hidden ones included, so it shows `connect_fix` and the new deny with the flag off. Registering the agent only while `experimentalConnect` is on needs `RuntimeFlags.node` in agent.ts's dependencies, a changed line in a 7.2 file away from its anchors.
- **Memory holds a refused fix as the built-in tool's.** This supersedes the CW-07 "Memory" bullet. memory.ts's `connected` helper (connect's own CW-05 hunk) also names `fix`, so a fix the tool refused before the hub, saved as `{ action: "fix", refused }`, holds memory as `subagent`. A custom tool's `connect` part with any other action is still held as `mcp`.
- **Background reviews share one allowance.** This refines the CW-08 "Spend" bullet. The guard stops counting a background review once the turn that started it ends, so each one holds, from the moment it is admitted, the most any review admitted then could be capped at (a priced model under connect's $2 and the tool's `max_cost`), lowered to its own cap when the adapter reports it through `working({ maxCostUsd })`, and to nothing for a review with no dollar cap. Every review admitted while others of the same session are going reads what they hold as spent (`RunSpend.heldUsd`), foreground ones included, and `effectiveCap` takes it off what the run has left, or off the one $0.50 outside a run with no dollar limit. A review that finds nothing left is refused with `budget` ("Background reviews of this conversation that are still going hold what the budget has left …"). The held amount double-counts what a background review already spent inside the turn, on the strict side. When CW-09 adds `connect.review.max_cost_usd`, the hub's `reserve` must pass it as the adapter will.
- **A background run is reported once.** `detach` reads the run it returns in the same step as it decides who reports the end, so a run that ends at the next yield is never also framed in the call's output.
- **Reverting the call stops the review.** This supersedes CW-08's left-open item (2). The `connect` job's metadata names the call that started it (`parentMessageId`, `callID`), and session/revert.ts also cancels jobs of type `connect`: one token in a file neither 7.1 nor 7.2 lists, and no other branch has a hunk there.
- **`vector run` waits for background reviews.** This supersedes CW-08's left-open item (1). cli/cmd/run/background-wait.ts gains `trackConnect` and `isConnectID`, and its "stopped waiting" line counts reviews apart. cli/cmd/run.ts calls `trackConnect` beside `trackShell`, and leaves connect run ids out of its task-stop call on timeout, because the task manager refuses a whole stop when one id is not a task. Neither file is on 7.1 or 7.2, and no other branch (hands, codium, vectorscope, vector-at-mention, cloud-workspaces, process-finish, release-coordination, finish-checks) has a hunk in them.
- **The merge hint only when there is something to merge.** A worktree fix's output tells the model to merge only when the child finished with changes in its worktree (`changed` or `diverged`). A child moved to the background gets "merge once its task note arrives", and one that changed no files gets no hint.
- **Left open.** An edit in any session can start the project's own tooling: the formatter after an edit, and the language servers it touches, which may load a linter's config or run a build script. A fix child that edits an owned file such tooling runs could reach the network that way. That is the format and LSP services' (outside connect's files), or the owned-path rule could leave tool configuration files out; neither is done here.

## 2026-11-12, CW-13 source checkpoint (report mode qualified; repair held)

- **Admission precedes the observable idle.** `ConnectOwner` registers an instance hook during runtime/server construction. The hook installs an awaited `EventV2` listener before `/event` can register its request queue. An observed busy-to-idle transition admits the real `connect` background job (`cr_` ID) and an assistant tool row before later listeners see idle. A cold or recovery idle without a busy transition starts nothing. The automatic row refers to the original human message, with an engine-created round/reviewed-through marker; no new human request is fabricated.
- **The notification anchor needs one more private port.** A blocking after-run repair can share a `TaskNotify` delivery with another task's note. Applying a cap only through the connected note's `ops` would be bypassed when another note leads that batch. The additive `task/notify.ts` change therefore aggregates private `connectRepair` constraints from every waking connected note. `canReply`, already called inside the actual runner's admission before the prompt loop, checks the captured hub request again and applies the combined bound. The authority is an internal effect and budget, never message metadata, a finding field or public input. Note lead, agent and model selection are unchanged.
- **Repair retains the remaining allowance.** The ended run's remaining dollar allowance (or $0.50 without a limit), less the review's accounted cost, bounds the repairing turn; remaining explicit step limits are kept too. An unknown review cost or exhausted allowance makes the note quiet. A new human request revokes the older automatic repair at admission.
- **Protected prerequisite remains unresolved.** `guard.tighten` may arm the next run when no run is active. Cancelling between admission and `begin` needs a release that cannot remove a later owner's budget or lend an unused bound to a different runner. The guard currently lacks that capability. The engine checks for an optional private lease and records repair quietly when it is absent. A protected-file extension is under separate owner review; no guard implementation has been changed by this checkpoint. The experimental flag stays off, and repair is not accepted as complete while that prerequisite and its actual cancellation tests remain unresolved.
- **Qualification is specific.** The initial focused runtime proved the real job/idle ordering, passive consent refusal, ended-budget review cap, guest exclusion and cold-idle exclusion. A real headless CLI now performs read, write, completion and an actual VectorScope review through the disposable provider, and observes its durable clean `cr_` result before exit. A separate baseline proved a blocking note wakes the actual owner. The complete focused after-run file passed all 22 cases (26.52 seconds), and the engine typecheck passed (100.67 seconds). These include both notification lead directions, new-human/guest/setting revocation, missing session or report, a genuinely busy parent, and the absent-capability quiet path. Test-only capability injection exercises aggregation with the real guard's tightening and begin operations; it does not certify a production lease or cancellation cleanup.

## 2026-11-12, CW-14 (settled task worktree targets)

- **The public target is a task id.** `Connect.Target` adds `{ kind: "task", task: SessionID }`; the model tool accepts `task` instead of `target`/`base`. The caller still supplies its own directory. A private isolation record selects the actual checkout and review base; findings and result text cannot pick a path. A task must belong to that exact launching conversation and have saved worktree changes. Running, copy-mode, deleted, unsaved, redirected or stale tasks refuse. No review falls back to the parent checkout.
- **The isolation anchor is read-only and held.** The additive `TaskIsolation.review` port reuses the existing task mutex, private registry validation and Git identity checks. It checks saved tip and clean checkout before the callback, and checks the current record/tombstone/range/attempt, actual head and checkout again afterwards. Resume/merge use the same mutex. Cancellation releases it, including after a real reviewer starts; deletion outside the mutex invalidates the accepted result. A later refusal preserves the adapter's observed cost, destination and actual helper ids without manufacturing Codium dollars.
- **Placement follows the existing session rule.** Connect loads a fresh work instance through `InstanceStore`, makes an owned hidden `vectorscope.place` child of the requester, and registers it with `TaskPlace`. `Review.run` receives that placement parent, so its own `Session.create` inherits the task's work directory and the requester's durable home. The helper handle admits only the current run's new hidden children of that placement. Every helper is stopped/joined before the review-owned instance is disposed. A work instance already in use is refused rather than disposed out from under another caller. No change was made to review/run.ts.
- **A full branch review, with task provenance.** Execution uses the private `reviewBase` (or launch seed) as the branch base and always sets `full: true`. Its public request/result retains the original task id. Since a full review does not read incremental branch state, `since` compares the earlier saved findings through the existing working-tree comparison, using actual changed paths; it compares only the same task id. A different valid task refuses before consent or a model call. Fixing a task review through the generic parent-checkout fix action refuses; the task is changed/settled and reviewed again by id.
- **Read authority survives placement.** Before consent, the hub checks the original and task checkout under relative names and explicit absolute aliases. A relative allow is not replaced by the default ask in an unrelated absolute namespace. Newly owned placed helpers receive only extra read denies, conservatively derived from requester denies and asks, including original-root, worktree and tool-relative aliases. An unmappable absolute authority refuses rather than widening. This also blocks a restricted file that appears after preflight. No helper gets a new allow.
- **Generation stays at the contract checkpoint.** The schema target is additive. The CLI's supported return type remains its actual three targets; it does not claim a task flag it does not provide. Public SDK/client generation waits for the CW-16 final batch as the handoff requires; generated sources are never edited by hand. App target decoding remains a separate root-owned integration check.
- **Qualification.** The final focused worktree file passed all 11 actual cases: different main/task file contents through the real reviewer read tool, live requester spend/lineage, full repeat and cross-task comparison, original absolute deny/ask, a restricted late file, running/foreign tasks, cancellation before and after a real model starts, deletion during a held real model response, observed cost/destination, and alias/default-ask compatibility. Core findings passed 16 cases; schema passed 24. The final focused file passed again after canonical service composition and honest CLI return typing (25.23 seconds), and the engine typecheck passed (94.27 seconds). All models are disposable test providers; no real model is called. Full CI/native integration remains head-specific and follows the source checkpoint.


## 2026-11-12, CW-17 — owner dismissal context and durable findings status

- **Only an explicit owner action writes review context.** The owner-only dismissal route uses the existing shared owner runner and reserves its click message before asynchronous review lookup. A model's proposal still accepts only `LearnedKind`; `review` is not a learned kind. The narrow Brain writer fixes `kind: review`, user origin, repository scope and user evidence, and uses the existing validation, redaction and SQL writer. Review deduplication is isolated from other learned kinds in both directions.
- **A dismissed claim is data.** Repository memory keeps the selected claim's bounded quoted title/path and the owner's bounded reason. Reviewer bodies, suggestions and instructions are not copied. This context is not a team rule or a general suppression. The VectorScope dismissal consumer remains a separate cross-branch integration gate; this checkpoint does not activate it.
- **The original review is updated in place.** The hub finds the original completed assistant `connect` tool row, validates the same owner conversation and selected finding IDs, and changes only their dismissal status. It keeps request identity, timestamps, cost, helper lineage and verdict; dismissal never creates a new clean review. After the memory write it checks that the original stored row still exists and is unchanged before updating it. If the row disappeared, the owner receives an explicit failure explaining that any already-saved review notes remain.
- **A batch is atomic for memory.** All selected notes use the existing Brain writer inside its canonical SQL transaction. A later invalid path or refused note rolls back earlier inserts and dedup/evidence changes in that click. Invalid/foreign run IDs and guest-marked conversations write no review notes. The click's acknowledgment carries only run/finding identifiers rather than another full `connect` result, so one review remains one durable review/card.
- **Qualification is source-specific.** Core Brain tests and Core typecheck passed at the separate Core checkpoint. Actual owner-domain tests proved durable repository notes, transaction rollback, foreign/guest refusal, one original review row and reload from a fresh instance; the HTTP file proved the real SDK dismissal path and preserved route authentication/cancellation; the shared-runner tests proved concurrent-human success/failure behavior. After explicit fixture-invariant narrowing, the final context file passed all 3 cases (2.12 seconds), the actual owner-domain file passed all 3 cases (4.95 seconds), and the final Engine typecheck passed (95.43 seconds). The unchanged full HTTP file passed all 7 cases and the shared-runner file both cases at the preceding source snapshot; Brain passed all 58 cases and the Core typecheck at its separate checkpoint. All provider fixtures are disposable; no paid model was called. Full native/CI acceptance remains tied to the eventual pushed head.


## 2026-11-12, CW-18 — directory reviews for desktop workspaces

- **A directory is not a conversation.** The public request remains `{kind: user}` with no requester session. An engine-created owner-private `vectorscope.directory` anchor owns only hidden execution/consent lineage. It contains no manufactured human message and no dangling or self-parented assistant receipt. The owner starts it in the authenticated current project and captured canonical directory/filesystem identity; task-isolation targets/directories still require their launching conversation and verified task placement.
- **Private receipt storage is necessary.** The existing assistant schema requires a user-message parent, while durable background-job metadata is limited to 4 KiB and has no result field. A complete findings-rich directory result therefore cannot honestly use either as its whole receipt. The narrow engine adapter stores decoded versioned receipts in the existing Storage namespace under hashed project/canonical-directory scope. It uses private single-run writer capabilities, strict safe basenames, regular/no-follow/nonblocking bounded reads (1 MiB), serialized terminal-fenced temporary-file/atomic-rename writes because general Storage read/write do not provide those guarantees. No public schema or database migration is introduced, and client-editable Session metadata supplies no verdict authority.
- **A queued ID is real before it is returned.** Directory execution is an existing BackgroundJob with durable private anchor/directory binding. The caller awaits that actual job identity through existing public store/lease ports and its bounded receipt before releasing execution or returning queued. A priced/repository-selected model stays behind the real owner Permission ask; returning queued never answers consent. The UI uses captured directory/client scope, fresh authenticated GET and matching pending asks, and explicitly replies or cancels.
- **Privacy routing does not invent a requester.** Connect event envelopes and Permission asks use the private anchor for existing audience filtering; the public run requester stays sessionless. Server-written ask metadata includes run ID and directory-review scope. The anchor must never be opened as a conversation. Cached and stored reads both check the current private anchor and genuine matching durable job; deleted, foreign or unknown authority cannot leave cached clean evidence.
- **Reload requires matching bytes and lifecycle.** Terminal receipt bytes are sealed with SHA256 in the actual durable job's existing bounded `metadata.ended` port after the job's terminal write barrier. A schema-valid edited verdict, wrong run/anchor/project/directory, missing/unknown job, malformed or oversized file supplies no trusted result. A crash before sealing cannot yield a clean persisted result, and restart never resumes paid work. The in-memory Run stays nonterminal until its bounded terminal/failure write completes; a stop arriving after actual reviewer work entered this finishing phase honestly returns false. A result too large to retain becomes failed/incomplete with its actual trusted cost/lineage preserved; it is never truncated into clean. Only terminal receipt files beyond the latest 50 are retired; active receipts and session accounting/lineage are untouched.
- **Consent does not freeze the checkout.** The hub captures canonical path plus bigint device/inode identity once, refuses unsupported inode-zero identity, rechecks current private authority after consent and before helper prompts, retains helper read denies, and repeats denied-read preflight at the existing working/diff boundary before the reviewer collects uncommitted changes. A restricted file appearing during model consent must refuse before any provider call. The existing genuine SessionAdmission.Current policy is also wrapped with a synchronous active-cap/frozen-directory check before each provider admission and otherwise delegates unchanged to the original reviewer reservation policy; this adds no budget or accounting writes and does not solve the separately held workflow/repair authority seams. Existing settings rechecks and model identity validation remain in VectorScope's adapter.
- **Optional automatic swarm review remains unavailable.** The current v2 parallel runtime keeps its reserved allowance in private active-run state and exposes no qualified reviewer-sharing port. An independent directory allowance would not prove the original worker's cumulative cap. The optional pre-merge automation stays off and unavailable until that authority is supplied; this checkpoint adds no accounting changes or paid background warmup. Local owner-click reviews require no GitHub connection and no cloud workspace.
- **Qualification.** The batch090 source passed all 15 domain cases (14.26 seconds), all 6 actual VectorScope/SessionPrompt disposable-provider cases (18.16 seconds), and all 8 real SDK/HTTP cases (29.65 seconds). After the finishing-cancel repair and exact fixture type fixes, all 15 domain cases passed again (19.25 seconds), and the final Engine typecheck passed (119.67 seconds). The pinned-public-catalog Node build passed (5.22 seconds), then the real Node 24.19.0 bundle smoke passed (4.21 seconds), including the genuinely queued sessionless directory route, fresh GET, private consent, empty anchor messages, unchanged visible conversations and explicit cancellation/withdrawal. Independent read-only review verified all 10 current source hashes and found no further concrete blocker after its four actual findings were repaired. App types and browser-source types passed at the root checkpoint before later task-guidance edits; those newer App checks and actual browser CI remain root-owned and pending. Full CI/native-platform acceptance is head-specific and still follows this source checkpoint. The experimental flag stays off; this checkpoint does not qualify the held repair/workflow authority seams, optional swarm automation, or the still-open safe-Git callback integration.

## 2026-11-14 — v2 integration safety and visibility repairs

- **Requester reads also govern collected diffs (C1).** A hub-qualified capability keeps copied requester rules and the original/canonical source roots. VectorScope checks every changed path (including untracked names and both sides of a rename) before model consent/working, then checks the collected anchors again immediately before the review/provider boundary. This uses requester permission evaluation without the review-tool deny exemption. Later explicit read allows remain meaningful; a restricted change refuses the hand-off instead of silently widening it. Missing, expired, copied or unqualified policy capabilities refuse. Existing tool-read and listing hide rules remain in place.
- **Unsupported hosts are unavailable (F4/C2).** Connected VectorScope reviews report that their private-Git port currently requires Linux. They are no longer advertised ready on Windows/macOS. This is an honest availability limitation, not cross-platform private-Git qualification or a fallback to ordinary Git.
- **Experimental surfaces remain hidden (F8).** The connected command is hidden from default CLI help, including when the broad experimental flag is overridden off for Connect. Default workflow guidance omits connected stages; only the Connect opt-in adds the separate guide, which still describes connected workflow execution as unavailable. The public configuration source describes reserved repair mode as unavailable/reporting only; generated schemas must follow the aggregate source. The already-merged conditional connect_fix registration remains required.
- **Automatic repair stays explicitly held (F9/A13).** `ConnectPolicy.REPAIR_AVAILABLE` is false in source. Owner starts, detached notifications and actual Notify admission all enforce it. Merely adding a future `SpendGuard.tightenLease` method cannot enable repair. Callable-lease test cases actively prove quiet reporting, no provider request or lease acquisition, and unchanged owner budgets while held. Their older positive capability assertions remain as future qualification coverage, but those future branches did not run and are not qualified by this checkpoint. Ordinary report delivery and busy-human context remain covered.
- **A fix child retains requester read questions (C10).** Only connect_fix inherits session read asks as additional restrictions. Absolute source rules also keep frozen relative/canonical aliases in the isolated checkout, using the actual parent worktree. Its own effective read denies and the requester's explicit denies remain hard ceilings; ordinary specialist delegation is unchanged. No session allow grants are copied into the hidden child.
- **Attached review cancellation uses the review endpoint (C9).** An attached CLI cancels tracked review IDs through session-scoped connect.cancel when leaving after timeout, disconnect or failure; stop-all also waits for this cancellation before exiting. Task/shell IDs never go to the review route. Failures are reported as unconfirmed rather than claimed stopped. Stopping the main turn alone retains its existing helper behavior.
- **Patch review before write consent (C7).** The terminal's connected write approval shows the actual multiline patch using its existing diff body. Terminal controls are sanitized line by line while preserving patch line boundaries. The App dock renders the complete patch as keyboard-focusable, scrollable text, never HTML; the explicit once-only decision is unchanged. Production baseline run 43623921815/job 132535820126 succeeded on exact source 2f88021845bd5fc74ea001d96729b4fcfb2524fa before this JSX/CSS change, with nine navigation measurements (three scenarios repeated three times), artifact 14125262951 and no providers. The same candidate after-check and actual browser regression remain separate required receipts.
- **Windows checkpoint evidence.** Core policy: 43 passed/768 assertions. Full hub: 42/325, including the actual outside-instance source alias. Qualified policy service: 3/15. Adapter: 25 passed/2 Linux-only model-selection skips/308 assertions; its actual Linux success and provider secret-negative branches remain not run locally. Workflow tool: 12/129 and two existing snapshots. Fix-child policy: 9/29, including absolute-source aliases. After-run: 22/112. CLI help flag target: 1/6. Attached cancellation helper: 1/7; Ctrl+C file: 13/63. Terminal patch presentation: 4/9. App consent helper: 5/19. The registry Actions file passed 10/63 with unavailable versus synthetic-ready Computer cases, explicit Connect opt-in and the original private-context/model fences. Engine types passed after the C1/C10 alias and registry follow-ups. App types (7.39 s), e2e source types (1.24 s), TUI types (4.44 s) and final Core types (6.35 s) also passed on the C7 source checkpoint. The actual browser assertion, candidate after-benchmark and aggregate CI checks remain separate and are not claimed passed here. No paid model, hosted fallback or live deployment was used for these checks.
- **Directory fixture repair, not a product fallback.** CI run 43621724139/job 132528677038 refused the task fixture with "No VectorScope model is set" because it configured only the project default. The synthetic fixture now chooses the disposable owner-global agent.vectorscope.model, restores/invalidate it on scope exit, and explicitly exports Config.node. Its original Linux provider, lineage, spend, read-denial and cancellation assertions are retained; the Linux rerun is required.
- **Remaining Tier B boundaries.** Named/default branch resolution still requires safe support in the qualified private Git view; resolving it through unrestricted ordinary Git is not an acceptable substitute. Directory request reservations intentionally consume unknown outcomes: existing tests forbid replacement after process exit or a failed O_EXCL job binding. Blind key release or deletion would violate that contract. Durable orphan reconciliation and retention need verified prepared-anchor/failed-tombstone evidence; a weak-map-only cleanup would not solve them. Neither is claimed fixed by this checkpoint. Default activation remains off.
