## 2026-11-15 — CW-20 close-out: ReviewGit collector path pinned, ConnectGit narrowed (CN-02)

Source: `connect-finish` at `0a06668b` (CN-01 on `0ee486e3`). This package changes `packages/engine/src/connect/git.ts`, `packages/engine/test/connect/git.test.ts`, `packages/engine/test/connect/vectorscope.test.ts` and three lane documents. It changes no route, ledger, schema, generated file, flag, workflow or public page. VectorScope's `review/**` is not edited, and no export is added there.

- **What changed.**
  - *The collector path is pinned first.* Two new Linux cases in vectorscope.test.ts, one loop with two rows, run the existing fake `Review.run` success path over an edit to `src/probe.ts`, which `src/call.ts` calls, and setup's edit to `src/list.ts`:
    - "a connect review's collector reads are answered by VectorScope's ReviewGit view, never by connect's own Git view" reviews the two edits uncommitted;
    - "a connect branch review's collector reads are answered by VectorScope's ReviewGit view, never by connect's own Git view" commits them on a `feature` branch and reviews it against the setup commit, so the collector's branch reads run too: in `cli/cmd/review.ts`, the base's `commitOf`, `localStatePath`, `ReviewSource.planRange` and `subjects`. `otherAuthors` runs only with `--checks`, and `ReviewSource.prDiff` only against a stored earlier review, so neither runs in this case; both are still pinned only by the refusal table in git.test.ts and the existing branch success cases.
    - When check 2 resolves the review model, `Git.Service` is connect's view. Each case spies every member of that view. The members are listed by name in a `{ [K in keyof Git.Interface]: K }` map, so a member added to `Git.Interface` fails typecheck until it is spied too. The spies pass through, and a scope finalizer restores them. `views.length === 1` is a tripwire against wrapping the shared layer Git by mistake.
    - Inside the fake review it records three things: `ReviewGit.Current` is in place, `Git.Service` there is connect's view, and the two are different objects. It also records how many `run` calls connect's view had answered by then.

Before the review they are check 1's `changes()` again, and for a branch, `stateFile()`'s `rev-parse --absolute-git-dir`. After the review they are `changes()` once more. `changes()` is the top level, the quiet verify of `HEAD`, (for a branch) the quiet verify of the base and `merge-base`, the worktree and `--cached` name-only diffs, both untracked listings, (for a branch) the base-to-head name-only diff, and the batch read. ## 2026-11-15 — C3a: named refs and origin/HEAD inside ReadOnlyGit (CN-03)

Source: `connect-finish` at `5c81f9f1`. This package changes `packages/core/src/read-only-git.ts`, `packages/core/test/read-only-git.test.ts`, this log and connected-workspace-handoff.md. It changes no engine file, route, ledger, schema, generated file, flag, workflow or public page. VectorScope's `review/**` is not edited, and no export is added. The package was handed back uncommitted, for the lead's commit. This entry describes the code after review round one (below).

- **What changed.**
  - *Shared validator.* `referenceName()` (`read-only-git.ts:746`) holds `head()`'s check-ref-format conditions, byte for byte. `head()` calls it with the same "invalid branch reference" message. Nothing else in `head()` changed: its loose and packed lookups, its messages and its use of `read()` are as before.
  - *`{ kind: "reference", name }`* (the `Read` union at :1297, the switch case at :1833). It answers like `rev-parse --verify --quiet`:
    1. *Name admission.* The name must be a string that is well-formed UTF-16. It must not be `@`, must not start with `-` or `main-worktree/`, must not be 40 hex digits in either case (Git's `get_oid_hex` reads uppercase too, and rev-parse takes 40 hex as an object id before any ref) and must not be HEAD-shaped (`/^[A-Z_]*HEAD$/`: HEAD, FETCH_HEAD, ORIG_HEAD, MERGE_HEAD and the like). A `refs/` name is admitted only under `refs/heads/`, `refs/remotes/` or `refs/tags/`. Then `referenceName()` must pass. Otherwise: "the reference name is unsupported".
    2. *Git's places.* The name is looked up in all six places rev-parse uses, in Git's order: the name itself, `refs/<n>`, `refs/tags/<n>`, `refs/heads/<n>`, `refs/remotes/<n>` and `refs/remotes/<n>/HEAD`. Only a match under `refs/heads/`, `refs/remotes/` or `refs/tags/` in the first five places, found in the common directory or in packed-refs, may answer. A match anywhere else refuses with "the reference name resolves outside branches and tags". That covers a file of that name in the Git directory, which rev-parse's first rule may read (`config`, a pseudo-ref, a hand-written file), `refs/<n>` outside those namespaces (`stash`, `notes/commits`) and a bare remote name meaning `<remote>/HEAD` (`origin`). Two answering matches refuse with "the reference name is ambiguous", where rev-parse would warn and take the first.
    3. *Loose names, before packed-refs, as Git reads them.* Each place is opened through the pinned common handle by `looseEntry()` (:785). Every component is opened with O_NOFOLLOW, and a value is bounded to 4 KiB.
       - ENOENT and a namespace directory count as missing, and packed-refs is then consulted, as Git does (a tag `release/1.0` beside a branch `release`).
       - ENOTDIR below a regular file also counts as missing, but packed-refs is not consulted for that place. Git's `lstat` fails there with ENOTDIR, and Git's files backend falls back to packed-refs only on ENOENT. So a stale packed `refs/heads/release/2.0` below the loose branch `release` reads as missing here, as it does in Git.
       - A linked leaf refuses with "linked references are unsupported". ENOTDIR below a linked directory refuses with "linked reference directories are unsupported" (`regularAncestor()`, :808). A FIFO or other non-regular entry refuses.
       - In a linked worktree, each place is also opened through the worktree's own Git directory, and any hit there refuses with "the reference name resolves outside branches and tags". Git reads only per-worktree refs (`refs/bisect/*`, `refs/worktree/*`, `refs/rewritten/*`) and pseudo-refs there, and none of those may answer. Git never reads a branch or tag there, so a branch-shaped file such as `<worktree git dir>/refs/heads/ghost` refuses too. Before review round one it answered, and it overrode a packed `refs/heads/ghost`.
    4. *packed-refs* is read once, bounded to 4 MiB, through the same pinned handle, and validated whole by `packedRefs()` (:831) before any lookup. It refuses with "packed-refs is malformed" unless the bytes are valid UTF-8, the file is empty or ends with a newline, a `# pack-refs with: ` header appears only as the first line, every other line is a `<40-hex> <name>` record whose name has no space or control character or a `^<40-hex>` line right after a record, and, under the `sorted` trait, the records are in byte order. Git binary-searches a file with that trait, so it could miss an out-of-order record that a line-by-line scan finds. A file this rejects poisons every read that consults it instead of hiding a name. A CRLF record, which Git reads as a name ending in a carriage return, refuses rather than reading as missing. Then each place needs at most one record (`packedReference()`, :855), or it refuses with "the packed reference has no unique commit".
    5. *Values.* A loose value must be plain lowercase 40-hex, or it refuses with "symbolic or non-SHA1 reference is unsupported". An empty file refuses as "invalid metadata line".
    6. *The snapshot answers.* The single match is checked with `cat-file -t <oid>` in the private Git directory. A missing object refuses with "the reference's object is not in the private snapshot; fetch or pass a commit". A commit answers. A tree or blob refuses with "the reference does not name a commit". A tag object without a packed peel refuses with "an annotated tag resolves only through its peeled packed line"; CN-04 replaces that refusal.
    7. *Packed peels.* A `^<sha>` line is accepted only after a `refs/tags/` record whose own object is a tag in the snapshot; otherwise "a peeled packed line follows only an annotated tag". `cat-file tag <oid>` must then begin `object <peel>\ntype commit\n`, or the read refuses with "the packed reference's peeled commit does not match its tag". The peeled commit must itself be a commit in the snapshot. A malformed peel line refuses with "packed-refs is malformed".
    8. *Answers.* `{ code: 0, bytes: "<40-hex>\n" }` for a match. `{ code: 1, bytes: "" }` only when none of Git's six places holds the name, as point 3 reads them, and the name cannot be an object id. When no ref matches, Git's `get_oid_1` goes on to `get_describe_name` and `get_short_oid`. So a name with no ref that is hex-shaped (4 to 39 hex digits, either case) or describe-shaped (ending in a lowercase `-g` and then 4 to 40 hex digits of either case) refuses with "the name may be an object id; fetch or pass a commit". Refs still answer first, as in Git: a branch `cafe` or `v1-gcafe` answers. `x-Gcafe` and 3-hex `abc` give code 1, because Git reads neither as an object id.
  - *`{ kind: "default-branch" }`* (:1300, switch :1924). It reads `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` through `looseEntry()` and answers like `symbolic-ref --quiet`.
    - A missing file, or one below a regular file, gives code 1, unless packed-refs holds a `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` record. Git never packs the symbolic one, so a packed record refuses. packed-refs is validated as in `reference`.
    - The file must be exactly `ref: refs/remotes/origin/<name>`, and the full name must pass `referenceName()` and must not be `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD`. Anything else refuses with "origin/HEAD does not name an origin branch", or "invalid metadata line" for an empty, CRLF or multi-line file. Links refuse as in `reference`.
    - The answer is `{ code: 0, bytes: "refs/remotes/origin/<name>\n" }`. The target's existence is not checked; CN-05 resolves it through `reference`.
  - Nothing calls either read yet. ConnectGit's `Query` is unchanged, so connect cannot reach them, and connect stays behind `VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL_CONNECT`, which is off.
- **Resolution stays inside the private view.** This is the boundary the 2026-11-14 entry's "Remaining Tier B boundaries" requires.
  - No ref name, ref file content, config or environment value reaches a subprocess. Git receives only a regex-checked 40-hex object id, in `cat-file -t` and `cat-file tag`, run in the private Git directory with the private config and environment (`args` and `makeCommands`).
  - Ref files are read only through the pinned common handle (and a linked worktree's pinned Git handle, where every hit refuses). No symlink is followed at any component, and a link refuses rather than hiding a name. Nothing reads the user's config or environment.
  - Every answer is a commit object that is present in the private snapshot. `fsck --strict --full` checked every object in that snapshot at open. fsck proves connectivity only from the snapshot's roots (HEAD, the index and shallow), so a named commit outside HEAD's history is known to exist and be well-formed, not to have its whole history. A later read that needs a missing parent fails at that read. For an annotated tag, both the tag object and its packed peel are checked against the snapshot.
  - Every failure throws `Unavailable` through `observe`, which leaves the view open. The tests check `isOpen()`, and a successful read after the refusals.
- **What it closes.** Part 1 of C3: review connect:correctness-security #3, v2-complete-plan §4.1 C3, and the first sentence of the 2026-11-14 "Remaining Tier B boundaries". The plan row's "resolve the named ref to a SHA before `ConnectGit.use`" is superseded, because that entry requires resolution inside the qualified view. Following CN-01's practice, the plan itself is not edited. C3 stays open until CN-05 wires these reads into connect.
- **Departures from the brief.**
  1. *Result shape.* Both reads return `{ code, bytes }`, not `{ ref, name }`. `Repository.read` has one result type, and VectorScope's `review/git.ts:126,162` and `review/memory-outcome.ts:188-190` (do-not-touch) pass the whole `Read` union to it. default-branch's bytes are the full ref; `name` is that ref without the fixed `refs/remotes/origin/` prefix.
  2. *Not found is code 1, not a refusal.* The brief is silent here. This matches `rev-parse --verify --quiet` and `symbolic-ref --quiet`, and lets CN-05 try its next candidate without poisoning ConnectGit. Code 1 promises two things: no ref of that name exists in any place Git would read it from, and the name cannot be an object id. It comes only for a name that is neither hex-shaped nor describe-shaped and that none of Git's six places holds (40 hex digits never reach a lookup), so a next candidate never replaces something Git would have found.
  3. *Loose refs go through `looseEntry()`, not `read()`.* `read()` refuses a directory and lets ENOTDIR through as an error, which would refuse an ordinary layout, a branch `release` beside tags `release/x`.
  4. *Git's six places, not the brief's three.* The brief's fixed order (heads, remotes, tags) is replaced by all six rev-parse places in Git's order. Because a second answering match refuses, the order changes no answer. The extra places refuse where a branch would otherwise answer although rev-parse takes `refs/stash`, a file in the Git directory or `origin/HEAD`. `heads/x`, `tags/x` and `remotes/x` answer through `refs/<n>`, as Git resolves them, and refuse as ambiguous when, say, both `refs/heads/x` and `refs/heads/heads/x` exist.
  5. *Only origin's HEAD.* Another primary remote would mean reading config.
  6. *The packed peel is checked against its tag object,* not only trusted; see the critique below.
  7. *packed-refs is validated whole,* not only the lines a lookup touches (review round one, finding 8, extended).
- **Plan critique.** Seven of the eight points were taken as written, and the DWIM point was taken in another form.
  - *ENOTDIR below a linked directory (major).* Taken. `regularAncestor()` treats the name as missing only when the first component that is not a directory is a regular file; anything else refuses. The plan's nested-symlink row now refuses. New: a detached view with `refs/heads` symlinked. Its open succeeds without reading `refs/heads`, and the branch, `refs/heads/<branch>` and even `missing` refuse. Also new: `refs/remotes/origin` symlinked for default-branch. Mutation M5.
  - *A packed peel after any ref (major).* Taken and extended. A `^` line is accepted only after a `refs/tags/` record whose object is a tag in the snapshot, and the tag object's own `object`/`type commit` header must equal the peel. New rows: a peel after a packed branch, a peel after a commit under `refs/tags/`, a packed tag whose object is missing from the snapshot, and a peel that disagrees with its tag. Mutations M7 and M8.
  - *DWIM forms (major).* Taken in another form. The critique proposed refusing every unqualified name that starts with `heads/`, `tags/` or `remotes/`, plus pseudo-ref shapes. This package instead looks the name up in every place rev-parse does, and refuses when any place outside branches and tags holds it or two places do. That covers the critique's cases: `refs/heads/x` beside `refs/heads/heads/x` refuses as ambiguous, HEAD-shaped names refuse before any read, and any file of that name in the Git directory refuses. It also covers `refs/stash`, `refs/notes/*`, a bare `<remote>` meaning `<remote>/HEAD`, every file in a linked worktree's own Git directory and `main-worktree/`. A lone `heads/x` still answers, as Git answers it. New case: "a name rev-parse would also find outside branches and tags refuses rather than resolve differently". Mutations M6, M9 and M10.
  - *Counter reset (minor).* Taken. The filter and fsmonitor are configured after the pre-open setup and the counter is cleared before open (`guarded`). Each fixture Git step during a view first asserts zero calls, then clears its own (`liveGit`).
  - *Well-formed names (minor).* Taken. A non-string or lone-surrogate name refuses as unsupported.
  - *An explicit ELOOP reason (minor).* Taken: "linked references are unsupported", asserted exactly for a linked branch and a linked origin/HEAD. Mutation M3.
  - *Mutation restore (minor).* Taken. Each mutation restored a backup copy and checked its sha256; no `git checkout` or `git restore` touched the worktree file.
  - *Engine typecheck (minor).* Taken. `bun typecheck` passed in packages/engine as well as packages/core.
- **Review round one.** All eight findings were taken; none is disputed. They are numbered here in the order the review listed them. Each code finding was first reproduced with Git 2.43.0 in a scratch repository under `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/exp`.
  - *Finding 1 (blocking): a linked worktree's own Git directory answered branch-shaped names.* Fixed as proposed: any hit through that handle refuses (point 3). Reproduced: with `<worktree git dir>/refs/heads/ghost` holding HEAD's commit, `git -C linked rev-parse --verify --quiet ghost` exits 1. New DWIM rows: `ghost`, and `ghost-packed` with a packed record and a stray file, refuse in the worktree view. The primary view gives `ghost` code 1 and `ghost-packed` its packed commit, as Git does from the linked worktree. Mutation M10.
  - *Findings 2 (non-blocking) and 5 (blocking): uppercase 40 hex was admitted.* Fixed: the admission is case-insensitive. Reproduced: `rev-parse --verify --quiet <UPPER40>` prints the lowercase object id. New row: `head.toUpperCase()` refuses as unsupported. Mutation M11.
  - *Findings 3 (non-blocking) and 6 (blocking): hex-shaped and describe-shaped names with no ref gave code 1.* Fixed with finding 6's refusal after the ref lookup (point 8). Finding 3's other option, refusing every 4-to-39-hex name at admission, was not taken, because it would also refuse a branch really named `cafe`, which Git answers. Reproduced: `<7-hex>^{commit}`, `x-g<7-hex>^{commit}`, `refs/heads/x-g<7-hex>` and `<7-HEX>` all answer the commit; `x-G<7-hex>`, `abc` and `x-gabc` exit 1. New rows: `cafe` and `v1-gcafe` answer from refs. 7-hex, uppercase 7-hex, 39-hex, `v1.0-3-g<7-hex>` and `refs/heads/x-g<7-HEX>` refuse, and each is also checked to resolve in Git. `deadbeef` refuses as well. `abc`, `x-gabc` and `x-Gcafe` give code 1, as in Git. Mutation M12.
  - *Findings 4 and 7 (non-blocking): the entry's wording.* Points 3 and 8, Departure 2, Known strictness and the hand-off notes are rewritten to the fixed behaviour, and the receipts list the new rows and mutations.
  - *Finding 8 (non-blocking): a CRLF packed line read as missing.* Taken, and extended into whole-file validation (point 4, Departure 7). Git itself reads a CRLF record's name as ending in a carriage return, so it reports the plain name missing. Reproduced: `rev-parse --verify --quiet crlf` exits 1. The refusal is therefore stricter than Git, and it keeps a malformed file from passing as "missing" for CN-05's next candidate. New rows: CRLF, an unsorted file under the `sorted` trait, a missing final newline, a peel after a peel, and a record whose name holds the byte 0xff. Decoded, that name matched `packed-branch\ufffd`, although Git compares other bytes. The malformed-peel row now refuses as "packed-refs is malformed". Mutations M13 and M15 to M17.
  - *Found while fixing, beyond the findings.* Two more cases where Git answered differently. Each is fixed and pinned:
    - ENOTDIR fell back to packed-refs. Reproduced: with a loose `refs/heads/release` and a packed `refs/heads/release/1.0`, `rev-parse --verify --quiet refs/heads/release/1.0` exits 1. Fixed in point 3. New rows `release/2.0` and `refs/heads/release/2.0` give code 1, and Git rejects both in the same fixture. Mutation M14.
    - The `sorted` trait over unsorted records. Reproduced: under that trait, with records `zz`, `aa`, `mm`, Git misses `zz` and finds the other two. Fixed in point 4. Mutation M15.
- **Known strictness.** These refuse although Git answers them, or answers that the name is missing. Each refusal tells the caller to fetch or pass a commit. Review round one found three cases that answered differently from Git: the worktree Git directory, uppercase 40 hex, and hex- or describe-shaped names. Two more were found while fixing: the ENOTDIR fallback and the sorted trait. All five are fixed. No case tested here now answers differently from Git 2.43.0, and code 1 is returned only as Departure 2 states.
  - A name Git resolves with an ambiguity warning.
  - A branch whose name equals a file in the Git directory (`config`, `index`, `description`, `packed-refs`), or, in a linked worktree, any file at one of the six places in that worktree's own Git directory (`commondir`, `gitdir`, a stray `refs/heads/<n>`).
  - A HEAD-shaped branch name.
  - 40 hex digits in either case, which Git reads as an object id.
  - A name with no ref that is hex-shaped or describe-shaped, whether or not Git finds an object for it: short SHAs, `git describe` output and, say, `deadbeef` naming nothing.
  - A loose ref that Git's lenient parser accepts: a SHA followed by whitespace and text, a SHA and a blank line, or `ref:` without its space.
  - Any packed-refs file `packedRefs()` rejects, for every name that consults it, even where Git's single lookup would succeed or report the name missing: a CRLF record, a record name with a space or control character, a stray line, a missing final newline, bytes that are not UTF-8, or records out of order under the `sorted` trait.
  - Loose annotated tags are CN-04's, and so is resolving short SHAs in the snapshot.
- **Held.** ConnectGit keeps `defaultBranch: fail` (`connect/git.ts:240`), and `Query` is unchanged. No flag changes. Q6 is open. `review/**` is not edited.
- **Hand-off notes.**
  - CN-04 replaces the "annotated tag resolves only through its peeled packed line" refusal with a peel through the private object store. The one-level `cat-file tag` header check in the peel branch is a starting point. CN-04 also adds short SHAs at `revision()` (:1633). It may then replace the "the name may be an object id" refusal with resolution in the snapshot, in Git's order: refs first, then describe output, then a unique short SHA. An ambiguous prefix must still refuse.
  - CN-05 maps a quiet verify of a revision that is neither HEAD nor 40-hex to `reference`, and builds `Git.Base` `{ name, ref: "origin/<name>" }` from default-branch's bytes. Only code 1 lets the next candidate (for example `origin/<name>`) be tried. Every thrown refusal, "the name may be an object id" included, poisons the review, which fails closed. Until CN-04 resolves short SHAs, a hex-shaped or describe-shaped base with no ref of that name fails closed, and CN-05 must not route it to a next candidate.
- **Receipts.** All runs were local, one file at a time, through `/home/user/.tools/vector-serial`, with `--timeout 30000`.
  - *How the Linux cases ran.* This container runs as root, and ReadOnlyGit refuses root ("this host has no supported unprivileged Git verifier", `read-only-git.ts:505-508`). As in CN-02, the runs went through `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/as-ci.sh`, which runs `unshare -m` with `/usr/bin/python3` pointed at 3.12, then `setpriv` to uid 1000, as CI runs unprivileged. Git here is 2.43.0.

Seven are in this package's code, and each repeats a pattern the file already has: a helper that returns nothing on one path, like `read()` (`looseEntry` and its catch callback, `packedReference`), `await expect(...).rejects`, and `as unknown as ReadOnlyGit.Read`. The other 48 are in lines this package did not write, including the validator's regex, moved unchanged from `head()`. One warning that review round one introduced, a regex where `startsWith` would do, was fixed.

## 2026-11-15 — C3b: short commit ids and loose annotated tags through the private object store (CN-04)

Source: `connect-finish` at `c6875c40`. This package changes `packages/core/src/read-only-git.ts`, `packages/core/test/read-only-git.test.ts`, `packages/engine/test/connect/git.test.ts`, this log and connected-workspace-handoff.md. It changes no engine source, route, ledger, schema, generated file, flag, workflow or public page. VectorScope's `review/**` is not edited. No export, `Read` variant or `INTEGRITY` value is added or changed, so nothing is regenerated. As the orchestrator instructed for this package, it was handed back uncommitted for the lead's commit, as CN-03 was. This entry describes the tree after review round one; the round-one changes are listed under "Review round one" below.

- **What changed.**
  - *Shared closures in `perform`* (`read-only-git.ts:1656-1697`).
    - `peel(oid)` is the fixed `rev-parse --verify --quiet --end-of-options <oid>^{commit}` invocation that `resolve` already used.
    - `named(name)` (:1660) is CN-03's lookup in Git's six places, moved verbatim out of `reference`. Every CN-03 refusal inside it is unchanged: links, matches outside branches and tags, any hit in a linked worktree's own Git directory, packed-refs validation, and a place without a unique packed commit. The one addition is a return type, so that a packed record carries its peel.
  - *`resolve`* (:1711). It admits four shapes:
    1. HEAD, and 40 hex digits of either case, lowercased, take the old path, `peel(revision(...))`. For HEAD and lowercase 40-hex the argv is byte for byte the old one, and a missing full id still gives code 1 and no bytes. Uppercase 40-hex now answers instead of refusing, because Git's `get_oid_hex` reads either case.
    2. For 7 to 39 hex digits of either case, `named()` runs first, as a guard only. It looks the spelling up as given, because Git compares ref names case-sensitively, and only through the pinned handles. Any ref of that spelling refuses with "the short object id is also a reference name; pass the full commit", and so does any refusal inside `named()`. A short id never answers through a ref.
    3. With no ref of that spelling, `rev-parse --disambiguate=<lowercase id>` runs in the private Git directory. It lists every loose and packed object with that prefix and never reads refs. The listing must be exit 0 and whole lines of lowercase 40-hex that each start with the prefix, or the read refuses with "the private object listing is malformed". No candidate refuses with "no object in the private snapshot has that short id; fetch or pass a full commit". More than one refuses with "the short object id is ambiguous; pass more digits". The one candidate is peeled with `peel()`. A tree, a blob or a tag of a tree refuses with "the short object id does not name a commit".
    4. Everything else falls to `revision()` (:1637), unchanged, and keeps "only a captured HEAD or exact SHA1 revision is supported". That covers fewer than 7 digits, 41 or more (64 included), names, revision syntax, a leading dash or space, the empty string and a non-string.
  - *`reference`* (:1904). The admission is unchanged. `named()` follows, then CN-03's no-ref answer (the object-id refusal or code 1), unchanged, then CN-03's tail with a new snapshot step. The tail stays inline, because only `reference` uses it; its `cat-file -t` check runs once and is inlined too.
  - *`reference`'s snapshot step.* This is brief item (3), unified for loose and packed refs as the critique asked:
    - A commit with no packed peel line answers, as before. A tree or blob refuses with "the reference does not name a commit", as before.
    - A packed peel line after an object that is not a tag refuses with "a peeled packed line follows only an annotated tag", as before.
    - A tag object is peeled only under `refs/tags/`. A branch or remote-tracking ref whose value is a tag object refuses with "only a tag reference may name an annotated tag" (:1946), loose or packed. CN-03 refused such a ref as well, with its two older messages.
    - A tag object under `refs/tags/`, loose or packed, with or without a peel line, is peeled with `peel()` in the private store. Nested tags peel to their commit. A tag of a tree, nested or not, refuses with "the reference does not name a commit". A packed peel line must equal that commit, or the read refuses with "the packed reference's peeled commit does not match its tag".
    - This replaces CN-03's "an annotated tag resolves only through its peeled packed line" refusal and its one-level `cat-file tag` header check. That check refused a packed nested tag, whose header names `type tag`.
  - *The `Read` union* (:1263-1266) gains a doc comment on `resolve`. The type itself is unchanged.
- **Resolution stays inside the private view.** CN-04 keeps the boundary that the 2026-11-14 entry's "Remaining Tier B boundaries" sets for named and default-branch resolution, and applies it to short ids.
  - Git receives only regex-checked digits: the lowercase 7-to-39-hex prefix in `--disambiguate=`, and a 40-hex object id followed by `^{commit}` or after `cat-file -t`. Every command runs in the private Git directory, with the private config and environment (`args`, `makeCommands`). No ref name, user config or environment value reaches a subprocess, and a ref file's content reaches one only as a regex-checked 40-hex object id.
  - Ref files are read only through the pinned handles, by `named()`, and for a short id only to refuse it. Refs are live, not snapshotted, so a ref made after open can turn a short id's answer into a refusal, never into another commit. The `late` row in the short-id case proves this.
  - Every answer is a commit in the private snapshot. Ambiguity is counted over the snapshot's objects only. The `later` rows prove that a commit made in the live repository after open is invisible to a short id, and that its full id still gives code 1.
  - Every failure throws `Unavailable` through `observe`, which leaves the view open. Each new case checks `isOpen()`, a successful read after its refusals, and zero calls of the repository-configured filter and fsmonitor programs.
- **What it closes.**
  - The abbreviated or uppercase `baseCommit` in review connect:correctness-security #3's failing scenario, in core. The engine row pins the read that connect's `mergeBase()` emits through `commitOf` (`adapters/vectorscope.ts:1149-1155`, called at :1161): `rev-parse --verify --quiet --end-of-options <UPPER>^{commit}` answers the full commit through ConnectGit's translator (`connect/git.ts:78`). The row's prefix is the shortest one of at least seven digits that holds a letter, so its uppercase spelling always differs from Git's. The rest of the app path is inferred from the code, not tested here. The app's `baseCommit` is admitted at `app/src/features/agents/agent-dashboard-model.ts:63-64`. In the engine it becomes `target.base`, then `mergeBase()` at `adapters/vectorscope.ts:972`, `scope.base` at :996 and the ReviewService target at :574.
  - C3 part 2 in v2-complete-plan §4.1. C3 stays open until CN-05 wires named and default bases. Following CN-01's practice, the plan is not edited.
  - The 2026-11-14 "Remaining Tier B boundaries" sentence is kept, not closed. It belongs to CN-03 and CN-05.
- **Departures from the brief and the plan.**
  1. *A short id that a ref spells refuses* (review round one, blocking). Round one followed CN-03's hand-off ("refs first, then describe output, then a unique short SHA") and critique point 4, and answered through the ref, as Git does. Review round one found that this contradicts brief item (1), which resolves a short id only against the private object store. A fetched tag or a local branch spelled like the id would then move connect's `mergeBase()` base (`adapters/vectorscope.ts:1161`) to another commit and change the reviewed range, and because refs are live, the answer could change between two reads of one review. So `named()` is now only a guard: any ref of the spelling refuses, as the plan's original "refuse a short id that is also a ref name" said. This is a departure from CN-03's hand-off, not from the brief or the plan. Answering through the ref, in Git's order, would be the lead's decision against the brief.
  2. *A packed tag record without a peel line answers* (critique points 2 and 3). CN-03 refused it. Git's rev-parse never reads packed peel lines: `peel_onion` reads the tag object for loose and packed refs alike. So the refusal was a lane restriction, not Git's behaviour. CN-03's refusal row is replaced by an answer, which live Git checks first.
  3. *Ambiguity counts objects of every type.* Git's `^{commit}` lookup passes a committish hint, so Git would pick the one commit or tag among blobs and trees that share a prefix. This read refuses instead.
  4. *A missing short id refuses,* as the brief asks. A missing full id keeps code 1.
  5. *The minimum is 7 digits,* as the brief sets; Git accepts 4. 4 to 6 digits sent to `resolve` refuse with `revision()`'s message.
  6. *41 to 64 hex digits refuse,* although the app admits up to 64. A SHA-256 store already refuses at open.
  7. *`revision()` is not widened* (CN-03's hand-off asked for short SHAs there). Merge-base, diff, log, tree and object reads still take only HEAD or lowercase 40-hex. Brief item (1) scopes short ids to `resolve`, and connect passes those reads only the 40-hex ids that `resolve` and merge-base return (`commitOf`, `mergeBase` and `from` at `adapters/vectorscope.ts:970-972`).
  8. *`reference` keeps CN-03's "the name may be an object id" refusal* (CN-03's hand-off allowed replacing it). `reference` is for names, CN-05 will send hex to `resolve`, and 4-to-6-hex and describe-shaped names still need the refusal so that code 1 never hides an object Git would find.
  9. *One engine test row,* beyond the brief's test list, in `test/connect/git.test.ts` (:396-407).
  10. *The `Read` doc comment* moves later lines down by four. The handoff's citations are updated "since CN-04".
- **Plan critique.** All nine points were taken in round one. Review round one reverses the outcome of point 4 (Departure 1).
  - *`update-ref` refuses a tag object under `refs/heads/` (major).* Taken. Reproduced with Git 2.43.0 under `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn04/exp`: `cannot update ref 'refs/heads/at-tag': trying to write non-commit object`. The branch file is written directly, and live Git resolves it to the commit first. `refs/remotes/origin/at-tag` is added with `update-ref`, which Git allows. Both now refuse in the view (Known strictness).
  - *Loose and packed refs disagreed (major).* Taken in the preferred form: one peel through the private store (above). Reproduced: after `pack-refs --all`, the nested tag and both tag-valued refs carry `^<commit>` lines, and live Git resolves all three. The tag case therefore checks every row twice, once with loose refs and once after `pack-refs --all`, with live Git first both times. It also asserts the packed peel lines, including `^<tree>` after the nested tag of a tree. The tag-valued branch and remote-tracking ref refuse in both forms, so loose and packed still agree.
  - *The comment and departure about packed peels described Git wrongly (minor).* Taken, and folded into the unified peel (Departure 2). The comment at :1947-1948 now says that Git reads tag objects and never a packed peel line. Known strictness lists where this read is still stricter than Git.
  - *The ref-name guard refused where Git answers (minor).* Taken in round one, first option, and reversed in review round one, because brief item (1) resolves a short id only in the private object store (Departure 1).
  - *Two CN-03 hand-off items were dropped without a record (minor).* Recorded as Departures 7 and 8, with reasons. CN-03's entry is cited here, not edited.
  - *Two claims went too far (minor).* Taken. This entry says CN-04 keeps the 2026-11-14 boundary, not that it closes it. It also says the engine row pins only the `commitOf` read, and that the app path is inferred from the code.
  - *Fixture assumptions were implicit (minor).* Taken. After `repack -a -d`, the test asserts that the tagged colliding blob has no loose object file and the unreachable one has. It also asserts that the colliding pair shares seven digits and differs at the eighth, without hard-coded hex. `later` is made with `commit-tree` through `liveGit` and no ref, so neither HEAD nor any ref changes. In the tag case, the tag objects are asserted packed and the refs loose before `pack-refs`, and the `fresh-object` tag object is asserted loose. The hex-named tag `abcdef0123` is asserted to match no object.
  - *Bun commands outside the lock (minor).* Taken. Every `bun` command below ran through `/home/user/.tools/vector-serial`.
  - *Commit or hand back (minor).* Handed back uncommitted, as the orchestrator instructed.
- **Review round one.** Six findings: one blocking, five not.
  1. *Blocking: `resolve` answered a short id through a live ref* (`read-only-git.ts`, then :1744). Fixed as suggested, first option: `named()` is a guard that refuses on any match, and `--disambiguate` always follows (Departure 1). The short-id case's `[sideBranch, head]` row became a refusal, after a live-Git check that Git takes the branch. A tag `abcdef0123` that matches no object refuses the same way, and a branch made after open with an 8-digit spelling of HEAD (`late`) turns that short id's answer into a refusal. The `Read` doc comment, the comment in `resolve` and the CN-05 hand-off note are rewritten to match.
  2. *Engine row could send an all-digit prefix* (`test/connect/git.test.ts`, two findings). Fixed as suggested: the prefix is `f.head.slice(0, Math.max(7, f.head.search(/[a-f]/) + 1))`, uppercased, the same rule as the core test's `lettered()`.
  3. *`reference` peeled a tag object under a branch or remote-tracking ref.* Taken, optional form: the `refs/tags/` condition is back, for loose and packed refs alike, and Known strictness lists it. With finding 1 fixed, this read is reached only through `reference`.
  4. *Mutation receipts ran against a source before the doc comment.* Fixed: every mutation was re-run against the final source, whose hash is recorded below.
  5. *A spelling that is both a ref and an object prefix answered quietly, where Git warns.* Closed by finding 1's fix, which refuses any ref spelling whether or not an object has the prefix too. No separate `--disambiguate` check is needed. The `sideBranch` row is that case: a 10-digit prefix of `side` that is also a branch.
- **Known strictness.** These refuse where Git 2.43.0 answers. Each one tells the caller to pass more digits, fetch, or pass a full commit, and ConnectGit turns it into a poisoned review that fails closed.
  - A short id that any ref spells, in any of Git's six places. Git answers through the ref, and warns when an object also has the prefix. This includes a ref made after open.
  - Every refusal inside CN-03's lookup also refuses a short id, because the guard runs it. That includes a malformed packed-refs file, a file named like the id in the Git directory or in a linked worktree's own Git directory, and a symbolic ref of that name.
  - A short id whose prefix another object shares, even when only one of them is committish (Departure 3).
  - A short id of 4 to 6 digits, and a SHA-256-length id (Departures 5 and 6).
  - In `reference`, a branch or remote-tracking ref whose value is an annotated tag, loose or packed. Git peels it. Git's own update-ref refuses to write one under `refs/heads/`, so only a hand-written file or a custom refspec makes one.
  - A packed peel line that disagrees with its tag object, or that follows a ref to an object that is not a tag. Git's rev-parse ignores peel lines and answers from the object.
  - Code 1 comes from `resolve` only for a missing full id. A short id never gives code 1.
- **Held.** ConnectGit keeps `defaultBranch: fail` (`connect/git.ts:240`), and names still reach `resolve` and refuse there. Both are CN-05's. No flag changes. Q6 is open. `review/**` is not edited. VectorScope's `readOperation` (`review/git.ts:162-175`) still admits only HEAD and lowercase 40-hex before `resolve`, so VectorScope's behaviour cannot change.
- **Hand-off notes for CN-05.**
  - Route a quiet verify of `<x>^{commit}` to `resolve` when x is HEAD or 7 to 40 hex digits of either case, and to `reference` otherwise. A hex-spelled ref never answers through `resolve`: a short id that any ref spells refuses there. So routing hex to `resolve` fails closed on such a ref instead of answering another commit than Git would.
  - Do not send a 7-to-39-hex spelling to `reference` to get Git's refs-first answer back. `reference` would answer the ref, and a commit-id base would depend on live refs again, which review round one removed. If CN-05 wants a hex-spelled branch as a named base, it decides that and records it.
  - With that routing, only 4-to-6-hex and describe-shaped names with no ref reach `reference`'s object-id refusal, and they fail closed.
  - Code 1 now comes only from a missing full id in `resolve` and from CN-03's two reads, never from a short id. `mergeBase()`'s candidate loop (`adapters/vectorscope.ts:1167-1171`) may move on to the next candidate only on code 1.
- **Receipts.** All runs were local, one file at a time, through `/home/user/.tools/vector-serial`, with `--timeout 30000`.
  - *How the Linux cases ran.* As in CN-02 and CN-03, through `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/as-ci.sh` (`unshare -m`, Python pointed at 3.12, then `setpriv` to uid 1000), because this container runs as root and ReadOnlyGit refuses root. Git here is 2.43.0.
  - *Experiments,* with Git 2.43.0 under `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn04/exp`:
    - `update-ref` refuses a tag object under `refs/heads/`.
    - `pack-refs --all` writes `^<commit>` after a nested tag and after a branch file or a remote-tracking ref that holds a tag, and `^<tree>` after a tag of a tree and a nested one.
    - rev-parse resolves the first three and rejects the tree tags.
    - `--disambiguate` lists the object for a lowercase or uppercase prefix and for a full id, exits 0 with no output when nothing matches, and prints nothing for a 3-digit prefix.
    - The blobs "collision 26993\n" and "collision 30025\n" are `2acdf8cd…` and `2acdf8c3…`.

Each ran against the four cases, failed the case named, and was then undone by copying back the final source and checking that hash. No `git checkout` or `git restore` touched the worktree.

## 2026-11-15 — CI repair after CN-04: newer Git in two fixtures, and the review pipeline pin

Source: `connect-finish` at `4ea10a92`. This repair changes `packages/core/test/read-only-git.test.ts`, one constant in `packages/core/src/review/model-evidence.ts` and this log. It changes no product code path, route, schema, generated file, flag or workflow.

- **What failed.** Vector Codium run `43681211114` (head `c6875c40`, CN-03). Shard 4/4 (job `132712042522`) failed `packages/core/test/read-only-git.test.ts`, and shard 1/4 (job `132712042530`) failed `packages/core/test/review/model-evidence.test.ts`, beside the inherited `docs-config-reference.test.ts`. Both files ran in the first minute of their shard, before the 5,000-line tail that the job-log API returns, so their error lines could not be read. The causes below come from the code, from a local run and from Git's own source. The runner's Git is 2.56.1 (the job's `git version` line); every local receipt in CN-03 and CN-04 used Git 2.43.0.
- **The review pipeline pin.** "scope > the pipeline's sources are pinned next to REVIEW_PIPELINE_REVISION" pins a digest over `REVIEW_PIPELINE_FILES`, which includes `packages/core/src/read-only-git.ts`. CN-03 and CN-04 changed that file and left the digest as it was. Reproduced at `4ea10a92`: 29 pass, 1 fail, printing `1012154642a04524`. `REVIEW_PIPELINE_SOURCES` is now that digest. `REVIEW_PIPELINE_REVISION` stays 4. CN-03's two reads have no caller yet. CN-04 changes `resolve` only for input it refused before (7 to 39 hex digits), and HEAD and full ids keep the same Git invocation, so no review that ran before reads a different answer. No certificates exist (main-integration-2026-11-14.md). This is an ownership deviation: spec §7.1 gives core `src/review/**` to VectorScope, and CN-01 said that it would not be edited. The edit is the one the pin asks for from whoever changes a pinned source. The VectorScope owner may still decide to bump the revision. Any later package that edits a pinned source (read-only-git.ts, engine `connect/hub.ts` or `connect/adapters/vectorscope.ts`, core `connect/policy.ts`) runs `test/review/model-evidence.test.ts` and updates the digest in the same commit. A merge that brings in another change to a pinned file recomputes it.
- **Describe output since Git 2.49 (CN-03's refusal case).** The case required live Git to resolve `refs/heads/x-g<7-HEX>^{commit}`. Since Git 2.49 (`get_describe_name` in object-name.c now calls `ref_and_count_parts_valid`), Git reads a name as describe output only when a ref name and a count come before `-g`, as in `v1.0-3-g<hex>`. On 2.56.1 that rev-parse fails, so the precondition throws. This is the failure inferred for `c6875c40`. The live-Git row now uses `refs/heads/x-1-g<7-HEX>`, which Git 2.43 and 2.56 both read as describe output. `refs/heads/x-g<7-HEX>` stays as a row that is only checked to refuse, because Git before 2.49 reads it as an object id. The view's describe pattern stays as broad as Git 2.43's. Against Git 2.49 and later it refuses some names that Git reports missing, and the review fails closed (known strictness).
- **pack-refs since Git 2.55 (CN-04's tag case).** This was predicted, not observed: no completed run holds CN-04 yet. The case packs a repository whose `refs/heads/at-tag` holds a tag object. Since Git 2.55 the check that refuses a non-commit on a branch is made in `ref_transaction_update` (refs.c), which `pack-refs` goes through, so `pack-refs --all` dies there. The test now removes the loose branch file before `pack-refs`. It then writes the branch's record, `<tag> refs/heads/at-tag` and `^<commit>`, as Git 2.43's `pack-refs` wrote it, in sorted place before the first record, and asserts the header and that position. Live Git and the view then read the packed form as before.
- CN-03's and CN-04's experiment notes and known strictness describe Git 2.43.0. Their describe-output and `pack-refs` statements hold only for Git before 2.49 and 2.55.
- **Receipts.** `test/review/model-evidence.test.ts`: 30 pass, 0 fail. `test/read-only-git.test.ts`, run unprivileged as CI runs it, with Git 2.43.0: 70 pass, 9 skip, 0 fail. Git 2.56.1 could not be run here, so the 2.56 behaviour was read from v2.56.1's object-name.c, refs.c, refs/files-backend.c and refs/packed-backend.c. `bun typecheck` passed in packages/core, and `prettier --check` passed on both code files. The Linux unit shards of the dispatched Vector Codium run are the authority for the 2.56 rows.

## 2026-11-15 — C3c: connect branch reviews accept named and default bases (CN-05)

Source: `connect-finish` at `8e547175`. This package changes `packages/engine/src/connect/git.ts`, `packages/app/src/features/agents/agent-dashboard-model.ts`, their tests (`packages/engine/test/connect/git.test.ts`, `packages/engine/test/connect/vectorscope.test.ts`, `packages/engine/test/cli/connected.test.ts`, `packages/app/src/features/agents/agent-dashboard-model.test.ts`), this log, connected-workspace-handoff.md and the spec's status line. It changes no core file, `review/**`, `adapters/vectorscope.ts`, `hub.ts`, route, ledger, schema, generated file, flag, workflow or public page, and no export. `REVIEW_PIPELINE_FILES` and `REVIEW_PIPELINE_SOURCES` are unchanged (core `test/review/model-evidence.test.ts` passes as it is). As the orchestrator instructed, the package is handed back uncommitted for the lead's commit, as CN-03 and CN-04 were.

- **What changed.**
  - *`query()`* (`connect/git.ts:82-91`). `rev-parse --verify --quiet --end-of-options <x>^{commit}` now refuses an empty `x` or one that starts with `-`. HEAD and 7 to 40 hex digits of either case go to core `resolve` as written. Any other `x` goes to core `reference` as `{ kind: "reference", name: x }`, and core validates the name. `Query` admits the `reference` variant. `default-branch` stays out of `Query`, so no argv reaches it.
  - *`defaultBranch`* (`connect/git.ts:273`, `defaultBranch: fail` before). It refuses a blocked view or another directory, then makes the private view's `default-branch` read. Code 1 with no bytes returns undefined, which keeps `mergeBase()`'s "Could not find the default branch to compare with. Pass one as the base." refusal (`adapters/vectorscope.ts:1176-1178`). Code 0 with `refs/remotes/origin/<name>\n` returns `{ name: "<name>", ref: "origin/<name>" }`, the shape `mergeBase()`'s candidates take (`origin/<name>`, then `<name>`, :1164-1166). Anything else poisons the view. There is no fallback to the ordinary Git service.
  - *One shared private read with a per-view pin* (`connect/git.ts:166-186`). `run` and `defaultBranch` share `read(operation: ReadOnlyGit.Read)`, which keeps the post-read `active()` check. Within one `ConnectGit.use`, the first answer for each `reference` spelling and for `default-branch` is kept and returned again. A view keeps at most 64 answers: a new spelling past that poisons the view before any read, and a kept one is still answered. Connect's own emitters use a few spellings per view, so only an unexpected caller reaches the cap.
  - *The app* (`app/src/features/agents/agent-dashboard-model.ts:63-65`). `directoryReviewTarget` admits 7 to 40 hex digits (64 before), because ReadOnlyGit reads SHA-1 only, and sends the base lowercased. The request body, the sessionStorage reservation key and `directoryRunMatches` all derive from this one value.
  - *No change* to `cli/cmd/connected.ts`, `tool/connect.ts`, the command template, `adapters/vectorscope.ts`, `hub.ts`, `owner.ts` or core `read-only-git.ts`.
- **What it closes.**
  - C3 (review connect:correctness-security #3; v2-complete-plan §4.1 C3, §5 step 12), fixed, Linux-only. Each start path in the brief is covered:
    - `connect {action: review, target: branch}` with no base: vectorscope.test.ts "with no base, the branch origin/HEAD names is the base" (:1470).
    - With `base: "main"`: "a base named by a branch is resolved in connect's view and reviewed from its merge base" (:1450). git.test.ts (:319) also runs this request through the actual adapter, CLI, `Review.run` and context collector, and the repository's required clean filter never runs.
    - `/vectorscope branch main`: command.test.ts:401 shows that it reaches the hub as `{ kind: "branch", base: "main" }`, using a fake adapter. The two cases above take it from there.
    - `vector connected review --base main`: connected.test.ts "a branch base is sent as written" (:55) shows the exact POST body, which decodes as `Connect.ReviewInput`. `owner.ts:348/438` and `tool/connect.ts:203-204` pass the target through unchanged.
    - The app's abbreviated or uppercase `baseCommit`: the app test and "an abbreviated uppercase base resolves to its commit" (:1524).
  - This supersedes v2-complete-plan §4.1's C3 "Remains". Following CN-01's practice, the plan is not edited.
  - The first sentence of the 2026-11-14 "Remaining Tier B boundaries": CN-03 closed the core half, resolution inside the private view, and CN-05 closes the connect half, because connect now uses only those reads, with no ordinary-Git fallback. Together they close that sentence on Linux. This reconciles CN-03's entry, which claimed the whole sentence, with CN-04's, which kept it open. The directory-key and orphan sentences stay open for CN-08 and CN-09.
- **Plan critique.** All seven points were taken, two of them in part.
  1. *A named or default base could move during one review (major).* Taken, with the per-view pin. git.test.ts:551-556 moves origin/main and origin/HEAD with live Git inside one view and gets the first answers again; a fresh view gets the new ones (:562-563). Mutation M11 removes the pin and fails :554. The cap is pinned at :567-584 (M12, M13). The pin is per spelling. `mergeBase()` sends the same spelling on every pass (the request's base, or candidates built from the pinned default branch), so the three check-1 passes and the review see one base. A fix runs in a new `ConnectGit.use` (`adapters/vectorscope.ts:645`, with `ConnectFix.targetOf` returning the name target, `fix.ts:120-122`), so it resolves the name again. Making the fix use the reviewed 40-hex base would need `adapters/vectorscope.ts` or `fix.ts` and a re-pin of the review pipeline, so it is recorded here and not done.
  2. *`defaultBranch` widens connect's view during the review (major).* Taken. Inside the review, connect's view is `Git.Service` (`fallback: true` in the read-for-read test), so code in that context can now get an answer from `defaultBranch`; before, it poisoned the view. `tool/memory.ts:472` is one such caller. Its next step, `git.show(root, "origin/<name>", file)`, sends `cat-file blob origin/<name>:<file>`, which `object()` refuses, so the review is still poisoned. Two rows join the parametrised "collector reads are answered by VectorScope's ReviewGit view" test (vectorscope.test.ts:629-646). Each row there carries its target, the spelling check 1 verifies its base as, and the calls to members other than `run`; only the commit row's base is filled in at run time. The two new rows:
     - named base: the quiet verify of `main^{commit}` appears only where check 1 makes it, and the other members are `{ branch: 1 }`;
     - default base: the quiet verify of `origin/main^{commit}`, and `{ branch: 1, defaultBranch: 2 }`, one call for each check-1 pass in the spied window (before and after the review) and none inside it.
  3. *The done criterion was met only by composition (minor).* Taken in part. One Linux real-adapter case was added (git.test.ts:319). The tool and CLI legs are still joined at the request shape (Departure 9).
  4. *Bookkeeping conflict (minor).* Taken, with the critique's wording (above).
  5. *Gaps in the test specs (minor).* Taken.
     - `root` comes from `rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD`, and both git cases assert that it differs from `head`.
     - `main` is created explicitly, with `update-ref` in git case A and `branch -f` in the vectorscope cases. Ordinary `defaultBranch` is asserted first, but only as defined, not as `main`: a configured `init.defaultBranch` naming another existing branch would win there.
     - The new `local(...)` cases pass 60_000 or 120_000.
     - Case A has no uppercase short-id row, because CN-04's row at git.test.ts:442 already covers it; case A keeps a lowercase one, which mutation M3 needs.
  6. *Bases a user is likely to type now give the generic refusal (minor).* Taken. vectorscope.test.ts:1559 sends `deadbee` and `HEAD~1`. A precondition shows that no object in the fixture has the prefix `deadbee`. Both are refused as unavailable, and nothing is reviewed. No tool or CLI description changed.
  7. *`read`'s parameter type (minor).* Taken. It is `ReadOnlyGit.Read`, and the pin lives in it.
- **Departures from the brief and the plan.**
  1. *Only the quiet verify is mapped,* not the brief's `[--quiet]`. CN-02 removed the non-quiet form, which is VectorScope's collector shape (`review/source.ts:153`). git.test.ts:675 and case B's last row pin it refusing. Connect's only emitter, `commitOf`, is quiet.
  2. *No lowercasing in the engine.* Core's `resolve` checks the spelling as given against refs, then lowercases it. If the engine lowercased, an uppercase-hex branch name would answer an object where Git answers the branch. Case B's `<upper>` row and mutation M2 pin this.
  3. *The default comes only from origin/HEAD.* Ordinary `Git.defaultBranch` also tries a single or `upstream` remote, `init.defaultBranch`, and local `main` or `master`. Connect refuses with the existing input message instead. vectorscope.test.ts:1487 and mutation M5 pin this. The comment at `adapters/vectorscope.ts:1157` ("found as `vector review` finds it") is now narrower inside connect. It is left unedited, because the file is pinned.
  4. *A branch whose name is 7 to 40 hex digits cannot be a bare named base.* `refs/heads/<name>` works (case B).
  5. *Names of 41 or more hex digits give code 1,* so they get the input refusal, not poison (case A).
  6. *Core refusal reasons still surface as ConnectGit's generic "unavailable" message.* Examples: `main~1`, a missing or ambiguous short id, a name that is both a branch and a tag, and an all-hex branch name such as `20261115`. vectorscope.test.ts:1559 pins two of them. This is unchanged and unscheduled.
  7. *App lowercasing* changes the reservation key only for uppercase bases, which never reached a review before.
  8. *The pin is per view,* so a fix resolves its source review's base name again (critique point 1). A moved base cannot widen the fix: owned paths are the reviewed findings' paths that are still changed (ConnectPolicy.fixable, fix.ts:43), and merging asks connect_write again with the diff.
  9. *The tool and CLI legs are joined at the request shape,* not proven by one end-to-end run (critique point 3).
  10. *Test-only refactor.* git.test.ts `setup()`'s `run` takes an optional target. Its two existing callers are unchanged.
  11. *The plan's (2b) assertion* that ordinary Git "would have answered main" is `toBeDefined()` (critique point 5).
- **Review round one.** Seven non-blocking findings; all taken.
  1. Line citations in this entry now name test titles and match the working tree; the read-for-read refactor (3) moved the later vectorscope.test.ts cases down six lines. The handoff's `connect/git.ts` citations were updated too.
  2. The pin key names `"default-branch"` directly instead of `operation.kind`.
  3. The read-for-read test's rows carry their target, verified spelling and other-member calls, which replaces two nested ternaries and a lookup.
  4. The pin goes beyond the brief, which asked only for the query mapping and `defaultBranch`. It is deliberate (critique point 1, Departure 8, M11), and the lead's commit body says so.
  5. The pin is capped at 64 answers per view (What changed; git.test.ts:567-584; M12 and M13). Before CN-05 any such caller poisoned the view, so the cap fails closed as that did.
  6. The `defaultBranch` comment now says that `mergeBase()` tries `origin/<name>` before `<name>`, both in this view, and the inner comment is gone. This matches the "falls back to the local branch of that name" case (vectorscope.test.ts:1509).
  7. Departure 8 says why a moved base cannot widen a fix.
- **Known strictness.** CN-03's and CN-04's carry over. For example, a name that matches in two of Git's places refuses as ambiguous, a branch whose value is a tag object refuses, and a short id needs at least 7 digits.
- **Held.** Q1 and Q6 stay open. Every flag stays off. `review/**` is untouched. Off Linux, ReadOnlyGit still refuses and the review stays unavailable (the vectorscope cases fall back to `unsupportedGit`, and the git cases skip through `local`).
- **Receipts.** Every `bun` command ran through `/home/user/.tools/vector-serial`, one test file at a time, with `--timeout 30000`. Linux-gated engine files ran under `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/as-ci.sh` (a private mount namespace, uid 1000, `CI=true`, Git 2.43.0). Logs are in `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn05/`.

## 2026-11-15 — C2 remainder: projects opened through a link (CN-06)

Source: `connect-finish` at `fc0ec0c3` (CN-05). This package changes `packages/engine/src/connect/git.ts`, `packages/engine/src/connect/adapters/vectorscope.ts` and one constant in `packages/core/src/review/model-evidence.ts`. Its tests are in `packages/engine/test/connect/vectorscope.test.ts` and `packages/engine/test/connect/git.test.ts`. It also updates this log, connected-workspace-handoff.md and the spec's status line. It changes no `hub.ts`, route, ledger, schema, generated file, flag, workflow, public page, `review/**` logic or task isolation code. The only new exports are `ConnectGit.LINKED` and `ConnectGit.canonical`. The package was built uncommitted and landed through the lane's commit gate as `fix(connect): report symlinked project paths honestly`.

- **Confirm first.** The review finding (connect:correctness-security #2) says `ConnectGit.use` refuses when `realpath(directory) !== path.resolve(directory)` (`connect/git.ts:319-320` at `fc0ec0c3`), while VectorScope's `describe()` reports ready on Linux. The tests were written first and run against the `fc0ec0c3` sources.
  - *The instance directory is canonical whenever its folder exists at load.* InstanceStore `load`, `reload` and `withFresh` all run `FSUtil.resolve` first (`project/instance-store.ts:161`, `:210`, `:353`). That is `realpathSync`, which falls back to the path as written only on ENOENT (core `fs-util.ts:228-236`). The HTTP middleware (`server/routes/instance/httpapi/middleware/instance-context.ts:31`), the CLI (`cli/effect-cmd.ts:88`) and the TUI all load through the store.
  - *`ConfigPaths.canonicalScope` is not what makes it canonical.* It runs only with the flag on (`config/config.ts:1914`), and it canonicalises a copy of the scope for config discovery and trust (`config/paths.ts:13-24`). `InstanceContext` stays as it was, so `ctx.worktree` keeps whatever the store was given.
  - *A discovered worktree is canonical too.* It is Git's `--show-toplevel`, run from the real folder (core `git.ts:188-204`). With Git 2.43.0, toplevel prints the real path when run through a link.
  - *Every connect caller sends the instance's own directory.* That covers `tool/connect.ts:206` and `:270`, and `packages/engine/src/connect/owner.ts:347`, `:428`, `:437`, `:476` and `:631` (each from `InstanceState.context`). So the finding's scenario, a project opened through a link, never reaches the equality check. The vectorscope cases "opened through a link to its folder" and "… to its parent folder" passed at `fc0ec0c3`: the instance is the real folder, VectorScope lists ready, and the review completes.
  - *The real mismatch is a worktree kept as written.* `TaskIsolation.loadWork` loads `{ directory: record.directory, worktree: record.directory, project }` (`task/isolation.ts:1415`). `record.directory` is under `Global.Path.data` (`:274`), and that data folder may pass through a link (the comment at `:657`). The store canonicalises only the directory, so `ctx.worktree` stays lexical. In that instance `describe()` said ready, while `use()` refused through core `metadataHead`, which refuses a root that is not its own realpath (`read-only-git.ts:1103-1107`). The refusal used the generic "This review's Git reads cannot be inspected safely, so Vector stopped it." At `fc0ec0c3`, the vectorscope case "an instance whose worktree is a link …" failed because the member was `ready`, and the git case failed at its second row because the refusal was generic.
  - *Why the fix was built.* The brief's stop condition, that instance directories are always canonical, holds for the directory but not for the worktree. The brief's "Otherwise" covers "the instance directory or worktree". Its "Done when" asks that the member state match what a review would do, and in this instance it did not. So the worktree half is in scope, and section 2 of the plan was built rather than stopping at a test-only commit.
- **What changed.**
  - *`connect/git.ts`.* `LINKED` (:305) holds the brief's sentence. `canonical(...folders)` (:311) realpaths each folder and compares it with `path.resolve`; it rejects when a folder is missing. In `use()` (:337), when the root is the instance's worktree (no task root) and it is not its own real path, the review is refused as `unavailable` with `LINKED`. This happens before `active()` and before any Git read. A `directory` that is not its own real path keeps the generic refusal (:333), and so does the hub's verified task root. The `catch` (:359) passes a `Connect.Refusal` through and maps anything else to the generic refusal, as before. The refusal conditions are unchanged, because core already refused a non-canonical root. Only the wording changes, and only for that one case.
  - *`adapters/vectorscope.ts` `describe()`* (:380-381). After the Linux check, VectorScope is listed `unavailable` with `LINKED` when `ctx.directory` or the root (`ctx.worktree`, or the directory for a non-Git instance) is not its own real path. The check runs before `plan()`, so no model is resolved. It uses `Effect.tryPromise`, so a folder that has gone fails as a typed error into the existing `catchCause`, which gives the generic "Connect could not check VectorScope's review model." (plan critique point 10).
  - *The hub needed no change.* Its gate (`hub.ts:922-927`) turns that state into "VectorScope is not ready: Open this project through its real folder path to use connected reviews.". It does so before the `connect` question, any model resolution or ConnectGit.
  - *The pin.* `REVIEW_PIPELINE_SOURCES` is now `99d748c262992c83`, the digest core `test/review/model-evidence.test.ts` printed. The old digest passed with the `fc0ec0c3` adapter source, so the pin was green before this change. `REVIEW_PIPELINE_REVISION` stays 4, because `describe()` decides whether a review can start, not what a review finds or posts. This is an ownership deviation under the rule in "CI repair after CN-04": spec §7.1 gives core `src/review/**` to VectorScope, and this is the one edit the pin asks for from whoever changes a pinned source. The commit body and the PR description name this re-pin as required by core `test/review/model-evidence.test.ts`, say that `REVIEW_PIPELINE_REVISION` stays 4, and ask the VectorScope owner to acknowledge the deviation and decide whether the revision goes up. The edit stays limited to the digest constant.
- **What it closes.** The symlinked-path part of C2 (review connect:correctness-security #2; v2-complete-plan §4.1 F4/C2 and §5 step 12), on Linux. With the platform part fixed on 2026-11-14, C2 is closed on Linux for instance reviews; a `task`-target review under a linked data folder is still refused generically (unscheduled, Observed (1)). Following CN-01's practice, the plan is not edited.
- **Decisions.**
  1. *Links are never resolved and admitted.* Read rules match paths as written, so admitting the resolved folder would widen them; that needs its own design. Mutation M7 shows the tests catch it.
  2. *The new wording appears only for the stable shape* (plan critique point 1). The store opens an instance at its real folder, so when `use()` sees a non-canonical instance `directory`, the folder was swapped after it loaded. directory-live.test.ts's `retarget()` does exactly that during consent. Telling the user to open "its real folder path" there would point them at the swapped-in checkout. So that case keeps the generic refusal, and so does the hub's verified task root. The tests are git row 1 with M9, and git row 3 with M5.
  3. *For a task-worktree instance the advice cannot be acted on* (plan critique point 2). The reachable case is a TaskIsolation work instance whose worktree lies under a data folder reached through a link, for example a linked home or `$XDG_DATA_HOME`. The user never opened that folder, and reopening the project does not help, because the cause is the linked data folder. The brief sets the reason's text, so `describe()` and `use()` use it as given, and the member state and the refusal still match. A reason that names the data folder is an unscheduled follow-up for the lead (Observed (4)).
  4. *`describe()`'s directory half is defence in depth.* No InstanceStore path gives a non-canonical directory for a folder that exists at load, so M3 (checking only the root) survives.
  5. *The "parent folder" row builds its own parent* (plan critique point 6). The repository is made inside a dedicated `tmpdirScoped()` folder, and that folder is linked, not the shared temp root.
  6. *No directory-live assertion was added* (plan critique point 1's option). One was tried: in "replacing the selected canonical directory during consent cannot authorize another checkout", the summary must not contain the `LINKED` wording. A probe showed that the case ends `failed`, refusal `not-allowed`, with the directory ledger's "The directory review result could not be saved within its receipt limits." That happened both with the fix and under M9, so no ConnectGit wording ever reaches that summary. The assertion could never fail, so it was removed, and directory-live.test.ts is unchanged. Git row 1 tests `use()`'s wording for a swapped folder directly.
- **Plan critique.** All ten points were taken.
  - Point 1 (major) narrowed the `use()` wording (Decision 2). In git row 1, `use(link)` under a canonical instance expects the generic refusal, and row 2 expects `LINKED`. M9 was added. The optional directory-live assertion was tried and dropped (Decision 6).
  - Point 2 (major): Decision 3, and the `use()` comment now separates the hub's verified task root from a task-worktree instance.
  - Point 3: `test/server/httpapi-connect.test.ts` and `test/connect/loop.test.ts` joined the regression list. `test/cli/connected.test.ts` only formats results (`formatConnectedResult`) and never goes through the hub, so it was not run.
  - Point 4: the canonicalScope and ENOENT points are in Confirm first.
  - Point 5: `connect/owner.ts` is cited by its full path.
  - Point 6: Decision 5.
  - Point 7: git row 3 maps `gitCommonDirectory: metadata.commonDirectory` and `tip: metadata.head`. It also calls `use(test.directory, …, root)`, so the canonical directory isolates the task-root branch.
  - Point 8: decided in Confirm first.
  - Point 9: Observed (2) is marked as read from code.
  - Point 10: `Effect.tryPromise`.
- **Observed and unscheduled.**
  1. A `task`-target review whose task worktree lies under a linked data folder is still refused with the generic message. The hub sends `placed.directory` (`hub.ts:1332`), and `describe()` runs in the requester's instance, so it cannot see the task's worktree.
  2. *Read from code, not run.* A non-Git instance (worktree `/`) lists VectorScope by its model state, because `describe()` plans at `ctx.directory`. `ConnectGit.use` refuses it at `connect/git.ts:328` (`root === "/"`). The member state and a review would then differ in the same way this package fixes, but no test confirms it. Suggested follow-up for the lead, outside CN-06: a test for a non-Git instance and, if it confirms the mismatch, `describe()` returning unavailable when `ctx.worktree === "/"`.
  3. The read-deny alias gap (`cf2373c4`, integration-runtime-boundaries.md) stays open. CN-06 neither resolves nor admits a link, so it does not close the gap.
  4. A clearer reason for the task-worktree instance (Decision 3).
  5. The swapped-folder directory review above is reported as "could not be saved within its receipt limits". That is a ledger message, and it does not say the folder changed. CN-06 does not touch that path. It sits next to C6's ledger work (CN-08, CN-09) but is not scheduled there.
- **Held.** Q1 and Q6 stay open. Every flag stays off. Off Linux, `describe()` still reports the platform reason first, and the new cases skip.
- **Mutations.** They ran on `connect/git.ts` sha256 `458e3f6dcb76d2e9ec430f3665631d2517759fa82d5d94d262c5800753542277` and `adapters/vectorscope.ts` `5bcbea70d5ae53ec429536a375439707f004b9d7d9d0870c5753ea3bbf94f342`. After review round one `connect/git.ts` is `222c5734d50d8fb426e42d930059df074c0c1040ac2f2d2a611f1e3588c05cc8`; only `canonical()`'s body changed, its result is the same for every input, and the mutations were not re-run on it (the git and vectorscope files were, in Receipts). `adapters/vectorscope.ts` is unchanged. Each mutation ran with `-t` on the named block or case. The saved source was then copied back, and `sha256sum -c` passed after every one (logs in `mut/`). The vectorscope link block holds rows (a) folder, (a) parent and (b). The git case holds rows 1 to 3, then the `entered` and control checks.

    | Mutation | Result |
    | --- | --- |
    | M1: no `describe()` check | Fails vectorscope (b), where the member is `ready` |
    | M2: `describe()` checks only the directory | Fails vectorscope (b) |
    | M3: `describe()` checks only the root | Survives: the directory half is defence in depth (Decision 4) |
    | M4: generic wording for an instance worktree | Fails git row 2 |
    | M5: `LINKED` for the hub's task root too | Fails git row 3 |
    | M6: `catch: () => refusal()` restored | Fails git row 2 |
    | M7: resolve and admit (no `realpath` equality check) | Fails git row 1, where the body ran and the message is `undefined` |
    | M8: `use()` checks the directory, not the root | Fails git row 2 |
    | M9: `LINKED` for a non-canonical directory | Fails git row 1. It survives the dropped directory-live assertion (Decision 6) |
- **Review round one.** The package review raised seven findings, none blocking.
  1. *Taken.* `canonical()` compared through an index into a second array and needed `folders[index]!`. It now compares inside the map, `(await Promise.all(folders.map(async (folder) => (await realpath(folder)) === path.resolve(folder)))).every(Boolean)`, so the `no-unnecessary-type-assertion` warning on it is gone. The function grew by one line, so the later `connect/git.ts` lines moved down by one, and the citations in this entry and the handoff follow.
  2. *Kept, with a reason.* The link block's `const { hub, requester } = yield* requesterIn` and `const { sessions, hub, requester } = yield* requesterIn` stay. They follow the file's own pattern for its setup helper, `const { directory, hub, requester } = yield* setup()` (`vectorscope.test.ts:366`, `:551`, `:754` and about twenty more), and the `recorder()` and `asker()` destructuring beside them. Switching this one block to dot access would make it the odd one out.

  4. *Taken as advised.* The non-Git instance stays out of CN-06. Observed (2) now carries the suggested follow-up.
  5. *Taken.* "C2 is closed on Linux" overstated the fix. What it closes, the handoff bullet and the spec's status line now say C2 is closed on Linux for instance reviews, and that a `task`-target review under a linked data folder is still refused generically (unscheduled).
  6. *Taken.* Receipts now say which cases need an unprivileged uid.
