## 2026-11-15 — C5 remainder: git status survival pinned, stale private-Git snapshots swept (CN-07)

Source: `connect-finish` at `539ec394` (CN-06). This package changes `packages/core/src/read-only-git.ts`, `packages/engine/src/connect/owner.ts` and one constant in `packages/core/src/review/model-evidence.ts`. Its tests are in `packages/core/test/read-only-git.test.ts`, `packages/engine/test/connect/background.test.ts` and `packages/engine/test/connect/owner.test.ts`, with a no-op sweep for engine test graphs in the new `packages/engine/test/fixture/connect-sweep.ts`, used by `test/connect/after-run.test.ts`, `dismissal.test.ts`, `directory.test.ts`, `directory-live.test.ts`, `test/server/httpapi-connect.test.ts` and `test/server/httpapi-connect-fixture.ts`. It also updates this log, connected-workspace-handoff.md and the spec's status line. It changes no route, schema, generated file, flag, workflow, public page, `hub.ts`, `connect/git.ts`, adapter or `review/**` logic. The only new exports are `ReadOnlyGit.sweepStale` and `ConnectOwner.Sweep`. The package was built uncommitted, then revised after one review round, and lands through the lane's commit gate.

- **What it closes.** C5 (review connect:correctness-security #5; v2-complete-plan §4.1 C5 and §5 step 12), on Linux. It supersedes that row's **B** ("compare HEAD plus the index tree OID and persist the private index") and **C** ("startup temp cleanup") entries. Following CN-01's practice, the plan is not edited. What C5 now means, exactly:
  - Stat-cache rewrites of the checkout's index no longer stop a review between its helper prompts. That covers `git status` with optional locks on, and `git add` of a file whose content did not change.
  - Staging a real content change between helper prompts still stops the review, with the plain "This review's Git reads cannot be inspected safely, so Vector stopped it." That holds when the file itself is then put back, so only the index differs. It is accepted fail-closed behaviour, not a gap: the review would otherwise report on a diff that no longer matches the index.
  - Stale `vector-readonly-git-*` folders from crashed or killed processes are reclaimed while Connect is on.
- **At `539ec394` before any change.** The brief does not start with "confirm first", so the confirm-first rule does not apply. The first three background rows were still run against `539ec394`'s `read-only-git.ts` (the product code that serves the check) with this package's tests, and all three passed. The fourth row, added in the review round, ran only on this package's sources. The semantic index compare (`unchanged`, `read-only-git.ts:1592` at `539ec394`, `:1607` now; commits `5c68981e` and `4ab747d2`) already let a review through a stat refresh. So the connect-level test pins existing behaviour, and `unchanged` is a regression target only; it is not changed. The git-status row passed, and the staged-change row ended `refused` with the plain message.
- **Why the private index is still not persisted.** The finding's "persist a refreshed private index" is rejected. `status()` keeps the normalized index stat fields empty because a refreshed Git stat cache once missed a real equal-size edit with the mtime restored, despite a changed high-resolution ctime (`read-only-git.ts:2003-2005`, `:1988-1990` at `539ec394`). The core test "a refreshed private stat cache still detects equal-size content edits with the original mtime restored" (`read-only-git.test.ts:1994`) pins that. The cost is that every check re-hashes the worktree through a stat-less private `git status`, bounded by `INTEGRITY.milliseconds` (10 s per command). That per-check re-hash cost stays **Tier C** and is unscheduled.
- **What changed.**
  - *Core `read-only-git.ts`.* The constants sit beside `retained` (:265-269): open()'s mkdtemp name shape `SNAPSHOT`, the marker file name `owner`, the marker shape `"<pid> <process start, epoch ms>\n"`, and the sweep's default age (24 h) and floor (1 h). `open()` writes the marker right after its `chmod 0700` (:1417-1426), with mode 0600 and `wx`. The write is best-effort: if it fails (for example `ENOSPC`), the folder has no marker, age alone protects it, and the view still opens. `close()` removes the marker with the folder. Git never reads it: `HOME` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` point at the folder, but global config, attributes and excludes are `/dev/null`. `sweepStale` (:2070) with `sweepEntry` (:2089), `claimed` (:2104) and `alive` (:2124) is new. No read, status, finding or post changes.
  - *Engine `connect/owner.ts`.* `ConnectOwner.Sweep` (:70) is a `Context.Reference` that defaults to `ReadOnlyGit.sweepStale`, following `TaskIsolation.Limits`. The layer binds it (:96). Just before `init`, and only when `flags.experimentalConnect` is on, it forks one sweep of `os.tmpdir()` into the layer's scope (:712-726, the `tool/truncate.ts` cleanup pattern). Removals or failures are logged at info level, and a failed sweep logs a warning. Nothing waits on it.
  - *The pin.* `REVIEW_PIPELINE_SOURCES` is now `b9b7e47e95691d6e`, the digest core `test/review/model-evidence.test.ts` printed for the final sources (before the edit: 29 pass, 1 fail). The old digest `99d748c262992c83` passed with `539ec394`'s `read-only-git.ts`, so the pin was green before this change. `REVIEW_PIPELINE_REVISION` stays 4, because the marker and the sweep change no read, finding or post. This is an ownership deviation under the rule in "CI repair after CN-04": spec §7.1 gives core `src/review/**` to VectorScope, and this is the one edit the pin asks of whoever changes a pinned source. The edit is limited to the digest constant. The commit body and the PR description must name this re-pin as required by core `test/review/model-evidence.test.ts`, say that `REVIEW_PIPELINE_REVISION` stays 4, and ask the VectorScope owner to acknowledge the deviation and decide whether the revision goes up. It also needs the lead's explicit acceptance as an exception to the do-not-touch rule for this package.
- **The sweep's rules.** `sweepStale({ directory, now, olderThanMs? })` returns `{ removed, kept, failed }`.
  - Off Linux it returns zeros and touches nothing, because `open()` refuses before mkdtemp there.
  - A threshold that is not finite or is under one hour, or a `now` that is not finite, returns zeros and reads nothing. The floor exists because a short threshold could take a folder another process made a moment ago, between its mkdtemp and its marker write.
  - Only direct children whose names match `/^vector-readonly-git-[A-Za-z0-9]{6}$/` are considered. Other names are never stat'ed or counted. A missing or unreadable `directory` gives zeros.
  - The names come from one `readdir`. Iterating `opendir` was suggested in review to bound memory, and is not used: Bun 1.3.14's `opendir` reads the whole listing on its first read (a probe read one entry of 5,000, deleted all 5,000, and the iterator still returned the other 4,999) and holds it as Dirents, so it would not bound memory and would hold more per entry. It also defers a missing folder's `ENOENT` to the first read. The names array is short-lived and the sweep is forked, so its size stays Tier C.
  - Entries are handled one at a time. An entry is removed only when all of these hold:
    - it `lstat`s as a real directory (a link is kept);
    - it is owned by this uid with mode exactly 0700;
    - the newer of its mtime and ctime is older than the cutoff;
    - its marker names no live process;
    - a second `lstat` shows the same directory, dev and inode.
  - A missing marker (a folder from before CN-07, or one whose best-effort write failed) or a malformed one names no process, so age alone decides. A marker that is a link, or not a regular file, throws (`O_NOFOLLOW`), so the entry is counted `failed` and kept. Any other error on one entry is also `failed`, and the sweep goes on.
  - Liveness is `process.kill(pid, 0)`; `EPERM` counts as alive. A pid of 0, or one above 2^31-1, names no process. The start time is recorded as evidence and never compared, so a reused pid only keeps a dead folder longer.
  - Removal is `fs.rm(entry, { recursive: true, force: true })`, the call `close()` uses. Bun removes links inside the folder, and at its top, without following them; the main sweep case checks that the link targets survive.
- **When it runs.** Whenever `RuntimeFlags.experimentalConnect` is on: `VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL_CONNECT`, or the umbrella `VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL` while that key is unset, until the review-fixes lane's PUB-7. It runs once per ConnectOwner build. The server builds ConnectOwner once per process through the shared memoMap (`effect/app-runtime.ts:143`, `httpapi/server.ts:405`), so each engine or CLI process that builds it sweeps once. It is forked and never holds up startup. A failure only logs. Two processes sweeping at once are safe, because `rm` uses `force` and each entry's error is counted. It covers only that process's `os.tmpdir()`. Because every caller shares `open()`, it also reclaims dead snapshots left by VectorScope's `review/git.ts` (`ReviewGit`, which the CLI's `vector review` uses) and by task isolation's `ReadOnlyGit.inspect` (`task/isolation.ts:736`), not only connect's. The marker is written whether or not the flag is on: about 20 bytes inside the process's own private folder, which `close()` removes.
- **Known limits.** All of them fail closed: the affected process's Git view becomes unavailable on its next check, its review is refused, and nothing leaks.
  1. A process in another pid namespace that shares the same temporary folder and uid (a container, flatpak or sandbox with `/tmp` bind-mounted) gets `ESRCH` from `kill(pid, 0)`. Its live snapshot can then be removed once it is more than 24 hours old.
  2. A process still running a version from before CN-07 writes no marker, and neither does one whose best-effort write failed. When such a snapshot's top-level mtime and ctime are more than 24 hours old, only age protects it.
  3. An `rm` interrupted in a short-lived CLI leaves a partial folder. Its mtime moved, so a later sweep finishes it once the folder is old again.
- **Test graphs never sweep the machine's temporary folder.** Engine test graphs that build ConnectOwner with Connect on get `noSweep` from `test/fixture/connect-sweep.ts`, a `ConnectOwner.Sweep` that returns zeros: owner.test.ts's runner graph, after-run.test.ts's `actual` and `bounded`, dismissal.test.ts, directory.test.ts, directory-live.test.ts, `httpapi-connect-fixture.ts`'s graph, and httpapi-connect.test.ts's production and web-handler graphs. The after-run case that runs the real CLI with Connect on starts it, on Linux (the one host where the sweep acts), with `TMPDIR` set to a folder inside its own test home. background.test.ts and vectorscope.test.ts build ConnectHub, not ConnectOwner, so they never swept. The real `sweepStale` runs in tests only in core's sweep cases, each on a folder of its own. The first build let these graphs run the real sweep on the test process's temporary folder; the review round replaced that, because a test run should not change shared machine state.
- **Tests.**
  - *Core `read-only-git.test.ts`.* Each sweep case makes its own `vector-sweep-fixture-*` folder and passes it as `directory`; none sweeps the real temporary folder.
    - "sweepStale removes only old, dead, private snapshot directories of this user" (:2037) uses a named fixture table, so the expected counts come from the rows. It has nine matching names: Old001 (no marker, with links out), Dead01 (marker pid 2147483647), Junk01 (malformed marker), Fresh1 (mtime two days ahead), Live01 (this process's marker), Mode01 (0755), Link01 (a link to a real old 0700 folder), File01 (a 0700 file) and Mark01 (its `owner` is a link). It also has four real 0700 folders with non-matching names, `outside/keep.txt` and the link target. Fixture folders are chmodded explicitly, because mkdir keeps a setgid parent's bit. A sweep now and a sweep with a 3-day threshold keep all nine. NaN, Infinity, 0, -1 day and one hour less 1 ms, and a NaN `now`, give zeros and change nothing. A sweep two days ahead gives 3 removed, 5 kept and 1 failed, leaves exactly the other entries, and leaves the link targets and the live marker intact. Link01 is still a link.
    - "sweepStale keeps an old snapshot that another user owns" (:2141) runs only on Linux as root. It chowns one old matching folder to 65534 and keeps it, and removes a root-owned twin, so the uid check has a committed test.
    - "sweepStale tolerates a missing folder" (:2156) runs on every platform.
    - "sweepStale leaves everything in place off Linux" (:2165) is skipped on Linux.
    - "open() marks its private snapshot with this process, and a sweep keeps it while the process lives" (:2176) needs an unprivileged uid. Of the snapshots that appear in the temporary folder during `open()`, it keeps only those whose `owner` reads this process's marker, so another process opening a snapshot there at the same time cannot fail it. It checks that exactly one remains, that its `owner` is a 0600 regular file holding `<pid> <timeOrigin>\n`, that the same text keeps an old fabricated snapshot, that `status()` still works, and that `close()` removes the folder.
  - *Engine `background.test.ts`*, "a background review and the checkout's index" (:1105), skipped off Linux. The real VectorScope adapter's faked `runReview` prompts two helpers through connect's bridge and pauses before the second. Each row then changes the checkout, checks that the index bytes really changed and what `git diff --cached` shows, and resumes:
    1. utimes on `src/other.ts`, then `git status --porcelain` with `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=1`: completed, clean, both helpers prompted, no "cannot be inspected safely".
    2. utimes, then `git add src/other.ts` with the same content: completed, clean, both helpers prompted.
    3. A new `src/other.ts`, then `git add`: staged `src/other.ts`; refused, `unavailable`, not-reviewed, "cannot be inspected safely", and only the first helper prompted.
    4. As row 3, then the file's original content written back, so the checkout's file matches the pause and only the index differs: staged `src/other.ts`; refused the same way.
  - *Engine `owner.test.ts`.* "with Connect off the owner starts no private Git snapshot sweep" and "with Connect on the owner sweeps the temporary folder once without waiting for it" inject a fake `ConnectOwner.Sweep` that records its input and stays pending. The on case reaches its body only because the layer did not wait for the sweep. It checks one call with `directory: os.tmpdir()`, a finite `now` and the default threshold. The runner cases now get `noSweep`.
- **Mutations.** Each was one exact replacement in the saved source, run on its named cases and then copied back, with `sha256sum -c` passing after every one. M1 to M12 and E1 to E3 ran on the sources before the review round; the round changed none of the lines they mutate (it made the marker write best-effort and added a comment in `read-only-git.ts`, and left `connect/owner.ts` alone). M13 and E4 ran on the final sources. Core sweep mutations ran as root with `-t sweepStale`, and the marker and background mutations ran through the wrapper. A mutation counts as caught only when a committed test failed.

    | Mutation | Result |
    | --- | --- |
    | M1: `claimed` always false (no live check) | Caught: the main sweep case (Live01 removed) and, unprivileged, the marker case |
    | M2: no uid check | Caught only by the root-only foreign-owner case. Unprivileged runs, which CI makes, cannot catch it |
    | M3: no mode check | Caught: the main case (Mode01 removed) |
    | M4: `stat` for both `lstat` calls | Caught: the main case (Link01 followed and removed) |
    | M5: `>= cutoff` for `!(… < cutoff)` | Survives, as an equivalent: the guard rejects a non-finite `now` or threshold, and lstat times are finite, so the NaN case cannot reach the comparison. The committed NaN rows test the guard |
    | M6: no threshold guard | Caught: the main case (NaN and 0 rows) |
    | M7: no one-hour floor | Caught: the main case (0, -1 day and 59:59.999 rows) |
    | M8: no name filter | Caught: the main case (non-matching folders removed) |
    | M9: no dev/inode re-check | Survives: no test can swap the folder between the check and the removal; it is defence in depth |
    | M10: no platform guard | Survives on Linux by construction; "sweepStale leaves everything in place off Linux" covers it on non-Linux runners, which did not run here |
    | M11: marker opened without `O_NOFOLLOW` | Caught: the main case (Mark01 followed the link to a live marker and was kept, not failed) |
    | M12: no age check | Caught: the main case |
    | M13: `open()` writes its marker under another name | Caught, unprivileged: the marker case (no snapshot of this process found) |
    | E1: owner sweeps with Connect off | Caught: "with Connect off …" |
    | E2: the sweep awaited, not forked | Caught: "with Connect on …" timed out after 30 s, because the layer never finished building |
    | E3: `unchanged` as a raw digest compare | Caught: background rows 1 and 2 refused. Row 3 still refused |
    | E4: `unchanged` skips its index comparison | Caught: background rows 3 and 4 completed instead of refusing; rows 1 and 2 passed. So the index comparison, not the worktree re-hash, is what refuses row 3 as well |
- **Review round.** One review of the uncommitted build gave seven non-blocking findings, and this entry reflects all of them. Taken: test graphs get a no-op sweep (above); the marker case counts only this process's snapshots; this entry names decisions instead of plan-critique points and scratch files; the re-pin note is kept, with the lead's acceptance asked for; the marker write is best-effort; background row 4 isolates the index. Not taken: iterating `opendir` instead of `readdir`, for the Bun reason under the sweep's rules.
- **Held.** Q1 and Q6 stay open, and every flag stays off. With Connect off nothing is swept, and the only change outside Connect is the marker inside each process's own private folder. Because that write is best-effort, a failed write never refuses a VectorScope review or a task inspection with Connect off. Off Linux the sweep does nothing.
- **Flag-off exception (accepted; recorded 2026-11-16 at the phase review).** The marker write is a deliberate change on the flag-off path, not an oversight: `open()` writes the `owner` marker whatever `VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL_CONNECT` says, so VectorScope and task isolation write it with Connect off, and only the sweep is gated. It must stay flag-independent. A Connect-on process sweeps every matching snapshot in the temporary folder, including those a Connect-off process opened, and a snapshot without a marker is judged by age alone, so writing the marker only when a sweeper can run would let a Connect-on sweep remove a live Connect-off snapshot older than 24 hours. The write is best-effort and stays inside the process's own 0700 folder. The release checklist §1.5 asks the lead to accept it with CN-16's Playbooks rule.
- **Deferred verification.** The foreign-owner case runs only as root, because CI runs unprivileged and cannot chown. It passed here as root. The off-Linux case runs only on non-Linux runners. Known limits (1) and (2), and the failed-marker path, are reasoned from the code, not run.
- **Receipts.** Every `bun` command ran through `/home/user/.tools/vector-serial`, one test file at a time, with `--timeout 30000`. "Through the wrapper" means `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/as-ci.sh`: a private mount namespace, uid 1000, `CI=true`, `TMPDIR=/tmp/vector-v2/connect/home` and Git 2.43.0. Logs are in `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn07/` (the review round's in `fix1/`). Counts are for the final sources unless noted.

  - Engine, through the wrapper. The runs marked * used a probe build of `noSweep` that also appended each call's directory to a scratch file, to show the no-op reaches each graph; the probe was then removed (the fixture's sha256 checked) and `owner.test.ts` re-ran on the final fixture.

## 2026-11-15 — C6a: directory-review keys reconcile to bound or consumed (CN-08)

Source: `connect-finish` at `7ce17182` (CN-07). This package changes `packages/engine/src/connect/directory-request.ts`, `connect/owner.ts` (`directoryReview`), `connect/hub.ts` (one new method, the admission gate, and three helpers shared with `directoryRuns`) and the code literals in `server/routes/instance/httpapi/groups/connect.ts:15`. It also regenerates the ConnectApiError union in `packages/sdk/openapi.json`, `packages/sdk/js/src/gen/types.gen.ts` and `packages/sdk/js/src/v2/gen/types.gen.ts`. Its tests are in `test/connect/directory-request.test.ts`, `directory.test.ts`, `owner.test.ts`, `test/server/httpapi-connect.test.ts` and the ledger worker `test/fixture/directory-request-worker.ts`. It adds no route, flag, sweep or startup work. With Connect off, `directoryReview` still fails `disabled` before `reserve`, so nothing here runs. It was built uncommitted, revised after one review round (below), and the lane's commit gate records its commit.

- **What it closes.** Part 1 of C6 (review connect:correctness-security #6: "directory key stuck pending"), which is v2-complete-plan §4.1 C6 **B**'s "bind or release in onExit, reconcile orphans". It also settles the key bullet of the 2026-11-14 entry's "Remaining Tier B boundaries": a key is never blindly released or replaced, and it reconciles only on prepared-anchor and failed-tombstone evidence. C6 part 2 (fixed caps and no pruning, `MAX_KEYS`/`MAX_TOTAL_KEYS`) is CN-09, and app recovery is CN-10. As in CN-01, the plan and findings files are not edited.
- **Brief correction (read from code, then pinned).** A member-unavailable refusal does not leave the key pending. The hub checks member readiness (`ConnectMembers.gate`) in `admit`, which runs inside the job after the binding gate. So that key is bound, and a retry replays the refused run: 200 with state `refused`, where the first answer was 400 `invalid`. This is recorded, not changed (Held). At `7ce17182` these paths did leave a key pending:
  - the agent was unavailable;
  - anchor creation failed;
  - `fenceIsolation` failed;
  - `ConnectDirectory.issue` failed;
  - the hub refused before `detach` (decode, and the private-authority check);
  - the durable lease, job start or receipt write failed;
  - the bind failed;
  - the owner was interrupted before bind;
  - the engine exited in that window.
- **Confirm first: the admission hang (§2b of the plan).** An owner interrupted after `background.start` but before admission was settled left its job waiting on `admission` forever, with a live heartbeat. The directory.test.ts case "an owner interrupted while its queued job awaits the binding gate never leaves that job running" uses only APIs that exist at `7ce17182` (reserve, fence, issue, then `hub.run` with `bind: () => Effect.never`). Run against `7ce17182`'s `hub.ts`, it failed: the job was still `running` 5 s after the interrupt. It passes now and stays as the pin.
- **What changed.**
  - *Two new O_EXCL record kinds* under each scope folder, written under the existing global-then-scope EffectFlock pair with the bounded reader:
    - `prepared/<key>.json` holds the reservation's fields plus `anchorID`, `writer {pid, start}` and `preparedAt`. `ConnectDirectoryRequest.prepare` writes it right after the anchor session exists and before `hub.run`.
    - `refused/<key>.json` is a terminal tombstone. It holds the reservation's fields plus `reason` (`agent | anchor | prepare | fence | issue | run | bind | abandoned`), an optional `anchorID` and `refusedAt`.
    - Each must repeat its reservation exactly (version, key, nonce, both digests, createdAt). These read `unavailable` and write nothing: a mismatch, a partial file, a binding beside a tombstone, a prepared anchor that differs from the binding's or the tombstone's, a record or non-file entry for a key the scope index does not list.
    - The reservation record itself is byte-for-byte unchanged and has no writer field, so keys already on disk and their digests stay valid. `index()` now creates and lists `prepared/` and `refused/`, and requires both to be subsets of the index.
  - *The tombstone rule.* A tombstone is written only when no binding exists, checked under both locks. The key stays consumed for good: nothing is deleted or released, and `bind` and `prepare` answer `consumed` for a tombstoned key. So a review can never run twice for one key.
  - *Uninterruptible writes.* Every locked check-and-write body runs under `Effect.uninterruptible` inside the two locks (`both`, `directory-request.ts:331`), so an interrupted caller waits for its own O_EXCL write. Acquiring the locks stays interruptible.
    - An interrupt that lands during that body surfaces as soon as the body ends, so the call's exit reads interrupted even when the write finished. `bind` therefore takes an `onBound` callback and runs it inside the body, right after the binding write and the token's removal (`directory-request.ts:619`). The owner and the hub learn of a written binding only from it, never from the call's exit.
    - The owner's evidence (`inspect`, below) is the one read that runs under the process-wide lock. It is bounded by `INSPECT_MS`, 10 s (`directory-request.ts:25`, `:520`), and a read that runs out answers `unknown`: `unavailable`, with no write. It writes nothing, so it runs interruptibly inside the body. `Options.inspectMs` lowers the bound for tests.
  - *Liveness* (`claimed`, `directory-request.ts:282`). A pending or prepared key reconciles only when no live claim can exist:
    - It is never in `live`, the process-wide set of keys this process holds a token for. Like `dropped`, the set is keyed by ledger root plus key, so two data roots in one process never mix. A key joins `live` on the reserve that wins, and leaves it on bind, refuse or release.
    - With no prepared record, the key must be in `dropped` (this process let its token go without a binding or tombstone) or older than the grace.
    - With a prepared record, one of these must hold: the grace has passed; the writer is another pid and `kill(pid, 0)` answers ESRCH (the existing `BackgroundJobStore.exited`, with its 5 s probe cache); or the writer is this pid with another start time, which is an earlier run.
    - The same pid and start (this process) with no token stays pending unless the key is in `dropped`. The owner always binds, refuses or releases, so in practice that leaves only a lost binding file, which waits for the grace.
    - The start time is `Math.floor(performance.timeOrigin)`, as in read-only-git's owner marker. The grace is `GRACE_MS`, 10 minutes. `ConnectDirectoryRequest.Options` is a `Context.Reference` read on every call; tests set its grace to 0.
  - *Reconcile order* in `reserve`, all under both locks:
    1. the existing mismatch and unavailable checks;
    2. read the binding, tombstone and prepared records;
    3. a tombstone answers `consumed`;
    4. a binding answers `bound`;
    5. `claimed` answers `pending`, without inspecting;
    6. with no prepared record, write an `abandoned` tombstone and answer `consumed`;
    7. with no `inspect`, answer `unavailable` with no write, since only the owner can tell whether a job was released;
    8. `inspect` the prepared anchor. `released` writes the binding `{…reservation, anchorID, runID}` O_EXCL and answers `bound`. `unreleased` writes an `abandoned` tombstone with the anchorID and answers `consumed`; its `stop` runs only after the locks are released. `unknown` answers `unavailable` with no write.
  - *Release evidence* (`ConnectHub.settleDirectory`, `hub.ts:564`), in three kinds:
    1. This process's ledger entry for that anchor and proof decides first. `admitted` (the binding gate released it) means `released`. Otherwise the answer is `unreleased`, with a `stop` that cancels the job and is forked into the hub's scope.
    2. No durable job row for the key means `unreleased`. The row is written before the binding gate can run.
    3. The answer is `unknown` for any of these: more than one row; a row failing `directoryJob`; a running row whose owner is not dead (`BackgroundJobStore.liveness`); an anchor failing `directoryAnchor`; a receipt that is missing (pruned, unreadable, or its directory identity changed) or names another anchor.
    4. A receipt with `time.started` means `released`. A running row whose owner is dead never resumes, so it is `unreleased`. So is a settled row whose seal matches its receipt. Anything else is `unknown`.
    - `directoryAnchor`, `directoryJob` and `sealed` (`hub.ts:470`, `:1904`, `:1916`) are the checks `directoryRuns` already made, moved into helpers so the two share them.
  - *Owner* (`owner.ts:407-528`).
    - `reserve` is the acquire of `Effect.acquireUseRelease`, so an interrupt landing just after it cannot skip settlement.
    - `reached` records the step, the anchor and the bind state. The bind state becomes `bound` only in the ledger's `onBound` callback (`owner.ts:485-498`), which runs inside the uninterruptible write, so a bind whose write finished still counts when an interrupt surfaces right after it. The first build set it in `onExit`, which an interrupt landing during the write turned into a failure (review round, below).
    - The release does nothing for a bound attempt or a successful bind. An interrupt-only exit calls `release(token)`: nothing is written, and the token is dropped so the next retry reconciles at once. Any other exit calls `refuse(token, {reason: bind called ? "bind" : step, anchorID})`, and a failure there is logged.
    - When the ledger refused the bind as `consumed` (another retry reconciled first), the owner answers `consumed`. The hub's own answer would have been `invalid`, "could not preserve its durable job and receipt".
    - The first answer of a refused start keeps its own code and message (`forbidden`, or `invalid` with the hub's summary). The retry answers `consumed`.
  - *Hub admission gate* (`hub.ts:1442-1583`). This fix is unconditional, because a job queued in memory before its durable row exists needs it whatever the confirm-first test showed. `detach` settles `admission` in `Effect.ensuring`, keyed on whether the binding gate bound the run (`gate.bound`). The ledger sets it through the `bound` callback that `Call.directory.bind` now passes down (`hub.ts:1539-1541`):
    - If bound, the job is released, and a retry replays it, which matches a response lost after bind.
    - The normal path releases the job when the gate succeeded or the ledger reported the binding (`hub.ts:1545`). So a binding whose call failed after the write (a lock-release defect) still runs its job, because the key now replays it.
    - If not, it is stopped.
    - `Entry.admitted` records the answer for `settleDirectory`. A second `Deferred.succeed` is a no-op, so the normal path is unchanged.
  - *Public codes.* `ConnectOwner.Error` and `ConnectApiError` gain `pending` and `consumed`, with status 400 and the handler unchanged. The ledger's `mismatch` and `unavailable` still map to `invalid` (`requestError`, `owner.ts:75`).
    - `pending`: "This review start is not confirmed yet. Retry it shortly; a retry never starts a second review."
    - `consumed`: "This review start did not complete and no review ran. Start a new review."
  - *Generators.* `./packages/sdk/js/script/build.ts` at `7ce17182`, before any edit, gave no drift. After the edits, `bun run generate` and `bun run check:generated` in packages/client gave no diff, because the client has no connect routes. The SDK script then changed only the ConnectApiError code union in the three SDK files above. Both used fresh XDG roots under `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn08/xdg`.
- **Plan critique decisions.** Every point was taken, with these exceptions:
  - *"A sealed row is unknown."* This is narrowed, not taken whole. A settled row whose seal matches a terminal receipt without `time.started` is a run that never executed, so `unreleased` is true there, and a lost binding then reads `consumed`. A sealed row whose receipt is gone (pruned) stays `unknown`. The directory.test.ts evidence case pins that.
  - *The lost-response replay case lives in directory.test.ts, not owner.test.ts.* The ledger can be wrapped there, so the case interrupts the owner inside the ledger's binding write (review round, below). httpapi-connect.test.ts "a lost actual directory POST response and case-variant explicit retry recover one run without another ask" covers a response lost after the run returned, end to end.
  - *The planned directory.test.ts "lost binding" case* is merged into the evidence case.
  - *The mutation matrix* is limited to the safety-critical rows.
- **Tests added.**
  - `directory-request.test.ts`, 6 new cases:
    - another live process's young prepared claim stays pending without inspection; with a grace of 0 its unreleased anchor is tombstoned (`abandoned`, with the anchorID), and that process's held `bind` answers `consumed` with no binding file;
    - a writer that exited: no job gives `consumed` once, then without inspecting again; a released job gives `bound` with the worker's anchor and the evidence run id;
    - an earlier run of this pid gives `consumed`. A writerless reservation is `pending`, then `consumed` with a grace of 0 and no anchorID. `unknown` evidence gives `unavailable` and writes nothing;
    - this process's live claim stays `pending` with a grace of 0. After `refuse` the key is `consumed` and the old token's `prepare` and `bind` answer `unavailable`. A released claim with no prepared record gives `consumed` at once;
    - an interrupted `bind`, at four delays, settles as `bound` or `consumed`, never `unavailable`;
    - seven corrupt or contradictory layouts stay `unavailable`, with no write and no inspection;
    - in the earlier-run case, an `inspect` that never answers (`Effect.never`, bound 50 ms) gives `unavailable` with no write, and the next `reserve` takes the lock at once.
    - The existing cases pass unchanged, including 6-process concurrency (5 pending), the frozen input and binding (pending after the binding is deleted), the tampered index, the failed O_EXCL binding, the missing pointer and index, and the scope cap.
  - `directory.test.ts`, 4 new cases:
    - the admission-hang pin (above);
    - an interrupted queued start: the retry gives `consumed`, the tombstone carries the anchor, the job leaves `running`, and there is no model call, ask or second anchor;
    - an owner interrupted inside the ledger's binding write (`directory.test.ts:642`): a test wrapper over the real ledger keeps `bind` in an uninterruptible region after the write, and the owner fiber is interrupted there. No tombstone is written, the bound job reaches its consent question, an uppercase retry replays the same run id while it is still going, and after approval the run completes with one model call and one job row. This case failed on the first build's `onExit` sources ("No directory consent was published"): the job was cancelled while its key stayed bound;
    - a lost binding: within the grace it is `pending`. With a grace of 0 it rebinds to the in-memory released job, and the rewritten binding is byte-identical. After a reload it rebinds to the durable started receipt and replays the done run. With the receipt also gone it gives `invalid`, with no binding or tombstone written.
  - `owner.test.ts`, 2 new cases. The real audience is used, with a test switch to fail or skip `fenceIsolation`. A fence failure, then a retry, gives `forbidden` then `consumed`. A hub refusal before the gate, then a retry, gives `invalid` (authority summary) then `consumed`. Each leaves one INTERNAL anchor, no job row, no ask and no LLM input.
  - `httpapi-connect.test.ts`, 1 new case. A key a real worker process holds answers 400 `{code: "pending"}` on the wire. A key it tombstoned answers 400 `{code: "consumed", message}`, also for an uppercase retry. The public spec the SDK is generated from (`OpenApi.fromApi(PublicApi)`) lists both codes in ConnectApiError's enum; the typed constant also checks them against the generated SDK type. No internal session, ask, run or LLM input.
  - The ledger worker can also `prepare`, take a `writer`, `refuse`, and `hold` its token for one `bind <runID>` line on stdin.
- **Review round (one).** One blocking finding and eight non-blocking ones (some repeated), all checked against the code.
  - *Blocking: the bind state came from `onExit`, outside the ledger's uninterruptible write.* Confirmed. An interrupt that landed during the write left `gate.bound` false, so the job cancelled itself while its key stayed bound, and a retry replayed a cancelled run. Fixed with the reviewer's option (b): the ledger's `onBound` callback, inside the body, sets both the owner's `reached.bind` and the hub's `gate.bound`. Option (a), making the hub's whole `bind` call uninterruptible, was not taken: it would also make the lock wait uninterruptible, and a binder that never answers (the admission-hang pin's `bind: () => Effect.never`) would then never let an interrupted owner go. The new directory.test.ts case pins the fix. The owner's own `bound` state is belt-and-braces: once the binding is written the token is gone, so `refuse` and `release` are no-ops anyway.
  - *The `unrecorded` JSDoc was attached to `directoryJob`.* Fixed: `directoryJob` and `sealed` now sit above that block (`hub.ts:1903-1926`).
  - *A missing receipt stays `unknown`.* Not taken; Known limits says why.
  - *No owner-level interrupted-after-bind replay case.* Added in directory.test.ts, which can wrap the ledger. Plan critique decisions says where the cases live.
  - *The httpapi code check could never fail.* Replaced with a check of the public spec's enum.
  - *The global lock is held across `settleDirectory`'s reads.* Bounded by `INSPECT_MS`, and recorded under Known limits. Removing the explicit `Effect.interruptible` around `inspect` is not caught by the new case, because Effect's `timeoutOrElse` interrupts its loser either way. It stays as a statement of intent: the evidence writes nothing.
  - *A writerless reservation waits out the grace.* The `claims/` record was not added; Known limits says why.
- **Known limits.**
  - A data folder shared across machines or pid namespaces can read a remote live writer's pid as gone. The result is a refused start (`consumed`) whose late bind is refused, never a second run.
  - Clock skew keeps a key pending longer.
  - A reservation with no prepared record reconciles only by age, or through this process's `dropped` set. It carries no writer identity, so a young one may be held by a live process the ledger cannot see; that is a possible live claim, and the brief's "Done when" holds. The review round's optional `claims/<key>.json` writer record was not added. With it, a dead reserver would reconcile at once, but the brief pins "no replacement after process exit" and the httpapi `pending` wire case on exactly this window, so the window is accepted for up to the 10-minute grace.
  - "Retry it shortly" can last up to the 10-minute grace while a foreign writer's pid answers.
  - The owner's acquire is uninterruptible. An aborted request therefore waits for the ledger locks (bounded by EffectFlock's timeout) and the reconcile reads. Those reads (`settleDirectory`: the session, audience, job store and receipt) run while the process-wide `directory-request:global` lock is held, so a slow database stalls every directory start until `INSPECT_MS` (10 s) answers `unavailable` for that key.
  - An engine crash after the durable job row is written but before its queued receipt (`jobStore.started`, then `complete`) leaves that key `unavailable` (public `invalid`) for good, never `pending`. The review round suggested reading "row present, no receipt" as `unreleased`, since `complete` comes before `bind`. That was not taken. A receipt that is missing cannot be told from one pruned after a done run (`ConnectDirectory.prune` removes done receipts, including one whose row went unsealed in a crash), and only a binding proves a release. So with a lost binding the new rule could answer "no review ran" for a review that ran. directory.test.ts's lost-binding case pins `invalid` for that layout.
  - An older engine running at the same time on the same data folder knows nothing of tombstones. Its bind could write a binding beside one, which reads `unavailable`, never a second run.
- **Held.**
  - CN-09: caps and retention.
  - CN-10: app recovery.
  - Q6: connect's routes and file ledger stay.
  - The bound-refused replay inconsistency (first answer 400 `invalid`, retry 200 `refused`).
  - The bound-but-unreplayable `invalid` answer.
  - Every flag stays off, and Q1 stays open.
- **Receipts.** Every `bun` command ran through `/home/user/.tools/vector-serial`, one test file at a time from packages/engine, with `--timeout 30000`. "Wrapper" means `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/as-ci.sh` (CN-07's: uid 1000, `CI=true`). Logs are in `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn08/` (the review round's in `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn08-fix/`).
  - `test/connect/directory-request.test.ts`: 14 pass, 0 fail, 109 expect() calls, both as root and through the wrapper. After the review round: 14 pass, 0 fail, 110 expect() calls, both ways.
  - `test/connect/directory.test.ts`: 22 pass, 0 fail, 131 expect() calls, through the wrapper; 22 pass as root. After the review round: 23 pass, 0 fail, 138 expect() calls, both ways.
  - `test/connect/owner.test.ts`: 6 pass, 0 fail, 36 expect() calls, through the wrapper; 6 pass as root. The same after the review round.
  - `test/server/httpapi-connect.test.ts`: 11 pass, 0 fail, 119 expect() calls, through the wrapper; 11 pass as root. The same after the review round.
  - `test/connect/hub.test.ts`: 48 pass as root (48 pass, 378 expect() calls, after the review round; not rerun through the wrapper). Through the wrapper it gives 47 pass and 1 fail ("missing snapshot capability lists configuration without probing MCP"). That case fails the same way under the wrapper with `7ce17182`'s `directory-request.ts`, `hub.ts` and `owner.ts` restored, so it is outside this package and recorded, not fixed.
  - `test/connect/directory-live.test.ts`: 7 pass, 0 fail, 58 expect() calls, through the wrapper, also after the review round. As root all 7 fail with "This review's Git reads cannot be inspected safely", with this package's sources and with `7ce17182`'s alike. That is the root sandbox; CN-07 also ran it through the wrapper.
  - The confirm-first pin failed on `7ce17182`'s `hub.ts` and passes now.
  - Mutations, each restored with `sha256sum -c`. Every row was caught:

    | Mutation | Caught by |
    | --- | --- |

The round changed no route, schema or public type (`onBound` and the hub's `bound` are internal), so nothing was regenerated.

## 2026-11-15 — C6b: directory-request ledger retention and pruning (CN-09)

Source: `connect-finish` at `409f8de3` (CN-08). This package changes `packages/engine/src/connect/directory-request.ts` and `packages/engine/src/connect/directory.ts` (a bounded receipt reader and `ConnectDirectory.settled`), with tests in `packages/engine/test/connect/directory-request.test.ts`. It changes no route, public schema, SDK type, flag, startup or background work, so nothing was regenerated. With Connect off, `ConnectOwner.directoryReview` still fails `disabled` before `reserve` (`owner.ts:110`, `:384`), so no prune or sweep ever runs. It deletes only this ledger's own files under `storage/connect-directory-requests`, never a receipt, job row or session. It was built uncommitted, and the lane's commit gate records its commit.

- **What it closes.** The second half of review connect:correctness-security #6, "the ledger is never pruned: after 4,096 reviews in one directory (or 65,536 overall) every new directory review is refused for good", which is v2-complete-plan §4.1 C6 **B**'s "prune". It also closes a permanent refusal that predates CN-09: a crash between an admission's pointer write and its global index write (or between the slot and the scope index) left every directory, or that directory, `unavailable` for good. With CN-08's reconcile, the engine side of C6 is fixed for keys whose review ends while the engine runs; the exceptions are under Known limits, and CN-10's app recovery remains. As in CN-01 and CN-08, the plan and review-findings files are not edited.
- **Retention contract.**
  - A settled key replays for `RETENTION_MS`, 30 days, measured from its tombstone (`refusedAt`) or from the end of the run it bound (the receipt's `time.ended`). A bound key whose receipt is gone, which only `ConnectDirectory.prune` causes and only after the run was done, ages from its reservation.
  - When the key's directory is at its cap, or the whole ledger is, an admission may prune a settled key once it is past `REPLAY_MS`, 24 hours, oldest first. The brief says eligible means older than RETENTION, and separately that nothing younger than the replay window is pruned and that a full directory prunes before refusing. The lane reads that as: retention is the floor without pressure, and the replay window is the floor under cap pressure. So key memory is bounded by RETENTION, and by REPLAY_MS while a directory or the ledger is full.
  - Nothing pending, prepared, live (a token this process holds), or younger than the floor is ever pruned, and neither is a binding whose run is still open or whose receipt cannot be read.
  - After that, a retried key is a new request and can start a second review. The app keeps its key in sessionStorage (`app/src/features/connect/directory-review.tsx:52`) and removes it only on `confirm()` (`directory-review-reservation.ts:58-63`), so a long-lived desktop window holding an unconfirmed key can retry across these windows. So can public HTTP and SDK clients, which choose and keep their own keys. CN-10's recovery should treat a key older than the replay window as possibly new.
- **What changed.**
  - *Limits are layer options* (`Limits`, `LIMITS`, `directory-request.ts:38`; `nodeWith` at `:965` for tests; `node` keeps the defaults). They are `maxKeys` (4,096), `maxTotalKeys` (65,536), `retentionMs` (30 days), `replayMs` (24 hours) and `pruneLimit` (64). Bad values die when the layer builds, with a plain range check (`valid`): the caps may only be smaller than the constants, `maxKeys ≤ maxTotalKeys`, `replayMs ≤ retentionMs`, and `pruneLimit` is 1 to 64. The index schemas and byte caps stay bound by the constants, so the same disk decodes under any configuration. `Options` (grace, writer, inspect bound) stays a per-call Reference, unchanged.
  - *Locators.* A new reservation carries `folder` (the two hashes its scope folder is named by, `:56`), as its last field so the decoded record's key order matches the literal. Index entries carry the reservation's `createdAt`. Both are optional, so keys written before CN-09 still decode. The sizes stay under the byte caps: about 150 bytes an entry, 0.6 MB for a full scope index and 9.8 MB for a full global index.
  - *The global index* (`:108`) gains three optional fields:
    - `pruning`, the prune journal: at most `PRUNE_LIMIT` keys, already gone from `entries`, whose scope entry and files may still exist.
    - `admitting`, the admission intent: one key in `entries` whose pointer, slot or scope entry may not be written yet.
    - `cursor`, where the last scan stopped. A scope index also gains its own `cursor`.
  - *Consistency checks explain only the journal and the intent* (`globalIndex` `:442`, `index` `:474`).
    - Every pointer must be indexed or journaled, and every entry must have its pointer, except the intent's own key.
    - In a scope, every entry must have its slot, and a slot or record that no entry names must be one the journal or intent names for that folder. With neither open, this is CN-08's equality rule.
    - `index` has three modes. `create` starts an empty scope, `open` is an existing one, and `inspect` is used for other directories while pruning: it creates no folders and writes nothing.
    - Unnamed files that neither explains still read `unavailable` with no write, so CN-08's tamper cases pass unchanged.
  - *Admission writes in a crash-safe order* (`:841-876`):
    1. the global index with the new entry and `admitting`;
    2. the pointer, O_EXCL;
    3. the slot, O_EXCL;
    4. the scope index;
    5. the global index without `admitting`.
    A reservation is returned only after step 5.
  - *The sweep* (`finish`, `:577`) runs at the start of every `reserve`, under both locks, before any other read.
    - An intent rolls forward when its scope index names the key with the entry's digest. Otherwise it rolls back: the slot and pointer are removed, then the entry. It refuses (unavailable) if a binding, prepared or tombstone file exists for that key. That cannot happen without tampering, because no caller ever held the key. Such an intent, like one whose scope index cannot be read or names the key with another digest, fails every `reserve` in every directory, replays included, until the ledger is repaired by hand. That is by design: only tampering or a corrupt disk leaves one. `prepare`, `bind` and `refuse` never sweep, so live claims still finish.
    - Journaled keys are finished one folder at a time (`forget`, `:555`): the scope index is rewritten without them, then their binding, prepared, tombstone and slot files are removed, then their pointers. Each step can be repeated.
    - A folder that cannot be finished (its index does not decode or cannot be rewritten, or a journaled key is live) stays journaled, and the sweep carries on. Its keys are never journaled twice: both scans skip every journaled key, including one its folder's index still names with all its files (`going`, `:611`). One corrupt or unwritable directory never blocks another, within the journal bound under Known limits.
    - Then the global index is rewritten with what is left. `prepare`, `bind` and `refuse` never sweep; `held()` only tolerates the journal and the intent.
  - *Pruning* (`prune`, `:602`) runs only on the new-key admission path, under both locks, before the cap check. Replays and the `pending`, `consumed` and `unavailable` answers never prune, because only an admission grows the ledger.
    - Up to three picks, sharing at most `min(pruneLimit, PRUNE_LIMIT − journal length)` keys:
      1. a full directory gives up its own keys past the replay window;
      2. a full ledger takes keys past the replay window from any directory;
      3. the remaining room goes to keys past their retention, in any directory.
    - Each scan (`walk`, `:649`) visits its index's entries oldest first by `(createdAt, key)`, starting just after its cursor and wrapping once. Entries created after `now − floor` are skipped with no I/O. Each scan inspects at most `pruneLimit` entries, and its cursor moves to the last one inspected, so entries that can never be pruned are passed over instead of starving the keys behind them.
    - An entry is a candidate only when all of these hold: its key is not live and not already journaled; its pointer reads and matches the entry's digest; its folder is known (from the pointer, or the current scope for a key written before CN-09); that folder's index passes the read-only check and names the key with the same digest; the slot matches; and the records are consistent. Its settled time is the tombstone's `refusedAt`, a done receipt's `ended`, or for `gone` the reservation's `createdAt`, each at least the reservation's `createdAt`. Anything else, including any read error, skips the key.
    - Applying is journal-first: the global index without the chosen keys and with them appended to `pruning`, then `finish`.
    - A refused admission writes only the scan cursors (when they moved), so the next admission inspects other entries. The refusal is now temporary, and its message is unchanged.
  - *Per-call bounds* (`FOLDER_LIMIT` 8 and `RECEIPT_BUDGET` 8 MiB, `:33`, `:35`). One admission inspects at most 2 × 64 = 128 entries, and at most 8 other directories. It stops reading receipts after 8 MiB. Its reads under the global lock are at most about 20 MiB:
    - up to five records of at most 4 KiB per entry, about 2.5 MiB;
    - receipts, at most 9 MiB with the last one read;
    - 8 foreign scope indexes of at most 1 MiB, each with 4 bounded readdirs.
    - On top of that come the global index writes (at most 16 MiB each). A plain admission now writes the global index twice (the intent and the final write; once before CN-09). A pruning admission writes it twice more (the journal and the sweep's clear).
  - *Receipt evidence* (`ConnectDirectory.settled`, `directory.ts:166`). It finds the receipt by the two folder hashes and `runID`, through the bounded reader that `load` now shares (`bounded`, `:107`; `load` behaves as before). It answers:
    - `done` (with `ended`) for a receipt whose run id, request id, anchor, `hash(projectID)` and `hash(canonical)` all match and whose run is done;
    - `open` for such a receipt whose run is not done;
    - `gone` only when the receipt file is missing but its folder exists;
    - `unknown` otherwise, which includes a missing folder (another data root, or a tampered tree).
    It skips `current()`, so a deleted or replaced directory can still be settled. It reports the bytes it read, for the receipt budget.
  - The comment on `both` (`:430-433`) records why cross-scope writes under the global lock are safe: every holder of a scope lock takes the global lock first, and only this file takes these keys.
- **Brief corrections and decisions.**
  - *D1, the journal replaces "a later prune sweeps record files that no index names".* A blind sweep would break CN-08's pinned tamper contract (unindexed prepared and tombstone records stay unavailable with no write, `directory-request.test.ts:513`). It would also go against the 2026-11-14 boundary that keys are never blindly released. The admission intent covers the other job the blind sweep would have done, recovering a crashed admission, with evidence.
  - *D2, a missing receipt counts as terminal only when its folder exists.* The hub writes the receipt before it binds the key (`hub.ts:1538-1539`), and only `ConnectDirectory.prune` removes receipts, done ones only (`directory.ts:215`). Without the folder there is no evidence the receipt ever existed under this data root.
  - *The two floors, and pruning on admission only,* as described above.
  - *Plan critique, taken.*
    - The scan cursors: the blocker. Without them, 256 never-prunable entries sorted ahead would stall pruning for good.
    - The admission intent: the critique's third point, so this entry may say C6 is fixed in the engine, within the Known limits.
    - Stuck journal items are kept and appended to, never replaced, and they block no other directory (within the journal bound under Known limits).
    - Read-only `inspect` mode for other directories.
    - The evaluation bound is lowered to `pruneLimit` per scan, with a folder budget and a receipt-byte budget; the worst case is the number above.
    - The `gone` folder check (D2).
    - The retention wording above.
    - A test for `held()`'s journal tolerance.
    - Journal and index bounds use the constants, and the limits check is a synchronous range check rather than a Schema decode.
  - *Plan critique, the open-binding point.* The second option was taken: the rule is kept, and the real rate is stated under Known limits. The owner-evidence callback was not built here, because it widens `ConnectOwner` and `ConnectHub` and needs its own tests. It is the follow-up below.
  - *No owner.ts or hub.ts change.* `reserve`'s interface and error codes are unchanged.
- **Known limits.**
  - *Open bindings are never pruned.* A binding whose receipt never reached done has nothing to show that its run ended. Every directory review still running when the app quits or the engine restarts leaves one of these, because runs in progress do not persist (spec §"Restarts", line 170) and only a live writer rewrites a receipt (`owner.ts` through `ConnectDirectory.write`). So the rate is one such binding per review interrupted by a quit or restart, not only one per crash. Each counts against its directory's 4,096 for good. A directory that collects 4,096 of them still refuses for good, so "unlimited reviews without permanent refusal" holds for reviews that end while the engine runs.
    - *Follow-up (unscheduled; it belongs with the 2026-11-14 boundary "orphan reconciliation and retention need prepared-anchor/failed-tombstone evidence").* Add an owner-supplied callback like `inspect` that answers terminal when the durable job row is settled, or is running with a dead owner (`BackgroundJobStore.liveness`), and treat such a binding as settled at its reservation's age. The ledger itself never guesses.
  - *Pending and prepared keys are never pruned.* Such a key is left when its owner never settled it (a crash before `refuse`), and it reconciles only when the same key is retried (CN-08). The app's keys live in one window's sessionStorage, so after that window closes no retry comes, and the key counts against the caps for good. The same follow-up could reconcile them through `inspect`.
  - *The journal holds at most 64 marks* (`PRUNE_LIMIT`, its schema bound), and stuck marks count against it. A mark is stuck while its folder cannot be finished, and every `reserve` retries it. While 64 are stuck, no admission anywhere prunes, so a full directory or a full ledger refuses again until a stuck folder can be finished; admissions under the caps still work. The bound is not widened, because the pointer folder's file bound (`MAX_TOTAL_KEYS + PRUNE_LIMIT`, `:444`) rests on it.
  - A partial O_EXCL tombstone, binding or prepared write (a crash mid-write) leaves that key `unavailable`, and it is never pruned, because `records()` fails and the candidate is skipped.
  - Keys written before CN-09 in another directory have no `folder`, so the global scans skip them. They are pruned through admissions in their own directory.
  - A directory that settles more than `maxKeys` keys inside 24 hours refuses new keys until they pass the replay window. The refusal is temporary, and its message is unchanged.
  - Oldest-first is per scan from its cursor: after a wrap, a scan restarts at the oldest entry.
  - A clock jump forward prunes earlier, though never under the jumped clock's floors. A jump back delays pruning.
  - An older engine on the same data folder reads `unavailable` while a journal or intent is open. Its admissions drop the cursors and write reservations without `folder`, which take the pre-CN-09 path. It never prunes.
- **Tests added** (`directory-request.test.ts`, 12 new cases; the 14 existing ones pass unchanged, including the 4,096-key cap case, which now takes the pre-CN-09 entry path). The time-based cases run under `TestClock`. Receipts are written with the real `ConnectDirectory.issue` and `write`, under the process data root, which `afterEach` cleans.
  - age pruning: a tombstone, a done binding, a binding with no receipt (`gone`), a running binding, a binding whose receipt folder is missing (`unknown`), and a key tombstoned at 29 days. At 29 days every key replays, and every file remains. At 30 days and 1 ms, the first tombstone and the `gone` binding are pruned from every place, while the done binding (ended at day 1), the running binding, the `unknown` binding and the late tombstone stay. At 31 days the done binding goes too. The pruned keys then start new requests with new nonces;
  - cap pressure: a full directory refuses a new key while its oldest tombstone is 2 hours old, and the tombstone still replays. At 24.5 hours the same key is admitted and only that tombstone is pruned. The next new key is refused, because the next oldest is 23.5 hours old;
  - pending, prepared, live and young keys are never pruned in a full directory and a full ledger. The record files are byte-identical, the index entries unchanged, the young tombstone still replays, and the live token still binds;
  - a scan cursor: four stale reservations, twice one scan's budget, sorted ahead of a tombstone. The tombstone is pruned by the third admission;
  - the global index prunes another directory's keys, one under ledger pressure, then two by age (the prune limit), leaving a third eligible key for the next admission, which a full directory then prunes;
  - one admission prunes at most the prune limit;
  - a prune interrupted after its journal write, after the scope index rewrite, and between file removals finishes on the next start. Contrast: a key missing from the global index with no journal entry, and an indexed key whose slot is missing, both stay `unavailable`, and nothing is removed;
  - an admission interrupted after its intent (no pointer), or after a partial pointer and slot, rolls back and the key starts fresh. One that wrote its scope index rolls forward, and the key then reconciles to `consumed` like any stale one;
  - an open journal that already left its scope index keeps a live claim's `prepare` and `bind` working, unswept, until the next `reserve` finishes it;
  - a corrupt journaled directory stays journaled while another directory admits and prunes, twice; its own key stays `unavailable`;
  - review round: a journaled key that its own full directory cannot finish (the folder is read-only, so the sweep reads its index but cannot rewrite it) is never journaled twice. Two admissions there answer `unavailable`, the journal names the key once and stays distinct, the key's pointer, slot, tombstone and scope entry remain, and another directory still admits. Once the folder is writable, the next start finishes every journaled key. Permissions do not hold for root or on Windows, so the case returns early there, as `hub.test.ts`'s unlistable-folder case does; the wrapper and the unprivileged Linux and macOS CI runners exercise it;
  - out-of-range limits die when the layer builds.
- **Review round.** Seven findings, two blocking; the text above already reads as fixed.
  - *Blocking, fixed (both findings name the same path).* A journaled key whose own folder's `forget` failed before its index rewrite (an unwritable folder, a full disk, or a Windows rename refused by a scanner) is still named by that folder's index with its pointer, slot and tombstone. When that directory was at its cap, the scope scan picked the key again, and `prune` appended it to the journal a second time. `atomic` does not decode what it writes, so the duplicate reached `index.json`, and from then on `GlobalIndex`'s `distinct` check made every `reserve`, `prepare`, `bind` and `refuse` in every directory `unavailable` for good. Now `prune` builds `going` from the journal (`:611`), and both scans skip those keys next to the `chosen` check, so they spend no inspection budget and do not move a cursor. The journal append also filters `going` as a backstop. The global scan could not hit this, because a journaled key is never in `entries`. Any other key a scope index names without the global index has no pointer (`globalIndex` refuses an unindexed pointer), so `evaluate` already skips it.
  - *Non-blocking, recorded rather than changed: a contradictory admission intent.* `finish` lets a failed `settleAdmission` fail the call, so such an intent blocks every `reserve`, replays included. It is now stated above as by design, with the reason. The other option the finding offered, keeping the intent and refusing only new admissions, would still refuse every new admission in every directory, because an admission's own intent write would replace the stuck one. Its only gain is replays during a state that needs tampering or a corrupt disk, so the simpler rule is kept.
  - *Non-blocking, recorded: the 64-mark journal bound.* Added under Known limits. Keeping stuck marks out of the room budget would need either a journal longer than its schema bound, which the finding rules out, or dropping stuck marks from the journal, which would leave their pointers unexplained and make `globalIndex` `unavailable` in every directory.
  - *Non-blocking, fixed.* The spec's status line now names the open-binding and pending-key exceptions. The handoff's CN-09 sentence now says that a receipt `ConnectDirectory.prune` already removed counts as done. In `finish`, `stuck` is built with `Effect.forEach` and `.flat()`, and `forget` clears `dropped` with `forEach`. `walk` keeps its `spent` and `scan` holders, because the scan breaks early on three budgets, and the loop reads more plainly than a fold there.
- **Held.** Q6 (connect's routes and file ledger stay). CN-10 (app recovery). The open-binding and orphan evidence follow-up above. Every flag stays off, and Q1 stays open.
- **Receipts.** Every `bun` command ran through `/home/user/.tools/vector-serial`, one test file at a time from packages/engine, with `--timeout 30000`. "Wrapper" means `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/as-ci.sh` (uid 1000, `CI=true`). Logs are in `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn09/` (the review round's in `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn09-fix/`).
  - `test/connect/directory-request.test.ts`: before any test edit, the 14 existing cases passed on the new sources (110 expect() calls). Final: 25 pass, 0 fail, 183 expect() calls, both as root and through the wrapper.
  - `test/connect/directory.test.ts`: 23 pass, 0 fail, 138 expect() calls, both ways.
  - `test/connect/owner.test.ts`: 6 pass, 0 fail, both ways.
  - `test/server/httpapi-connect.test.ts`: 11 pass, 0 fail, both ways.
  - `test/connect/hub.test.ts`: 48 pass as root. Through the wrapper: 47 pass and 1 fail, the same known wrapper failure CN-06 and CN-08 recorded ("missing snapshot capability lists configuration without probing MCP").
  - `test/connect/directory-live.test.ts`: 7 pass, 0 fail, through the wrapper. It fails as root in this sandbox, as CN-08 recorded.
  - Mutations, each run against the full test file and restored with `sha256sum -c`:
