## 2026-11-15 — CI repair after CN-08: the review pipeline pin follows hub.ts

Source: `connect-finish` at `6f3a78ad`. This repair changes one constant in `packages/core/src/review/model-evidence.ts` and this log. It changes no product code path, route, schema, generated file, flag or workflow.

- **What failed.** Vector Codium run `43723800137` (head `409f8de3`, CN-08), Codium tests shard 1/4 (job `132841239654`), failed `packages/core/test/review/model-evidence.test.ts` beside the inherited `packages/web/test/docs-config-reference.test.ts` ("Codium shard 1/4: 165/167 test files passed"). The file ran before the 5,000-line tail that the job-log API returns, so its error line was read from a local run instead.
- **Cause.** "scope > the pipeline's sources are pinned next to REVIEW_PIPELINE_REVISION" pins a digest over `REVIEW_PIPELINE_FILES`, which includes `packages/engine/src/connect/hub.ts`. CN-08 changed hub.ts (`settleDirectory`, the `directoryAnchor`, `directoryJob` and `sealed` helpers, and the admission gate in `detach`) and left the digest at CN-07's `b9b7e47e95691d6e`. The CN-04 repair entry above asked any package that edits a pinned source to update the digest in the same commit; CN-08 did not. Of the files changed since `7ce17182`, hub.ts is the only pinned one (CN-09 changed only directory-request.ts and directory.ts). Reproduced at `6f3a78ad`: 29 pass, 1 fail, printing `935a21d2165f9d29`.
- **Fix.** `REVIEW_PIPELINE_SOURCES` is now `935a21d2165f9d29`. `REVIEW_PIPELINE_REVISION` stays 4. The hub.ts changes decide whether a directory-review request key is bound, replayed, tombstoned or stopped, and when a queued job is released. They do not change what a review reads, finds or posts, and the extracted helpers keep the same checks. This is the same ownership deviation as CN-06 and CN-07: spec §7.1 gives core `src/review/**` to VectorScope, so the VectorScope owner should acknowledge it and may still decide to bump the revision. It needs the lead's acceptance.
- **Not this branch's.** Shard 1's `docs-config-reference.test.ts` ("company computer policy samples decode and state their minimum engine version", `toContain("1.99.112")` at :46) and shard 3/4's `cloud-agents-ledger.test.ts` and `model-plan-gateway-hooks.test.ts` (`ReferenceError: Cannot access 'METER_EPOCH_PATTERN' before initialization` at `api/_lib/cloud-agents-run-token.ts:57`) are inherited from release-finish `cf2373c4`, as in every CI check since CN-04. Their fixes are on their owning lanes.
- **Receipts.** `test/review/model-evidence.test.ts` from packages/core: 30 pass, 0 fail, 393 expect() calls. `bun typecheck` passed in packages/core. The full-tree typecheck is run before the push. *CI:* none is claimed; the dispatched Vector Codium run's shard 1 is the authority.

## 2026-11-15 — C6c: the app recovers from a consumed directory review key (CN-10)

Source: `connect-finish` at `be1eef43`. This package changes four app files in `packages/app/src/features/connect/`: `client.ts`, `directory-review-reservation.ts`, `directory-review-controller.ts` and `directory-review.tsx`. Its tests are in `client.test.ts`, `directory-review-controller.test.ts` and `directory-review-reservation.test.ts`, plus one new case in `packages/app/e2e/regression/directory-review.spec.ts`. It changes no route, public schema, SDK or generated file, flag, workflow or engine file, and nothing was regenerated: CN-08 already added `pending` and `consumed` to `ConnectApiError` and to the regenerated SDK type (`packages/sdk/js/src/v2/gen/types.gen.ts:4705-4707`). With Connect off, `DirectoryReview` mounts no controller, because it needs `capabilities().connections`, which is `flags.experimentalConnect` (engine `server/routes/instance/httpapi/handlers/experimental.ts:370`). So nothing here reads, posts or writes storage.

- **What it closes.** The app half of review connect:correctness-security #6's failing scenario: "the app keeps the key in sessionStorage and only offers 'Retry review start', which answers pending forever". That is v2-complete-plan §4.1 C6 **B**. With CN-08 (keys reconcile to bound or consumed) and CN-09 (settled keys are pruned), C6 is closed except for the Known limits those two entries record and the Held items below, including the replay-window item CN-09 asked for. As in CN-01, the plan and review-findings files are not edited.
- **Confirm first.** C1 and C2 (controller) and R1 (reservation) were written before any fix and run against `be1eef43`'s sources. All three failed:
  - C1 failed at the discard count (expected 1, received 0);
  - C2 failed because there was no `admissionAnswer`;
  - R1 failed because sessionStorage still held the first key after the engine answered `consumed`.
  At `be1eef43`, every start failure went down one path (`directory-review-controller.ts:207-214`): `admissionUncertain` stayed true and the message was generic. So a consumed key kept "Retry review start", which answered `consumed` for good, and "Review with VectorScope" stayed disabled. The reservation removed its key only in `confirm()`.
- **How the SDK error reaches the app.** `features/connect/client.ts` `directoryReview` passes `{ throwOnError: true, signal }`. `createVectorClient` installs `wrapClientError` (`packages/sdk/js/src/v2/client.ts:92`). With `throwOnError`, that interceptor (`packages/sdk/js/src/error-interceptor.ts:13-43`) throws `new Error(message, { cause: { body, status } })`. Its `message` prefers `body.data.message`, then `body.message`, then `body.name`, and falls back to the request URL and status (`describe`, `:45`). A fetch failure is rethrown as-is, with no such cause.
- **What changed.**
  - *`client.ts`.* `directoryReviewAnswer(error)` (`:68`) returns `{ code: "pending" | "consumed", message }` only for an `Error` whose `cause` has `status === 400` and an object `body` with one of those two codes and a non-empty string `body.message`. Anything else returns `undefined`: another status, another code, an empty or missing message, a non-Error, or a fetch failure. It reads `cause.body.message`, never `error.message`, so the request URL fallback is never shown as the engine's words. The type `DirectoryReviewAnswer` (`:62`) narrows the generated `ConnectApiError["code"]` with `Extract`, so a renamed engine code fails the app typecheck.
  - *`directory-review-reservation.ts`.* `confirm` and the new `discard` (`:66`, `:68`) share one `clear` (`:31`). It throws "The saved review request changed." unless storage still holds this view's exact captured raw marker; otherwise it removes the key and forgets the retained request. So a marker another view wrote stays. `discard` first treats an empty slot (`getItem(key) === null`, `:70`) as already cleared: another view in the same tab confirmed or discarded the same raw key, so nothing is left to protect. It forgets the retained request and returns. `confirm` keeps its strict check. The next `hold(false)` mints a new key. `hold`, `pending`, `request` and the strict 3-field stored format are unchanged.
  - *`directory-review-controller.ts`.*
    - `DirectoryReviewState.admissionAnswer` (`:25`) holds the engine's answer for the saved key. It is a separate field because `read()` clears `error` on every owner read (`:115`, `:128`), and `start()` always refreshes after the POST (`:235`).
    - The `admission` input requires `discard` (`:65`).
    - The busy update at the start of `start()` clears `admissionAnswer` (`:186`).
    - The start failure handler (`:219-247`):
      1. returns at once when the view is no longer current, so a stale answer never discards, just as a stale successful POST never confirms;
      2. on `pending`, keeps the key, sets `admissionUncertain` and stores the answer;
      3. on any other failure except `consumed`, keeps today's generic path;
      4. on `consumed`, calls `discard()` inside a `try` that wraps only that call (`:235-242`), like the `held` IIFE before the POST. It then stores the answer and sets `admissionUncertain` to whether the discard was refused (`:245`). A refused discard (another view replaced the marker, or storage threw) keeps the key held and `admissionUncertain` true, but the engine's answer is still kept for the card. There is no chained `.catch` after the handler, so a fault in `update()` or `changed` there still rejects `start()`, as before CN-10.
    - `read()` drops a `consumed` answer once another view's run replaces it (`:126-137`): when the shown run is live, or is not the run that was shown when the answer arrived. `consumedRun` (`:83`) records that run's id in the consumed branch (`:244`). So the message stays beside the finished run it was answered beside, and goes for good once a live run or a newer run appears, even if that newer run is first seen already done. A refused discard keeps its answer, because its held guidance still applies.
    - Nothing calls `start()` on its own. The existing guards already refuse `start(true)` once `admissionUncertain` is false, so a consumed key can never be retried. A new key is minted only inside the person's next `start()` click (`hold(false)` → `requestKey()`).
    - `bound` is unchanged: a 200 replay goes through `.then` → `confirm()`.
  - *`directory-review.tsx`.*
    - `changed` passes `admissionAnswer` explicitly (`:110`), as it already does for `run` and `permission`. Otherwise the store's shallow merge would keep a previous controller's answer. The unavailable reset clears it too (`:94`).
    - On `consumed`, the card shows the engine's message while no live run is shown (`:227-229`), and the primary button reads "Start a new review" (`:268-272`). It is the same button and the same `controller?.start()`.
    - On `pending`, the engine's message takes the place of the generic "The review start is unconfirmed…" paragraph inside the Retry block (`:232-236`). "Retry review start" is unchanged and is still shown only while `admissionUncertain` is true, so it stays on `pending` and disappears on `consumed`.
    - On a `consumed` answer whose discard was refused, the Retry block keeps the engine's message above it and shows "This view can no longer track its saved review request. Reopen the workspace to continue." in place of the generic paragraph (`:235`). It hides "Retry review start" (`:239`), because that retry can only fail at `hold(true)`. Reopening captures whatever marker is saved now. The primary button stays disabled while `admissionUncertain` is true.
- **Decisions.**
  - A separate `admissionAnswer` field, not `error`, because owner reads clear `error`.
  - The primary button is relabelled for the consumed case. There is no second button.
  - Only a typed 400 `pending` or `consumed` answer is acted on, and everything else keeps the key held, as before. That includes `invalid`, `forbidden`, a 5xx, a body with an unknown or missing code or message, and a fetch failure.
  - Strings stay hard-coded English, as everywhere in these files. The i18n parity test checks only two command keys and stays green.
  - The plan's six-code `Record<ConnectApiError["code"], true>` was dropped, as the critique suggested. The one caller acts only on `pending` and `consumed`, so the helper narrows to those two.
  - The critique's R3 point was right and option (a) was taken. The reservation test R3 checks behaviour only after a consumed answer whose discard is refused: the replacement marker is unchanged, `admissionUncertain` is true, `admissionAnswer` is the engine's `consumed` answer (review round one), and `start()` and `start(true)` send no further POST. The plan's hoisted `UNTRACKED` literal was not added. The "cannot be tracked safely" text R3 also checks comes from the later `start(true)`, whose hold failure returns before any refresh. A message in `error` set in the failure handler would be wiped by the refresh that follows, which is why the held guidance hangs off `admissionAnswer`.
- **Held.**
  - *CN-09's replay-window request is not built.* CN-09's entry asked that "CN-10's recovery should treat a key older than the replay window as possibly new". CN-10 does not do this. An age would change the strict 3-field marker that `read()` checks (`Object.keys(value).length !== 3`), and the brief scoped this package to the three engine answers. The risk is concrete. After the engine prunes a settled key (after `REPLAY_MS`, 24 hours, while its directory or the ledger is full, or after `RETENTION_MS`, 30 days), a retried key is a new request and can start a second review, which may be billed. A retry is always an explicit click, and the engine asks for consent under its policy, but a saved `connect_model` approval can skip that ask. Follow-up, unscheduled: a `savedAt` field in the marker, read in both the old and new shapes, so an old key can be shown as possibly new before a retry.
  - The bound-but-unreplayable answer is `invalid` ("This request key is consumed, but its original owned run is unavailable. No new review was started.", `engine/src/connect/owner.ts:429-433`), so the app keeps that key held for good. Its words say consumed, but the app acts only on the typed code. Changing that engine answer is out of scope (CN-08 Held).
  - The bound-refused replay inconsistency (first 400 `invalid`, retry 200 `refused`) stays as CN-08 recorded it. In the app, the 200 replay confirms and shows the refused result.
  - A first start refused with `forbidden` or `invalid` keeps its key until one explicit retry answers `consumed`, which CN-08 makes the retry's answer. That retry then clears the key and offers "Start a new review".
  - A second mounted view that captured the same raw marker, and retries after another view discarded or confirmed it, stays blocked ("cannot be tracked safely" from `hold(true)`) until it remounts. That is the same behaviour `confirm()` already had. Since review round one, a second view whose own POST answers `consumed` after the slot was emptied recovers: `discard` sees no marker, and the view offers "Start a new review" (R5).
  - `start(true)` called directly on a consumed answer whose discard was refused clears the answer at its busy update and then fails at `hold(true)`, so the generic uncertain text returns. The card hides Retry in that state, so this is reachable only from code (R3 does it on purpose).
  - A run selected by a stored `runID` is read with `get` alone, as before CN-10. So once a finished run is shown, a run another view starts later is not listed until this view reselects (a start or a remount), and a consumed message shown beside that finished run stays until then.
  - While `pending`, if a later owner read finds the bound run, the pending message and Retry stay until a click, as today's Retry block does.
  - Q6 neutral: this uses only the existing `POST /experimental/connect/review` route and the file ledger. Flags stay off, and Q1 stays open.
- **Deferred verification.** The new e2e case, "a consumed review key is cleared and only an explicit new start sends a fresh key" (`directory-review.spec.ts:317`), was not run locally (rules), and no CI run is claimed for it. It runs in `connect-app-validation.yml` (push to `main` or `workflow_dispatch`; `--retries 0`) and in test.yml's app e2e job on a pull request (`bun --cwd packages/app test:e2e`, retries 2 in CI). A push to `connect-finish` alone runs neither. After this package's push, dispatch `connect-app-validation.yml` on `connect-finish` and record the run here; until then it is deferred, with no CI run. Its flow:
  1. a lost POST;
  2. Retry answers 400 `pending`: the engine's message shows, Retry stays enabled, and one marker is saved;
  3. Retry answers 400 `consumed`: the engine's message shows, Retry is gone, "Start a new review" is enabled, and no `connect.directory-admission.` key is left;
  4. a Refresh and a `connect.run.updated` event send no POST, and the three bodies are identical;
  5. "Start a new review" sends a fourth body with a new UUID and the same target and background, the card shows "Review in progress", and no marker is left.
  `bun run typecheck:e2e` (static tsgo, no Playwright) passes.
- **Receipts.** All from `packages/app`, through `/home/user/.tools/vector-serial`, one file at a time with `--timeout 30000`.
  - `src/features/connect/directory-review-controller.test.ts`: 27 pass, 0 fail, 151 expect() calls. That is 13 at `be1eef43`, plus C1, C2, C3 and six C4 cases, plus five from review round one: C5 (a refused discard keeps the key held and the engine's answer, also beside a live run listed later), C6 (the answer stays beside the finished run it was answered beside), two C7 cases (another view's later running or done run replaces the answer for good) and C8 (a consumed retry beside another view's live run leaves only that run).
  - `src/features/connect/directory-review-reservation.test.ts`: 9 pass, 0 fail, 83 expect() calls. That is 4 at `be1eef43`, plus R1 to R4, plus R5 from review round one: two views hold the same key, both retries answer `consumed`, the second discard finds the slot empty, both views offer a new start, and the second view's click sends a fresh key. R3 now also asserts the kept `consumed` answer, and R4 now asserts that `discard` on an empty slot is a no-op that mints nothing.
  - `src/features/connect/client.test.ts`: 6 pass, 0 fail, 31 expect() calls. That is 5 at `be1eef43`, plus one.
  - `src/i18n/parity.test.ts`: 1 pass, 0 fail, 68 expect() calls.
  - Mutations, each run against all three connect test files and restored from a saved copy, verified with `sha256sum -c`:

    | Mutation | Caught by |
    | --- | --- |
    | `consumed` handled as the generic failure | C1, R1 |
    | `discard` does not remove the key | R1, R3, R4 |
    | `discard` skips the exact-raw check | R3, R4 |
    | `consumed` starts again automatically, once | C1, R1 |
    | the same, without a bound (an automatic retry loop) | R1. The controller file hangs instead: its fakes answer only in microtasks, so the loop starves the 30 s test timer. The run was stopped by `timeout 120`. |
    | `directoryReviewAnswer` ignores the status | C4 (a consumed code on another status), and the client case |

    | a stale answer discards (no `current()` check) | C3 |
    | the message read from `error.message`, with the body-message check removed | C4 (a consumed answer without a message), and the client case |
    | only the returned message taken from `error.message` | the client case, through its `data.message` body. Before that assertion was added, this mutant was equivalent for the engine's real body shape. |
    | the busy update keeps `admissionAnswer` | C1, C2, R1, R2 |

## 2026-11-15 — CW-21a: Hands list-only adapter (CN-11)

Source: `connect-finish` at `bf7fb313`. This package adds `packages/engine/src/connect/adapters/hands.ts` and changes `packages/engine/src/connect/adapters/index.ts` and `packages/engine/src/connect/members.ts`. Its tests are the new `packages/engine/test/connect/hands.test.ts`, one new case in `test/connect/vectorscope.test.ts`, and updated expectations in `test/connect/hub.test.ts`. The spec's status line and §6 Hands row, and the handoff's Remaining work, are updated. It changes no route, public schema, SDK or generated file, flag, workflow or core file, and nothing under Hands' own files (engine `computer/**`, `tool/computer.*`, schema `hands.ts`), which are only imported.

- **What it closes.** The first half of CW-21: spec §9's CW-21 row (:520, "connect/adapters/hands.ts (list-only, `ComputerAvailability.unavailable()`)"), §5's Hands row (:339, "List-only adapter … This branch, CW-21"), §6's Hands row (:396, "This branch writes adapters/hands.ts"), and the parts of I10 (:537) and I11 (:538) that connect owns. The second half, connect rows in the Tasks pane (CW-21b), is CN-12 in CN-01's package table. No connect review finding covers CW-21. v2-complete-plan.md lists CW-20–25 among the Tier C build work outside the draft ("Connect Q6 consolidation and CW-20–25", in the list after §6's table). The critique pointed to §5 step 12, but that step names only the Tier B fixes (C3, C5, C6, C9, F10). As in CN-01, neither the plan nor the review-findings file is edited.
- **Before.** At `bf7fb313`, `members.ts` `toolMembers` (:221-243) had a static `computer` row. It read `ready` whenever the registry's tool ids held `computer`, whatever Hands' real availability was, and `not-installed` "Not in this build." (reach `none`) otherwise. Without tool ids, `toolMembers` returned nothing. `adapters/index.ts` held VectorScope's adapter alone.
- **What changed.**
  - *`adapters/hands.ts`* (self-export `ConnectHandsAdapter`). `ID` is `Hands.ID` ("computer", the member id and the tool id), `Services` is `ComputerAvailability.Service`, and `DEPS` is `[ComputerAvailability.node]`. The factory returns `{ id, owner: "hands", describe }` and nothing else: no `handle`, no actions, no `sendsTo`. `describe()` maps `ComputerAvailability.unavailable()` to a `tool` member titled `Hands.NAME`, owner `hands`, place `local`, reach `computer`. With no reason it is `ready`; with any reason it is `unavailable`, with `ComputerAvailability.message(reason)` as its reason.
  - *`adapters/index.ts`*. `Services` widens to `VectorScopeAdapter.Services | ConnectHandsAdapter.Services`, `ADAPTERS` is `[VectorScopeAdapter.factory, ConnectHandsAdapter.factory]`, and `DEPS` spreads both. `ConnectHub.node` (hub.ts:1742) already spreads `ADAPTER_DEPS`, so it now builds `ComputerAvailability.node`. The registry already builds that node (`tool/registry.ts:659`), so the engine graph builds no new service. The comment names only CW-22 as still to come.
  - *`members.ts`*. The static `computer` element of `toolMembers` is gone. `compose` now passes each adapter member through `registered` (:124, :157). A member whose `reach` is `connect`, `ui` or `none` is kept as it is. A member reached through a tool is left out without tool ids, kept as described when the registry offers its tool, and otherwise becomes `not-installed`, "Not in this build.", reach `none`. `NAMED_TOOLS` keeps `computer`, so it never becomes a `tool:computer` row. The doc comments on `Sources.adapters`, `NAMED_TOOLS` and the file header say so. No exported signature changed.
- **Decisions.**
  - *Why `members.ts` applies the registry's offer.* `Adapter.describe()` takes no input. The adapter cannot depend on `ToolRegistry`, because the registry depends on `ConnectHub.node` (`tool/registry.ts:704`), which would close a cycle. Passing the tool ids into `describe` means editing `connect/hub.ts` (`members` and `describeSafely`) and `adapter.ts`. `hub.ts` is in `REVIEW_PIPELINE_FILES` (core `test/review/model-evidence.test.ts`), so editing it would force a repin of VectorScope's `REVIEW_PIPELINE_SOURCES` in core `src/review/model-evidence.ts`. That is the §7.1 ownership deviation CN-06 to CN-08 needed. So CN-11 edits neither file. The rejected alternative was to widen `describe(input?: { tools })` and repin.
  - *Two sources, on purpose.* The brief asks for one source of the Hands member. `hands.ts` is the source of the member, its state and Hands' reason. `members.ts` `registered` is a second, deliberate source for one fact, whether this build offers the tool, together with its "Not in this build." text and reach `none`, which HEAD's static row already used. The rule is general: any later adapter whose `reach` is a tool id falls under it, and the comment at `registered` says so for CN-13.
  - *`not-installed` keeps reach `none`*, as HEAD's row did, although the brief lists reach `computer` for the member: with no tool there is nothing to reach. In that case the row reads "Not in this build." even where Hands' own reason (for example `desktop`) would be more precise.
    Outside the desktop app (CLI or TUI) the registry offers `computer` only when `VECTOR_COMPUTER_BRIDGE_URL` and `VECTOR_COMPUTER_BRIDGE_TOKEN` are set (`tool/registry.ts:427-429`), so on those clients the row reads "Not in this build." rather than Hands' `desktop` reason, as HEAD's static row did. Follow-up for CN-12 or CN-20: keep the adapter's own `unavailable` reason (with reach `none`) whenever Hands reports one, and use "Not in this build." only when Hands reports ready but the tool is missing.
  - *`describe()` always runs.* `hub.members` describes every adapter before it composes the list (hub.ts:1665), so Hands' `unavailable()` is called once per list even when the row is then dropped or marked not installed (hands.test.ts asserts `probes` 2 for those two lists). Without the bridge environment variables its probe is `Effect.succeed`, with no network call. With them, it is Hands' existing loopback probe: Egress-gated, cached for 30 s, and already made by the registry each turn. It is not a new destination (I4). The adapter's only I/O is that probe. `hands.ts` is not added to hub.test.ts's text scan "no network or model call outside adapters", because that test exempts adapters by name and checks only a file's own text.
  - *No catch of its own.* A defect in `describe()` falls to the hub's `describeSafely` (hub.ts:1828-1848), which shows `unavailable` "Connect could not check whether it is ready." That fallback has kind `agent`, title `computer`, summary "" and reach `none`. `registered` passes every reach-`none` member through, so after a failed probe the row stays in that shape whatever the tool ids say. It never reads ready (I10). hands.test.ts case 4 asserts the whole shape for all three lists. A catch in `hands.ts` would only change the look, so none was added.
  - *Order.* The Hands row moves from after Browser to just after VectorScope, because adapters come first. The app's `memberRows` keeps the engine's order, and no app test asserts the position.
  - *Critique taken.* Keep-green runs now include `after-run`, `directory`, `directory-live`, `worktree-target` and `test/tool/connect.test.ts`. Of those, `after-run.test.ts` (:270 with :284) and `directory.test.ts` (:155 with :166) list `ConnectHub.node` but replace it with `nodeWith` in the same graph, and the other three build the real hub, through the registry for the tool test. A layer-dependency mutation (below) showed hub.test.ts's own graph builds the real hub too. The not-installed and no-tool-ids cases assert `probes` 2, and only the refused hand-offs assert 0. Case 4 asserts the fallback's full shape. The plan's case 8 (subagent and helper refusals, which hub.test.ts already covers) and case 11 (Hands' own `sessionReason`, which Hands' `availability.test.ts` covers) were dropped. The I11 live case follows the existing in-process pattern: off Linux it asserts the refusal, no helper prompt, no provider input and no child session, rather than skipping.
  - *Not taken.* The spec's §5 Hands row (:339, "On main today: Listed as 'not in this build'") stays as it is. Its column describes `main`, and CN-01 kept §5 as contract history under the status line's reading note. The status line and §6 carry CN-11's state.
  - *Ownership pre-check.* `git fetch origin hands-finish` succeeded (`bca15854`). That branch has no `connect/adapters/hands.ts`, and its `members.ts`, `adapter.ts`, `adapters/index.ts` and `computer/availability.ts` blobs equal `bf7fb313`'s (`833bf3cb`, `7e64f26f`, `fd57dbed`, `e81f22b4`). So nothing is duplicated, and the `Reason` union is the one Hands' branch has. The lane's commit gate repeats the fetch. A failed fetch is recorded, never read as "no change".
- **Review round one.** Seven non-blocking findings, no blocking one.
  - *Taken.* (1) The non-desktop note above (`members.ts:161`), with its follow-up. (2) The held background job is pinned in hands.test.ts (`:240`); its graph adds `BackgroundJob.node` so the test can read the job, and the comment points to the held item. (6) hub.test.ts's members comment (`:1675`) now says the hub under test is built over fakes (`nodeWith`), so it no longer reads as contradicting the mutation table's "hub.test.ts's own graph builds the real hub too". (7) vectorscope.test.ts's I11 case says by its platform branch that the Linux branch needs an unprivileged user, because ReadOnlyGit refuses root (CN-02), so run as root it fails at its first assertion. The test is neither skipped nor loosened.
  - *Not taken, by decision.* (5) Keeping Hands' own reason when the registry does not offer `computer` (`members.ts:161`). The brief fixes that case as `not-installed`, "Not in this build.", and finding (1) asks to keep the brief's behaviour for now, so the change is the follow-up recorded above rather than part of CN-11. (3) A failed Hands probe shows the hub's reach-`none` fallback even without tool ids (`hands.test.ts:205`). The reviewer asks for no change: the row is `unavailable`, never `ready`, `ComputerAvailability.unavailable()` has no error channel so only a defect reaches it, and case 4's comment explains it. A later package may drop a reach-`none` fallback whose id is in `NAMED_TOOLS` when `tools` is undefined. (4) `hub.members` describes adapters one after another (`hub.ts:1665`), so Hands' first uncached probe (Hands' own 5 s timeout, `computer/availability.ts:139`, only when the bridge variables are set and the bridge is down) holds up the list, also for lists that then drop the row. `hub.ts` is in `REVIEW_PIPELINE_FILES`, so it is left; the options for a later package are `Effect.forEach(..., { concurrency: "unbounded" })` or skipping tool-reached adapters when `input.tools` is undefined. "`describe()` always runs" above stays accurate.
- **Safe by default.** Hands stays off until its onboarding, which is Hands' own config and consent; the adapter only reads. With Connect off the member list is reached only through the flag-gated owner, so the adapter is never described. Nothing reaches Hands through connect: the hub refuses every hand-off to `computer` as `unknown` ("computer is only listed; connect cannot hand work to it.", hub.ts:929-932) before it describes the member or asks anything, for the conversation's agent, a person, a workflow stage, a background run and an after-run review alike (hands.test.ts). The model's `connect` tool accepts only `to: "vectorscope"`, `HELPER_MEMBERS` lacks `computer`, and `ConnectPolicy.FIX_TOOLS` excludes it, so the `connect_fix` child is never offered `computer`, whatever the rules say. Q1 and Q6 are neutral: no route, flag or ledger changed.
- **Held, pre-existing, unscheduled.**
  - A refused hand-off to `computer` (reachable only through the owner-authenticated review route's `to`) is still recorded in the ledger, so it shows as activity on the Hands row. A background one first starts a `connect` background job titled "VectorScope review" (or "VectorScope fix") with `member: "computer"` (hub.ts:1489-1500) before admission refuses it. Both are in `hub.ts`, which is pinned, so they are left as they are. hands.test.ts's hand-off case pins the job: for each background hand-off, `BackgroundJob.wait` (5 s) finds a `connect` job with that title, `status: "completed"` and `metadata` `{ background: true, runID, member: "computer", action }`, ended with the refused run, and only the two background hand-offs start a job. A change to this held behaviour, either way, now fails that case.
  - The `computer` tool's own exclusion from subagents, workflows and helper sessions is Hands' `ComputerAvailability.sessionReason` (`unattended` for any `parentID` or `internal`), tested in Hands' own `availability.test.ts`. Connect only relies on it.
  - CN-12 (Tasks pane rows), CW-23's `connect_cloud`, and the hosted computer stay held as in CN-01.

## 2026-11-15 — CW-21b: connect runs as rows in the desktop Tasks pane (CN-12)

Source: `connect-finish` at `a350a80c`. This package adds `packages/app/src/features/connect/task-rows.ts` (the pure model), `task-rows-section.tsx` (the view), `task-rows.css` (its scroll box) and `task-rows.test.ts`, mounts the view with a two-line additive hunk in `packages/app/src/features/background-tasks/background-tasks-pane.tsx` (the import at :58 and the mount at :577), and adds two cases at the end of `packages/app/e2e/regression/connected-tools.spec.ts` (:549 and :666, with `child`, `openTasks` and `task` added to its import). Review round one adds an optional `signal` to `cancel` in `packages/app/src/features/connect/client.ts`. The spec's status line and the handoff's Remaining work are updated. It changes no route, public schema, SDK or generated file, flag, workflow, engine, core or website file, and no Hands file: `background-tasks.css`, `background-tasks-button.tsx`, the computer card and the rest of the pane are untouched.

- **What it closes.** The second half of CW-21: spec §9's CW-21 row (:520, "connect rows in the Tasks pane"), §5's Hands row (:339, "List-only adapter and Tasks pane rows"), §3.5 (:197, "The Tasks pane gains connect rows after Hands merges") and §8's hands row (:470). CN-11 built the first half. No connect review finding covers CW-21, and as in CN-01 neither v2-complete-plan.md nor the review-findings file is edited.
- **Before.** At `a350a80c` nothing in `features/background-tasks` listed connect runs; a search for `connect` there finds only the `reconnects` input. The conversation's runs were visible only in Settings → Agents → Connected tools and as findings cards in the timeline.
- **What changed.**
  - *`task-rows.ts`.* Plain named exports, as the other app `features/connect` files use (`connect-model.ts`, `client.ts`, `handoff.ts`); the `export * as ConnectX` self-export is the engine and core connect pattern. `taskRows` keeps only runs whose `request.requester.sessionID` is the root session, so another conversation's runs and directory runs (no session) are dropped. Runs still going come first, in the engine's newest-first order, then finished ones. There are at most `MAX_TASK_ROWS` (10) rows in all, but a run still going is never cut, so runs going can push the total above 10 (four going leave room for six finished). Each row has every key, an absent value being `undefined`: `id`, `title` (the registry member's title, else `request.to`), `action` (Review or Fix), `status` and `tone` (queued: Queued/pending; running: Running/running; done by `result.state`: Done and Partial/done, Refused and Failed/failed, Cancelled/stopped), `verdict` (`verdictLabel` only for a finished review that completed or is partial), `sendsTo`, `spend`, `detail` and `cancellable`. A finished run says "Code sent to: " with `destinationLabel(result.sendsTo)` and never borrows the member's current destination; a run still going says "Sends code to: " with the member's. A run is a Codium run when the member's owner is `codium`, the member or the result sends to `vector-codium`, or the cost kind is `plan`, and then its spend is always "Plan usage". A finished run whose member is unknown (it left the registry, or the registry read failed on a fresh key) fails closed: it uses `costLabel` only when `result.sendsTo` names a destination, which by then is not `vector-codium`, and otherwise shows "Usage unavailable", so "never dollars for Codium" does not rest on the registry read succeeding. Other finished runs use `costLabel`, and runs still going show no spend. `Result` has no token field, so a row never shows tokens. `detail` is the progress message while going and `result.summary` (written by Vector code, schema :408) for a refused or failed run; finding titles and bodies are never read (I2). `cancellable` is `status !== "done"`, queued included, as `hub.cancel` (hub.ts:636-640) cancels any run that has not finished.
  - *`readTaskRows`* returns `undefined` with no request while `connections` is off or there is no session. Any failed runs read (a guest's 403, the engine's `forbidden`, `disabled` or `invalid`, the network, an abort) also gives `undefined`, and the registry is then not read. No runs gives `{ rows: [] }` without a registry read. The registry (`list(sessionID)`) is read only when a run names a member the caller does not already know; a failed registry read keeps the known members, so titles fall back to ids. `cancelTaskRow` makes no request for a row that is not cancellable ("ended") and otherwise posts `cancel(runID, sessionID, signal)`: true is "cancelled", false "ended", a rejection, an abort or a timeout "failed".
  - *`task-rows-section.tsx`* (`ConnectTaskRows`). It reads through the pane's directory SDK (`useSDK`), so every read and cancel uses the owner-only, directory-scoped routes (`packages/server/src/auth.ts:203-209`; owner.ts `requireSession`). Its key is the client, the root, the stream's reconnect count and an authority epoch. The epoch is bumped on `server.connected` and `global.disposed` (global) and on the directory's `server.instance.disposed`, as findings-card.tsx and connected-tools.tsx do, which aborts the read in flight, hides the rows at once and forces a fresh owner read. Rows show only from the latest read for the current key, so an older response can never repopulate them. After a refused or failed read the key is blocked, as findings-card's `blocked` client is: no further owner reads, and no event subscription, until the key changes. While the root session has a pending revert the key is empty, so no read is made (`requireSession` would answer `invalid`) and settling the revert gives one fresh read. `connect.run.updated` events for the root session refetch at most once per 500 ms; events for other sessions are ignored, and their payload never becomes a row. Nothing polls, and nothing reads while the pane is closed, because `Pane` mounts only while it is open. `resource.latest` is read only once the resource is `ready` or `refreshing`, because before the first answer it would suspend the surrounding boundary. Rows are keyed by run id, so a refresh keeps each row's DOM and focus with its own run. The section renders only with rows: a refused read, a guest, no flag or no runs show no heading and no error. Markup reuses the pane's classes (`vector-bg-tasks-section`, `-section-head`, `-section-label`, `-shell-list`, `-shell`, `-meta`, `-card-status[data-status]`, `-actions`, `-notice`), its heading is "Connected tools" with the list labelled "Connected tool runs", all text renders as text, and the only button is Cancel while a run is going. A cancel is posted with `AbortSignal.timeout(30_000)`, as the read is, so a hung cancel resolves to "failed" and the Cancel buttons re-enable. A cancel whose key changed meanwhile drops its outcome; "ended" shows "This run had already ended." and "failed" "Could not cancel this run.", and either way the rows are refetched. The notice keeps its run id and shows only while that run is listed, and "Could not cancel this run." also goes once the row is no longer cancellable; the next cancel and an authority change clear it too.
  - *Changed on 2026-11-16 (entry "connect-finish lane: phase-review fixes").* `readTaskRows` now answers `"refused"` for a 4xx (the guest's 403 and the engine's `forbidden`, `disabled` and `invalid` above) and `"failed"` for the network, an abort, a timeout, a 5xx or an answer that does not decode. Only a refusal blocks the key. A failed read keeps that key's last rows and blocks nothing, so the next `connect.run.updated` event, a reopened pane or an authority change reads again. The two bullets above describe the behaviour before that change.
  - *`task-rows.css`.* The mount sits beside the pane's only scroller (`.vector-bg-tasks-list`, `flex: 1`, background-tasks.css:338-349) inside the `overflow: hidden` pane (:106-115), so the section scrolls on its own: `flex: none`, `max-height: 45%`, `min-height: 0`, `overflow-y: auto` and the list's 8px gutter. It never splits the pane's height with a second `flex: 1`, and the task list or the empty state keeps the rest. Two small classes style the title and detail lines. `background-tasks.css` is not edited.
  - *Pane hunk.* `<ConnectTaskRows rootID={tasks.rootID()} connections={tasks.capabilities().connections} />` between the task list's closing `</Show>` and the `vector-bg-tasks-sr` live region, far from `ComputerCard` (:477) and Hands' regions. The section sits below Running, Background commands and Finished.
  - *E2E cases.* The brief asked for one CI-only case; two were added. The main case (:549) covers the rows, Cancel, events, authority changes and refusals. The second (:666) answers the scroll-box critique below: it proves the last row's Cancel stays reachable below a long task list, which needs 14 busy child sessions and so did not fit in the first case. Both are pending in CI (see Deferred verification).
- **Critique taken.**
  - *Scroll box (major).* Taken as `task-rows.css` above, with the e2e case "connected runs scroll in their own box below a long task list, so the last Cancel stays reachable": with 14 task cards and 10 running connect rows, the last row's Cancel scrolls into view inside the section, the pane itself stays at `scrollTop` 0, the section ends inside the pane, the task list keeps a height, and the click posts that run's cancel.
  - *Authority changes (major).* Taken as the epoch above. The earlier "an instance reload without a reconnect refreshes only on the next event" is no longer held. The main e2e case sends `global.disposed` while an owner read is held, waits until the fresh read is held as well, and asserts the section is gone while both are still pending. It then releases the fresh read as a 403 and the old read as an owner answer, and asserts the rows stay gone. Next it sends `server.connected`, asserts nothing shows while the fresh read is held, and sees the rows return only after that read is released.
  - *No reads after a refusal (minor).* Taken as the blocked key. The throttle is 500 ms rather than connected-tools' 250 ms, because each `hub.runs({ sessionID })` scans the session's stored messages and a row needs only coarse progress. The main e2e case asserts that two more root events after a refusal make no `/runs` request.
  - *Cap wording (minor).* One rule everywhere: at most 10 rows in all, and runs still going are never cut (the doc comment, this entry and unit case 3, which adds the four-going-plus-six-finished case).
  - *Row shape (minor).* Every key is always present. Case 1 uses `toStrictEqual` and case 13 asserts the exact sorted key list.
  - *Revert (minor).* Taken: the revert marker is part of the key, as described above.
  - *Serial wrapper (minor).* `prettier` and `oxlint` ran as `/home/user/.tools/vector-serial bunx …`.
  - *Status wording (minor).* The spec status line and the handoff say "built uncommitted" and that the e2e cases' CI run is pending. The ownership check is repeated at the commit gate.
- **Decisions.**
  - The view is its own file, so the pane hunk is two lines and the view can import its own stylesheet.
  - Cancel covers queued as well as running, as `hub.cancel` does. Owner proof is the successful owner read that produced the rows, and the engine checks again on the cancel route.
  - Any refused or failed read hides the section and blocks further reads until authority, the stream, the client, the root or the revert state changes: it fails closed, also for a timeout.
  - Tests live in `features/connect` rather than the "app background-tasks tests" of §9's Tests column, because the model belongs to connect; the two named background-tasks files are kept green.
  - Not taken from the plan: a component test of the view. The app has no rendering test for the pane, so the view's behaviour is covered by the two CI-only e2e cases.
  - The second e2e case goes beyond the brief's one case. It is kept separate because the reachability of the last Cancel needs a long task list (14 busy child sessions) that would change every count and position the main case asserts. It adds CI-only surface, and no new product code.
- **Ownership pre-check.** `git fetch origin hands-finish` succeeded (`bca15854`). Its `background-tasks-pane.tsx` blob equals `a350a80c`'s (`b89b1756`), and no file under `features/background-tasks` differs between the two, so nothing is duplicated. The lane's commit gate repeats the fetch; a failed fetch is recorded, never read as "no change".
- **Held.**
  - The empty-state copy ("No background tasks") can show above connect rows, and the launcher badge does not count connect runs: both are Hands' files (`background-tasks-pane.tsx` outside this hunk, `background-tasks-button.tsx`).
  - A `connect_fix` worktree child also appears as its own task card, as spec :282 intends for merging, so a worktree fix shows twice.
  - Runs whose requester is a child session of the root are not listed in the pane. That includes a `connect` call made by a background subagent whose session shows as a task card in the same pane. `taskRows` keeps only `requester.sessionID === rootID`, and the engine's `runs(sessionID)` filters the same way. This fails closed, and spec §3.5 (:197) and §9's CW-21 row (:520) do not mention child sessions. Listing them would need one owner read per child, or an engine read that walks the session tree.
  - A run whose member has left the registry re-reads the registry on each refresh, because its id stays unknown. That is bounded by the 500 ms throttle and only happens while events arrive.
  - CN-11's follow-up (keep Hands' own reason when the `computer` tool is absent) is not taken and stays for CN-20 or later.
  - The TUI and the phone are unchanged.
  - Q1 and Q6 are neutral: no route, ledger or flag changed.
- **Safe by default.** `VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL_CONNECT` stays off. Off, `connections` is false, as it is for an older engine or a missing capability route, so `readTaskRows` makes no request and the section never renders. A guest's 403 and every engine refusal render nothing. A click can only cancel: there is no Fix, Dismiss, worktree or composer action, and finding text never enters a row. Codium shows plan usage, never dollars or tokens. Reads happen only on mount, a key change, a root `connect.run.updated` event or a reconnect, and only while the pane is open.
- **Deferred verification.** The two e2e cases have not run anywhere: the rules forbid Playwright here. They run in test.yml's app e2e job on the PR (`bun --cwd packages/app test:e2e`) and in `connect-app-validation.yml` (push to `main` or `workflow_dispatch`), which the lead can dispatch on `connect-finish` and record. `bun run typecheck:e2e` checked them statically. No CI run is claimed.
- **Review round one.** Nine non-blocking findings; none was blocking. This entry describes the code after the round.
  1. *Codium spend without the registry member.* Taken: the fail-closed rule under `task-rows.ts` above. New unit case 12 has a failed registry read on a fresh key: a `codium`-id run with a priced result and no `sendsTo` shows "Usage unavailable", and an unknown member whose result names a provider still shows dollars. Case 2's unknown `fixer` run, whose result names no destination, now expects "Usage unavailable" instead of "Free model".
  2. *Authority bullet ordering.* Taken, as the first option: the fresh read after `global.disposed` is held too, and the section is asserted gone while both reads are pending, before the fresh one is released as a 403. Held reads now take the answer they are released with. The "Authority changes" bullet above is reworded to match.
  3. and 7. *Two e2e cases where the brief asked for one.* Both are kept, and the reason is recorded under What changed (*E2E cases*) and Decisions.
  4. *Notice outlives its run.* Taken in part. The notice keeps its run id and shows only while that run is listed, and "Could not cancel this run." also goes once the row is no longer cancellable. Not taken: dropping "This run had already ended." when the row's status changes. The refetch that the cancel itself starts is the read that shows the run as ended, so the notice would vanish on that very read and only flash. It goes once the run leaves the list, or on the next cancel or authority change.
  5. and 9. *Child-session runs.* Recorded under Held; the code is unchanged, because the filter fails closed.
  6. *Hung cancel.* Taken. `createConnectClient(...).cancel` takes an optional `signal`, passed to the SDK call as `list`, `runs` and `get` already do. `cancelTaskRow` passes it through, and the section calls it with `AbortSignal.timeout(30_000)`. Unit case 9 adds a cancel that never answers until its signal aborts: it resolves to "failed". The two existing callers (`directory-review.tsx`, `connected-tools.tsx`) are unchanged.
  8. *Citation.* The owner gate is cited as `packages/server/src/auth.ts:203-209`.
- **Receipts.** Every `bun` and `bunx` command ran through `/home/user/.tools/vector-serial`, one test file at a time from packages/app, with `--timeout 30000`. Logs are in `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn12/` and the mutation run in `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn12-mut/`. Review round one's reruns are in `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn12-fix/`.
  - *After review round one,* on the final sources: `src/features/connect/task-rows.test.ts` 13 pass, 0 fail, 62 expect() calls. Keep-green: `connect/client.test.ts` 6 pass (31 expect() calls), `background-tasks/task-board-model.test.ts` 22 pass (75), `background-tasks/background-tasks-state.test.ts` 18 pass (45), all with 0 fail. `bun typecheck` and `bun run typecheck:e2e` passed in packages/app. `prettier --check` passed on the five changed code files, and `git diff --check` is clean. oxlint on the same five files gave 0 errors and 2 warnings, both on lines this package did not change (connected-tools.spec.ts :111-112). There were two mutations, each restored from `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn12-fix/orig/` with its sha256 checked: dropping the unknown-member rule failed cases 2 and 12; dropping `signal` from `client.cancel` made case 9 time out.
  - `src/features/connect/task-rows.test.ts` (new): 12 pass, 0 fail, 56 expect() calls, on the first submission.

All had 0 fail.

  - Static checks:

The ownership fetch was repeated: `origin/hands-finish` is still `bca15854`, its pane blob is still `b89b1756`, and it changes nothing under `features/background-tasks`, `features/connect` or `connected-tools.spec.ts` since the merge base `0ee486e3`.

## 2026-11-15 — CW-22: GitHub agent list-only adapter (CN-13)

Source: `connect-finish` at `21d35b87`. This package adds `packages/engine/src/connect/adapters/github-agent.ts` and `packages/engine/test/connect/github-agent.test.ts`. It changes `packages/engine/src/connect/adapters/index.ts`, `packages/engine/src/connect/members.ts` and `packages/engine/src/connect/hub.ts` (pinned, see Decisions), plus one constant in `packages/core/src/review/model-evidence.ts` and two expectations in `packages/engine/test/connect/hub.test.ts`. The spec's status line and §6 @vector row, and the handoff's Remaining work, are updated. It changes no route, public schema, SDK or generated file, flag, effect, consent or workflow, and no @vector file: core `github-agents.ts` and `review/workflow.ts`, engine `cli/cmd/github.*` and desktop `main/github-agents.ts` are only read or imported.

- **What it closes.** CW-22 as far as it goes without the owner: spec §9's CW-22 row (:521, "Installed state and CLI pin are correct"), §5's @vector row (:340), §6's @vector row (:397, "`adapters/github-agent.ts`: installed state from `WORKFLOW_FILE`, the CLI pin against `GithubAgents.MIN_AGENTS_CLI`"), §8's table (:472, "this branch for the adapter file"), and the parts of I8 (:535) and I10 (:537) that connect owns. No review finding covers CW-22. The plan and review-findings files are not edited.
- **Before.** At `21d35b87`, `hub.ts` `members()` (:1683) set `githubAgent` from `isFile(path.join(ctx.worktree, WORKFLOW_FILE))` (:2195-2199): a `stat` that follows links, with no size bound and no read of the file. On a folder outside Git, `ctx.worktree` is "/", so it looked at `/.github/workflows/vector.yml` at the filesystem root. `members.ts` `githubAgentMember` (:319-333) turned that boolean into the row: `ready` whenever the file existed, whatever it pinned, or `not-installed` otherwise.
- **Before → after, as users see it.** A workflow file that exists used to read `ready`. Now it reads `ready` only when every `npm install -g @vectordevai/cli` line pins an exact semver of at least `GithubAgents.MIN_AGENTS_CLI`, and a header that @vector's reader accepts agrees. Its summary then says "Pinned to <version>." `MIN_AGENTS_CLI` is "1.99.113", a "provisional unpublished release reservation" (core `github-agents.ts:11`). So today every published install, including every legacy v2 workflow (pinned to, for example, 1.17.14), reads `unavailable` with the below reason until that CLI ships. The row also moves from the end of the list to just after Hands, because adapters come first in `compose`, and an adapter's member wins over any other source with the same id.
- **What changed.**
  - *`adapters/github-agent.ts`* (self-export `ConnectGithubAgentAdapter`). `ID` is "github-agent" and `MAX_BYTES` is 1 MiB. `factory` is `Effect.succeed({ id, owner: "vector-at-mention", describe })`: no services, no `handle`, no actions, no `sendsTo`. The row keeps HEAD's id, kind `agent`, title "@vector on GitHub", owner, place `your-cloud`, reach `none` and the "Connect never posts to GitHub." sentence. `describe()` (:28-37) returns `not-installed` at once for a "/" worktree. Otherwise it reads the file through `read()` (:125) and `walk()` (:157) and maps it through `member()` (:74) and `pin()` (:102). The states and reasons:

    | What is there | State | Reason or summary |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | No file, a missing parent, or a parent that is a regular file; any folder outside Git | `not-installed` | "Not set up in this repository (.github/workflows/vector.yml)." (HEAD's text) |
    | Pinned: every install line exact and ≥ MIN, any accepted header agrees | `ready` | summary "Answers @vector on pull requests from your own GitHub Actions. Pinned to <version>. Connect never posts to GitHub." |
    | The lowest exact version (an install line or the header) below MIN | `unavailable` | "Pinned to <version>; @vector needs Vector CLI <MIN> or newer." |
    | An install line with no version, `latest`, a range, an expression or an over-long version | `unavailable` | ".github/workflows/vector.yml does not pin a Vector CLI version." |
    | No install line, an accepted header whose version is not exact, or header and jobs disagree | `unavailable` | "Connect could not read which Vector CLI version .github/workflows/vector.yml pins." |
    | A link at `.github`, `.github/workflows` or the file | `unavailable` | "Connect reads .github/workflows/vector.yml only as a regular file, never through a link." |
    | A directory, FIFO or other non-regular file | `unavailable` | ".github/workflows/vector.yml is not a regular file." |
    | Over 1 MiB | `unavailable` | ".github/workflows/vector.yml is larger than the 1 MiB connect reads." |
    | Any other read error (EACCES, ELOOP), or a file that grew, shrank or was replaced while it was read | `unavailable` | "Connect could not read .github/workflows/vector.yml." |

  - *`adapters/index.ts`*. `ADAPTERS` gains `ConnectGithubAgentAdapter.factory` after Hands'. `Services` and `DEPS` are unchanged, and the comment says why.
  - *`members.ts`*. `Sources.githubAgent`, its `compose` candidate and `githubAgentMember` are gone, so the adapter is the one source. The header comment names the adapter.
  - *`hub.ts`*. The `WORKFLOW_FILE` import, the `InstanceState.context` read used only for it, the `githubAgent` source and `isFile` are removed. Nothing else changes. `core/review/workflow` is now imported by the adapter, which the hub loads, so the module graph is the same.
- **Decisions.**
  - *Non-ready is `unavailable`, never `ready`* (I10). Only "nothing there" is `not-installed`, and every doubt about the file or its pin reads `unavailable` with a plain reason.
  - *Links are refused at every component under the worktree,* not only the leaf. Each component is `lstat`ed in order, only a regular file is opened, and the open uses `O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK` with an `fstat` recheck and a read that loops until the end or one byte past the size, as `directory-request.ts` does, so only a real grow or shrink reads `failed`. After the read the same walk runs again and must reach the same file by `dev` and `ino`, as core `plugin/oauth-approval.ts` checks around its open: a parent swapped for a link before the open, or a leaf link a Windows runtime followed despite `O_NOFOLLOW`, then reads `linked` or `failed`. Only a swap timed around that second walk escapes, because Node has no per-component `openat`; that remainder is accepted, because the read is bounded, local and only shown to the owner (comment at :120-124).
  - *1 MiB bound.* The installer's largest file holds the schedule prompts (at most 120 kB, core `review/workflow.ts:463-468`) three times: in the header and in two `VECTOR_SCHEDULE_PROMPTS` env values.
  - *Only install lines are read (critique, major, taken).* The plan's regex counted every mention of `@vectordevai/cli` in the file, so owner-written schedule prompts could decide the state (I2). `INSTALL` (:69) matches only an `npm install -g @vectordevai/cli` line, built from @vector's `CLI_PACKAGE` (core `review/workflow.ts:15`) with its regex characters escaped, so a rename there moves the pattern and the tests' fixtures together, as a step's `run:` (optionally a `- run:` list item) or as a line of a `run: |` block, using the desktop's anchor (desktop `main/github-agents.ts:341`), widened to those two forms so a hand-added unpinned install is not missed. The installer writes with `lineWidth: 0` (core `review/workflow.ts:880`), so prompts stay JSON on one line inside the header or an env value and never start such a line. A version-less line reads unpinned. A long `run:` folded by hand before the package name (the installer never folds) is not matched, so a file whose install lines are all folded reads unreadable, never ready; the unreadable case pins that. Comments and other commands that name the CLI (`npx`, `bun add`) are ignored, as the desktop ignores them. The test pins an own-key file at MIN whose schedule prompt names `@vectordevai/cli@latest`, `@vectordevai/cli@1.0.0` and `npm install -g @vectordevai/cli@latest`: it reads ready, "Pinned to <MIN>".
  - *Exact, bounded semver only* (I2): `semver.valid(spec) === spec` and at most 64 characters (:116). So "v1.2.3", "=1.2.3", build metadata, ranges and expressions never reach the row, and repository text never adds words to it.
  - *The header (critique, minor, taken as desktop parity).* The header is optional: the v2 workflow has none and is read by its install lines alone. One that @vector's own `readWorkflowOptions` accepts must name an exact version and agree with the jobs. One it does not accept (broken JSON, or an option a newer CLI adds that this engine's `workflowOptions` refuses) is ignored, as the desktop ignores it (`options && …`), so the jobs decide. The plan's fail-closed rule would have read such a forward-compatible file as unreadable. The lowest version decides "below", which matches the desktop's `cli-too-old` for a prerelease of the minimum, a below header or one below job. Disagreeing versions that are all at least MIN read unreadable, because the file is then not the installer's. `MIN_AGENTS_CLI` is read when the row is built, so a bump from brain-transport needs no change here.
  - *A hyphenated prerelease (critique, minor, taken).* `buildAgentsWorkflow` accepts, for example, `1.99.114-rc-1`, but its own-key review job comes from `buildWorkflowYaml`, whose `cliVersionSpec` (core `review/workflow.ts:34-37`) rejects a hyphen inside the prerelease and falls back to the desktop's `vectorRequiredCliVersion` ("1.99.99" today). The installer's own file then disagrees between jobs. The test builds that file and pins today's outcome, `below` with the fallback version, next to a guard that the fallback is below MIN, so a release fallback that reaches MIN fails the case loudly instead of quietly taking another branch. @vector's builder is not changed.
  - *No catch of its own.* `read()` errors become the `failed` row through `Effect.tryPromise(...).pipe(Effect.orElseSucceed(...))`, so `describe()` has no error channel. Only a defect reaches the hub's `describeSafely` fallback (hub.ts:1824).
  - *The private union is `Workflow`, not `File`* (critique, minor, taken), so it does not shadow the global `File` type.
  - *Review pipeline pin.* `hub.ts` is in `REVIEW_PIPELINE_FILES` (core `test/review/model-evidence.test.ts`). `REVIEW_PIPELINE_SOURCES` is now `a29f830e0e5b3608` (was `935a21d2165f9d29`), computed after prettier had run on hub.ts (critique, minor, taken). `REVIEW_PIPELINE_REVISION` stays 4: the hub change removes a member-list source and does not change what a review reads, finds or posts. This is the same core `review/**` ownership deviation as CN-04 and CN-06 to CN-08. It needs the lead's acceptance and VectorScope's acknowledgement, and VectorScope may still bump the revision. The digest must be recomputed after the pre-push fetch and merge and after any later hub.ts change. A concurrent pin change from scope-finish is recomputed on merge, never merged by hand.
  - *Ownership pre-check.* `git fetch origin brain-transport` succeeded (`a89fe7a0`). Its diff against `0ee486e3` touches no connect file and changes no `MIN_AGENTS_CLI`, `WORKFLOW_FILE`, `CLI_PACKAGE`, header line, install line or `readWorkflowOptions`. The commit gate repeats the fetch. A failed fetch is recorded, never read as "no change".
  - *Critique on scope, taken.* The plan's source-scan case and four of its eight mutations were dropped. The cases kept are pinned, below, unpinned, missing, unreadable, oversized, links, not a regular file, outside Git, Vector Local, registration and hand-off refusal, plus the schedule-prompt and hyphenated-prerelease regressions.
- **Review fixes (round one).** Seven non-blocking findings, none blocking.
  - *Short reads (taken).* `read()` loops until a read returns 0 bytes or the `size + 1` buffer is full, then compares the total with `fstat`'s size, as `directory-request.ts:290-300` does. A network filesystem or a signal no longer reads `failed`.
  - *The hyphenated-prerelease expectation (taken).* The nested ternary that re-derived the rule is replaced by `below(fallback)` plus `expect(semver.lt(fallback, MIN)).toBe(true)`, with a comment to update the case when the fallback moves past MIN.
  - *Hand-folded install lines (taken, comment and case).* The `INSTALL` comment says a folded line is not matched and reads unreadable. A new variant of the unreadable case folds every install line of the installer's file before the package name; a mutation that lets `-g` be followed by any whitespace (`-g\s+`) fails it.
  - *Loop style (kept).* The component check stays an ordered `for` loop with early returns, now in `walk()`, and `read()` keeps `try`/`finally` to close the handle: the order is the check, and the sibling connect readers use the same shape. `walk()`'s comment says so.
  - *The review pipeline pin (kept, as recorded under Decisions).* The one-constant edit to core `review/model-evidence.ts` stays the same ownership deviation as CN-04 and CN-06 to CN-08. These fixes do not touch `hub.ts` or any other `REVIEW_PIPELINE_FILES` entry, and core `model-evidence.test.ts` still passes on `a29f830e0e5b3608`. Before the push: the lead's acceptance and VectorScope's acknowledgement; at the commit gate, after the `git fetch origin connect-finish` merge, re-run that test and repin only if it prints a new digest; never merge a conflicting pin by hand. `REVIEW_PIPELINE_REVISION` stays 4 unless VectorScope decides otherwise.
  - *`CLI_PACKAGE` (taken).* `INSTALL` is a `RegExp` built from `CLI_PACKAGE` with `.*+?^${}()|[]\` escaped, so a rename by @vector cannot leave the pattern behind while the fixtures follow it.
  - *A parent swapped between the walk and the open (taken, partly).* The second walk with the `dev`/`ino` comparison above turns a swap that is still there after the read, or one swapped back, into `linked` or `failed`. `realpath` was not used: on a case-insensitive volume (macOS, Windows) it can return a different spelling than `.github/workflows/vector.yml` and read a correct file as linked. A swap timed around the second walk remains, as the comment says; no test can time that race, so it is untested, and the existing cases show the recheck refuses no correct file.
- **Held, owner-gated.**
  - CW-22's `task` action (the local agent asks to post a `/vector` request, asking each time, with `post` and `network` effects) is not built. It waits for the owner's yes.
  - Whether `connect` is withheld on GitHub Actions is @vector's decision. The registry's `actionsWithheld(id)` (engine `tool/registry.ts:134`) calls `GithubAgents.withheldOnActions` (core `github-agents.ts:72`). Both are untouched. Today `withheldOnActions("connect")` is false, so `connect` is offered on Actions only when `experimentalConnect` is on.
  - Q1 and Q6 are neutral: no route, flag or ledger changed.
- **Held, pre-existing, unscheduled.** As for Hands (CN-11's entry), a refused hand-off to `github-agent` through the owner-authenticated review route is still recorded in the ledger, so it shows as activity on the row. A background one first starts a `connect` job titled "VectorScope review" or "VectorScope fix" with `member: "github-agent"` before admission refuses it. Both are in pinned `hub.ts` and are left. The hand-off case pins that job.
- **Safe by default.** With `VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL_CONNECT` off, the member list is reached only through the flag-gated owner routes, so the adapter is never described. When it is described, it reads one file under the instance worktree: bounded to 1 MiB, regular files only, no link at any component, non-blocking. It makes no network call, starts no process and calls no model, and nothing is read outside Git. There is no `handle` and there are no actions, so the hub refuses every hand-off as `unknown`, "github-agent is only listed; connect cannot hand work to it." (hub.ts:931), before it describes the member or asks anything, whoever asks: the conversation's agent, a person, a workflow stage, a background run or an after-run review. A background hand-off still creates its job row (the held behaviour above). The model's `connect` tool accepts only `to: "vectorscope"`, and `HELPER_MEMBERS` lacks the id. Vector Local reads a ready row `off` (place `your-cloud`).
- **Deferred verification.** The link cases do not run on Windows and the FIFO step runs only on Linux. The `dev`/`ino` recheck runs in every case, Windows included, where only core `oauth-approval.ts` uses the same lstat-against-fstat comparison today. test.yml's engine shards are the authority, and no CI run is claimed.
