## 2026-11-15 — CW-23a: the cloud effect and connect_cloud always-ask (CN-14)

Source: `connect-finish` at `e4978408`. This package changes `packages/schema/src/connect.ts` (safe zone), `packages/schema/src/permission-policy.ts` (one line, the §7.2 `CONNECT_ALWAYS` anchor), `packages/engine/src/agent/agent.ts` (one line, the §7.2 defaults anchor), `packages/core/src/connect/policy.ts`, `packages/engine/src/connect/{adapter,hub,members}.ts` (safe zone; `hub.ts`, `policy.ts` and `agent.ts` are pinned, see Decisions), and one constant in `packages/core/src/review/model-evidence.ts`. Its tests are in `packages/schema/test/{permission-policy,connect}.test.ts`, `packages/core/test/connect/policy.test.ts`, `packages/engine/test/connect/hub.test.ts` and `packages/engine/test/tool/connect.test.ts`. The generators rewrote `packages/sdk/openapi.json`, `packages/sdk/js/src/gen/types.gen.ts` and `packages/sdk/js/src/v2/gen/types.gen.ts`. The spec (status line, §1, §3.2, §6 Codium row, I3) and the handoff's Remaining work are updated. It adds no route, event, flag, adapter or member, and touches no Hands, Codium, cloud-workspaces or VectorScope file. `requiresApproval`'s doc comment (Hands') is untouched.

- **What it closes.** Our side of CW-23: spec §9's CW-23 row (:522, "`connect_cloud` joins `CONNECT_ALWAYS` in permission-policy.ts and gets an `"ask"` default in agent.ts; the hub accepts the `cloud` effect", acceptance "`connect_cloud` asks even under auto-accept and with an allow rule"), §3.2's "Later effects" row (:156, "`connect_cloud` will join `requiresApproval`"), §5's hosted-computer row (:346, "asks `connect_cloud` every time"), review note :664, I3 "Cloud will always ask", I4's "only after a `connect_cloud` ask that names the destination", I10's "An unknown effect is refused", and the CN-01 table row. No review finding covers CW-23; the plan and review-findings files are not edited.
- **Before → after.** At `e4978408` `Connect.Effect` was `read | write`, and the hub refused `cloud` from every member as an unknown effect (`ConnectPolicy.basePermission` had no entry). Now `cloud` is an effect connect knows, but the hub accepts it only in an action whose adapter declared it, with the destination's name, when the hub's layer was built, and asks `connect_cloud` on every hand-off before the adapter starts. No adapter declares one (`ADAPTERS` is vectorscope, hands and github-agent; `grep -n cloud packages/engine/src/connect/adapters/*.ts` finds only github-agent.ts:81 (`place: "your-cloud"`); no adapter sets a `cloud` field), so every `cloud` effect is still refused with `effect` before any question. Nothing a person sees changes.
- **What changed.**
  - *schema `connect.ts`.* `Effect` is `["read", "write", "cloud"]`, and its comment says `cloud` exists, is accepted only from a build-time declaration and is asked through `connect_cloud`; `run`, `network`, `post` and `screen` still arrive with the package that first needs one. New `CloudDestination` (1-200 characters, no control characters, at least one non-space character, and no bidirectional or zero-width character (U+200B-U+200F, U+202A-U+202E, U+2066-U+2069, U+FEFF), identifier `Connect.CloudDestination`): plain words for the service, fixed by Vector code when the adapter is built. `ConsentPermission` gains `connect_cloud`. `Consent` gains `destination: optional(CloudDestination)` and a third filter: a `connect_cloud` consent has an empty `always`, a `destination`, and a message that contains it ("connect_cloud is asked every time, is never saved, and its message names where the code goes."). The two existing filters are unchanged.
  - *schema `permission-policy.ts:9`.* `CONNECT_ALWAYS = new Set(["connect_write", "connect_cloud"])`. Every client already derives its answers from this file: `canAutoApprove` and `canAlwaysApprove` (app, TUI, ACP, `vector run --auto`, the workflow CLI), engine `Permission.ask`, `reply` and `canAllow`.
  - *engine `agent.ts`.* `connect_cloud: "ask"` after `connect_write: "ask"` in the defaults, so the build agent's `*` allow does not reach it. Nothing else in the file changed.
  - *core `connect/policy.ts`.* `BASE_PERMISSION` maps `cloud` to `connect_cloud`. `narrowing` and `admit` take `cloud?: { pattern }`: `cloud` without it refuses with `effect`; with it, an action whose effects leave `cloud` out also refuses with `effect`; and the `connect_cloud` deny check uses that pattern (`<member>.<action>`) instead of the touched paths. `CONSENTS.connect_cloud` is `{ default: "ask", asked: "every-time", saveable: false }`. New `cloudConsent({ member, pattern, destination })`: patterns `[pattern]`, `always: []`, `destination` clipped to 200 (the file's `clip` cuts in UTF-16 units, as the schemas count, and drops a surrogate pair the cut would split), and the message "<member> sends your code or screen to <destination>. It leaves this computer, so connect asks every time." `localGate` takes `effects?` and reads any member with a `cloud` effect `off` under Vector Local, "Off in Vector Local: it sends your code or screen to a Vector cloud service."
  - *engine `connect/adapter.ts`.* `Adapter.cloud?: Readonly<Partial<Record<ActionID, CloudDestination>>>`, documented as Vector code's declaration that the hub reads once. The header and `HandoffContext.ask` say only the hub asks `connect_cloud`.
  - *engine `connect/hub.ts`.* At layer build, `clouds` snapshots each adapter's `cloud` entries that decode as `ActionID` and `CloudDestination`. `admit` looks up `(adapter.id, action.id)` there and builds `cloud = { pattern: "<to>.<action>", destination }` for a declared action only, which both `narrowing` calls receive. After the `connect` ask, `if (cloud)` asks `cloudConsent(...)` with `cloud.destination` through the same `asker`, never deduplicated. `asker` refuses `connect_cloud` with `permission` if `requiresApproval` ever stops covering it (the same guard as `connect_write`), sends it with `always: []`, and adds `destination` to the ask's metadata. The unattended branch already answers only `connect` and `connect_model`; a comment now says `connect_write` and `connect_cloud` are never answered for after-run work. The context's `ask` dies on a `connect_cloud` consent, so an adapter cannot raise it itself.
  - *engine `connect/members.ts`.* `gate` passes the member's action effects to `localGate`, so both the list and admission (hub.ts `ConnectMembers.gate`) read a member with a cloud action `off` under Vector Local.
  - *Generated.* `ConnectEffect` gains `"cloud"` in `openapi.json` and in both `types.gen.ts` (3 lines). `Connect.Consent` and `CloudDestination` are not in the OpenAPI document (`grep -c ConnectConsent packages/sdk/openapi.json` is 0), so `destination` stays internal. The client contract has no connect schema: `bun run check:generated` produced no diff.
- **Decisions.**
  - *"Per request" is every hand-off*, matching §5 :346 ("asks `connect_cloud` every time") and the acceptance line; a person answers each one, as for `connect_write`.
  - *Only the hub asks `connect_cloud`, after `connect` and before the adapter starts.* The adapter's own `context.ask` refuses it (a defect, so the run fails), so a member cannot reword, repeat or time the question.
  - *The build-time declaration decides, never `describe()` alone (critique, major, taken).* `describe()` runs on every request. A declared action whose description leaves `cloud` out is refused with `effect`, fail closed (I10), instead of starting on `read` or `write` alone; an undeclared action that describes `cloud` is refused the same way. The hub asks whenever the snapshot declares a destination. A declaration added to the adapter object after the build changes nothing.
  - *The deny check uses `connect_cloud`'s own pattern (critique, minor, taken).* A pattern-scoped `connect_cloud: { "hosted.review": "deny" }` refuses at narrowing, before `connect` is asked, which keeps the hub header's order. The plan's boolean `cloud` became `cloud: { pattern }` for that.
  - *Vector Local turns off any member with a cloud action, whatever its web setting* (critique (a), recorded). The brief says "when Vector Local blocks the network"; `localGate` already reads `vector-cloud` and `your-cloud` places `off` under any enabled policy, `ask` and `PENDING` included, because code leaving for a Vector service is not a request Vector Local decides per host. Sending code to a Vector cloud service is the same, so this is the stricter reading, on purpose.
  - *The whole member is off, its non-cloud actions included* (critique (b), recorded; the review round's `policy.ts:371` note, kept). The list carries one state per member, and gating per action would show a ready row whose cloud action is refused. Both readings (any enabled policy, the whole member) are stricter than the brief's literal "when Vector Local blocks the network", on purpose, and the first cloud member's package must revisit them (precondition (4) below).
  - *After-run work never sends to a cloud.* An unattended call refuses `connect_cloud` with `permission`, "The automatic review was not run because it needs your approval: …", with no ask.
  - *The member listing is unchanged.* `compose` gates on the described effects; a declared action that describes itself without `cloud` lists by its description and is refused at admission with `effect`.
  - *`destination` and `CloudDestination` are internal.* `Consent` is not public, so only `Connect.Effect` regenerated.
  - *Anchors and ownership.* Only the §7.2 anchors were used in Hands' files (permission-policy.ts:9, agent.ts's defaults). `git fetch origin hands-finish codium-finish connect-finish` succeeded: `origin/hands-finish` `bca15854`, whose diff against `0ee486e3` in permission-policy.ts and agent.ts is empty; `origin/codium-finish` `539cddf5`, whose `packages/engine/src/connect/adapters` holds only `index.ts` and `vectorscope.ts`, with no hosted-computer adapter or `cloud` declaration; `origin/connect-finish` `e4978408`, nothing to merge. Spec §9 asks for "Hands' owner agreeing" and §13 :591 pre-agreed the anchor; Hands' acknowledgement is requested at the PR review. The commit gate repeats these fetches; a failed fetch is recorded, never read as "no change".
  - *Review pipeline pin.* hub.ts, policy.ts and agent.ts are in `REVIEW_PIPELINE_FILES`. `REVIEW_PIPELINE_SOURCES` is now `7bb35e278bedcc0b` (was `a29f830e0e5b3608`), computed after prettier and after the review round's hub.ts and policy.ts edits (the intermediate `508d923ae4fd138f` and `e915b9236ca81c72` were superseded). `REVIEW_PIPELINE_REVISION` stays 4: VectorScope declares no cloud action, and what a review reads, finds or posts is unchanged. This is the same core `review/**` ownership deviation as CN-04, CN-06 to CN-08 and CN-13 (the lead's acceptance, VectorScope's acknowledgement). Repin after the pre-push merge and after any later edit to a pinned file; never merge a conflicting pin by hand. The repin is its own `fix(core): repin review pipeline sources after the CN-14 hub change` commit (as be1eef43 did), or the CN-14 commit's body names `packages/core/src/review/model-evidence.ts`, so VectorScope's owner can acknowledge the touch at PR review.
  - *Critique on coverage and stale text (taken).* The keep-green runs add `background`, `owner`, `vectorscope`, `directory`, `directory-live`, `worktree-target`, `context` and `loop`, which all pass through the hub's admission and the wrapped `context.ask`. The spec's §1 line and I3 no longer say `cloud` waits or "will" ask, and hub.test's `any effect other than read or write is refused with no ask` is now `an unknown or undeclared effect is refused with no ask` (unchanged body: no adapter in that graph declares `cloud`).
  - *Surfaces held (critique, minor, held with preconditions).* The app's `features/connect/consent.ts` `PERMISSIONS` and the TUI's `connect-presentation.ts` `connectedConsent` do not list `connect_cloud`, so today it would show as a generic question, without Always (the clients read `canAlwaysApprove`), and without the destination sentence. Adding it to the two lists alone would title it "Connected review" in the TUI (`routes/session/permission.tsx`, outside the §7.3 safe zones), which is wrong for a cloud hand-off, and no member reaches either surface in this build. So it is held, as a hard precondition (below).
- **Review round (seven non-blocking findings).**
  - *hub.test.ts after-run case (taken).* It also asserts the summary contains the destination, so the refusal is shown to be `connect_cloud`'s ("needs your approval: <cloud consent message>"), not an earlier check.
  - *`localGate` reads the brief strictly (kept, no code change).* Recorded under Decisions, with precondition (4) for the first cloud member.
  - *hub.ts redundant `cloud && destination !== undefined` (taken).* `cloud` now carries `destination`, and `if (cloud)` alone decides.
  - *`clip` could split a surrogate pair (taken, a different remedy).* The suggested code-point clip (`Array.from(value).slice(0, max - 1)`) would let a clipped string exceed `max` in UTF-16 units, which is what `Schema.isMaxLength` and `isLengthBetween` count, so a clipped destination or message could fail its own schema. `clip` keeps cutting in UTF-16 units and drops a trailing high surrogate instead, so the result is at most `max` units and never ends in a lone half. Every `clip` caller in policy.ts gets this; output changes only when a cut would have split a pair.
  - *REVIEW_PIPELINE_SOURCES touch (taken).* Repinned after these edits; see the pin decision for the commit split.
  - *Before -> after grep receipt (taken).* Corrected to what the command prints.
  - *`CloudDestination` accepted blanks (taken, with the optional part).* It now needs a non-space character and refuses bidirectional and zero-width characters; a blank or hidden-character declaration fails the hub's `Schema.is` snapshot, so its action stays undeclared and refused with `effect`. schema connect.test refuses `" "` (alone and as a `Consent` destination whose message trivially contains it) and five hidden characters.
- **Held, owner-gated.**
  - The hosted-computer adapter (connect/adapters/hosted-computer.ts) and its reserved slot are Codium's (spec §6 :398, §5 :346) and need the owner's yes. Any hand-off that sends code or a screen to Vector's cloud, or spends money, is not built.
  - *Hard preconditions for the first member that declares `cloud`* (also in spec §6's Codium row): (1) an app and TUI presentation of `connect_cloud` that shows the destination sentence and a cloud title; (2) a `Connect.SendsTo` form for a Vector cloud service other than `vector-codium`, so its result can say where the code went (I4); (3) the old-client gap closed or the client floor raised: a 1.99.111 client attached to a newer engine has its own `requiresApproval` without `connect_cloud`, so its auto-accept would answer it (its `requiresCloudApproval` covers only `vector_cloud_*` names), the same gap as CW-07 "Left open" (1); (4) a decision on whether Vector Local gates the cloud action alone instead of the whole member, and only when it blocks the network, instead of under any enabled policy (the two stricter readings above).
  - The `run`, `network`, `post` and `screen` effects; CW-22's `task` action; workflow connect stages, which `plan.ts:284` limits to read-only actions, so a `cloud` stage is refused there. Q1 and Q6 stay neutral: no route, flag or ledger changed.
- **Safe by default.** With `VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL_CONNECT` off, `admit` refuses first. With it on, no adapter declares `cloud`, so the snapshot is empty and every `cloud` effect refuses with `effect` before any question, whoever asks. If one were declared: Vector Local reads it off; a deny rule refuses it before anything is asked; after-run work refuses it; every ask reaches a person with `always: []`, so no rule, saved approval or auto-accept answers it and an "always" reply saves nothing; the adapter cannot ask it itself; a description cannot add or drop a declaration. The model's `connect` tool reaches only `vectorscope`. The hub still makes no network call (hub.test's import case stays green). Nothing in this build sends code to a cloud.
- **Deferred verification.** Nothing here is platform-specific. The Linux ReadOnlyGit keep-green files ran through the unprivileged wrapper below; the Linux engine shard of test.yml is the authority for them. No CI run is claimed.
- **Receipts.** Every `bun` command ran through `/home/user/.tools/vector-serial`, one test file at a time from the package directory, with `--timeout 30000`. "Wrapper" is `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/as-ci.sh` (uid 1000, `CI=true`), which the ReadOnlyGit cases need because this container runs as root. Logs are in `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn14/`, the mutation logs in `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn14-mut/`.

## 2026-11-15 — CW-24a: the engine reports a cloud-workspace place from the runtime marker (CN-15)

Source: `connect-finish` at `4bdedc40`, which is `origin/connect-finish` after the fetch below (nothing to merge). This package changes `packages/engine/src/effect/runtime-flags.ts` (the §7.2 anchor: a one-line comment and a four-line entry, as prettier wraps it, after `runBackgroundWaitMs`), `packages/engine/src/connect/{members,hub,adapter}.ts` and `packages/engine/src/connect/adapters/hands.ts` (safe zone; `hub.ts` is pinned, see Decisions), and one constant in `packages/core/src/review/model-evidence.ts`. Its tests are the new `packages/engine/test/connect/place.test.ts`, with its snapshot `packages/engine/test/connect/__snapshots__/place.test.ts.snap`, and one assertion in `packages/engine/test/effect/runtime-flags.test.ts`. The spec (status line, §4.1 :221, §5 :348, §6 :400, §7.2 :433, §8 :474, §9 :524, §13 :595) and the handoff's Remaining work are updated. It adds no route, event, schema field, SDK or generated change, flag default, adapter or member, and touches no cloud-workspaces, Hands-owned or VectorScope `review/**` file except the pin. VectorScope's adapter (`connect/adapters/vectorscope.ts`) is not edited. How `VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL_CONNECT` is read is unchanged. No generator ran: `Connect.Place` already has `vector-cloud` (schema connect.ts:42), and `RuntimeFlags` is not part of any API.

- **What it closes, and what it does not.** CW-24a, worded everywhere as "the engine place and Vector Local gating": spec §9's CW-24 row (:524, "the engine reports its place from the runtime marker"), §5's cloud-workspaces row (:348, "The engine's place comes from the runtime marker"), §6's row (:400), §8 :474, §13 :595, and the CN-01 table row (:264). No review finding covers CW-24; the plan and review-findings files are not edited. It does **not** meet §4.1's "Connect withholds the "local" wording there" (:221) or §13's "so connect does not call a review in a container "local"" (:595): a completed review in a container whose model is on the workspace's own loopback still says "Where the diff went: this computer (local)". That wording is held below, as a precondition for the owner's pilot activation and for the "Connect inside containers" gate. The spec rows say so.
- **workspaces-finish D28 and D31 (critique, major, taken).** `docs/vector/build/cloud-workspaces.md` on `origin/workspaces-finish` `7ecd64a1` (D28 :599, D31 :635, and :606, :625, :662) leaves this file's stale CW-24 rows to the Connect lane. Their lines 347, 399, 473, 523 and 594 are this file's :348, :400, :474, :524 and :595 at `4bdedc40`. All five are now spec rows that name CW-24a (CN-15, built) and CW-24b (CN-19, not built) as this branch's packages, the CW-24b hunk additive in cloud-workspaces' protocol.ts: §5 :348 in both its "On main today" cell (the supervisor, its finite proxy with no connect route, and the marker are in `release-finish`) and its owner cell; §6 :400 in its Where and its "What works before" cells; §8 :474; §9 :524's How cell; §13 :595. This closes D28 and D31 for CN-15's half. CN-19 keeps the proxy half and D34's scope question (cancel), which the lead reconciles before CN-19 edits protocol.ts.
- **Before → after.** At `4bdedc40` every connect member read `local` in a cloud workspace (members.ts hard-codes it, and the hub's fallback at hub.ts:1887 too), and Vector Local let a member in the container run as if it were on the user's computer. Now, when `VECTOR_CLOUD_WORKSPACE_WORKER` is exactly `"1"`, every member whose place is `local` reads `vector-cloud`, in the list and at admission, so Vector Local refuses a hand-off with "Off in Vector Local: it runs on Vector's servers." before anything is asked, and Hands' summary reads "The agent uses apps in this Vector cloud workspace, with your approval." With the marker unset, the list is byte-for-byte what it was (the snapshot below, for Vector Local off and on).
- **What changed.**
  - *engine `effect/runtime-flags.ts`.* `cloudWorkspace: Config.string("VECTOR_CLOUD_WORKSPACE_WORKER").pipe(Config.map((value) => value === "1"), Config.withDefault(false))`, after `runBackgroundWaitMs`, with a one-line comment. `Info` gains `cloudWorkspace: boolean`; every other use builds `Partial<Info>` or reads fields, so no other file changed. The hunk is 5 lines (the brief's "one additive line" is one entry; prettier wraps it).
  - *engine `connect/members.ts`.* `Sources.cloudWorkspace?: boolean` (optional, documented as set only by the hub from the engine's flags). New `located(member, cloudWorkspace)`: a `local` member becomes `vector-cloud` when the flag is set; any other place, and the input object itself when nothing changes, is returned as is, and `sendsTo` is never touched. `compose` applies it to each candidate before `gate`, so Vector Local reads the cloud place. The header and `compose`'s doc say so. The name avoids the hub's `placed`/placement words, which belong to task placement.
  - *engine `connect/hub.ts`.* Admission gates `ConnectMembers.located(member, flags.cloudWorkspace)` instead of `member` (its comment says Vector Local applies at this engine's place), and `members` passes `cloudWorkspace: flags.cloudWorkspace` to `compose`. `flags` is the hub's existing `RuntimeFlags` binding. `Interface["members"]` stays `{ tools?, sessionID? }`. The `describeSafely` fallback member (place `local`) is covered by `compose`.
  - *engine `connect/adapters/hands.ts`.* `Services` and `DEPS` add `RuntimeFlags` (the hub's DEPS already hold `RuntimeFlags.node`; AppNodeBuilder builds it once). The summary is the cloud wording when `flags.cloudWorkspace`; `place` stays `local` (this engine), with a comment that the hub reports it.
  - *engine `connect/adapter.ts`.* `describe`'s doc: a member's `place` is relative to this engine, `local` reads `vector-cloud` in a cloud workspace (`ConnectMembers.located`), and an adapter words its own summary for that place.
- **Decisions.**
  - *One central rule at both exits.* `located` runs where a member leaves the hub: the list (`compose`) and admission. Adapters keep `local` meaning "in this engine", so VectorScope's file, also pinned, is untouched: only VectorScope has `handle`, and the GitHub agent and Hands are refused as unknown before `describe()`.
  - *"Runs" follow their member.* `Connect.Run` has no place field (schema connect.ts:529), and adding one changes the public API, so it is held. A run's place is its member's, because admission gates the located member.
  - *`sendsTo` keeps the model's real destination* (for example `provider:vector-cloud` or `this-computer`), per the brief; no `SendsTo` literal changed.
  - *The marker counts only when it is exactly `"1"`*, as worker/entry.mjs:3 requires; `Config.boolean` would also accept `"true"`, `"yes"` and `"on"`, so it is not used. It is read once with the other flags at layer build, from the same `ConfigProvider.fromEnv()` server.ts:87 uses, and never from a request: the members input has no such field, and place.test's `// @ts-expect-error` call pins that (an unused directive would fail `bun typecheck`).
  - *Vector Local inside a cloud workspace (critique, major, taken: recorded, not called fail closed).* With the marker and Vector Local on, `located` plus `gate` read every ready non-MCP member off, "Off in Vector Local: it runs on Vector's servers.", as the brief's "ConnectPolicy.localGate follows the cloud place" asks. Only connect hand-offs are refused by it (hub admission). For every other member (the agent the user is talking to, subagents, skills, workflows, tasks, browser, `tool:*`, research, codespace, swarm, brain, help, parallel-workspaces, Hands) "off" is the list's label under Vector Local: nothing in connect or LocalMode stops them inside a cloud workspace. At `4bdedc40` "off" in the list matched what was enforced; inside a cloud workspace with Vector Local on it no longer does. Whether Vector Local means anything inside a container is the "Connect inside containers" owner gate (cloud-workspaces.md on workspaces-finish :563, :606, :609), so it is named under Held. MCP servers keep MCP's own status, as before.
  - *Members a person reaches in the app* (review round, taken). A primary agent's session loop runs in this engine, and in a cloud workspace the engine is in the container, so the primary agent (`agent:build`, `reach: "ui"`) reads `vector-cloud` like everything else here, and place.test pins it. Only the desktop's own features (codespace, swarm, help, parallel-workspaces) have an open place question. The central rule relabels them too, place.test leaves their place unpinned (its `DESKTOP` set), and the question is held for the owner below.
  - *Local MCP servers read `vector-cloud`; your-cloud members stay* (the GitHub agent, remote MCP servers).
  - *Hands words its summary from the flag; the hub owns the place.* Hands' availability reasons are Hands' own words in Hands' file and are not edited.
  - *Ownership.* Spec §9 said CW-24 is "done by its owner"; CN-01's lane plan (:264) gives CW-24a to this lane, and the engine side lives in connect's files plus the §7.2 runtime-flags anchor. `git fetch origin connect-finish workspaces-finish hands-finish review-fixes` succeeded: `origin/connect-finish` `4bdedc40` (nothing to merge); `origin/workspaces-finish` `7ecd64a1`, where `git grep -n "VECTOR_CLOUD_WORKSPACE_WORKER\|cloudWorkspace" origin/workspaces-finish -- packages/engine` prints nothing and whose diff from `0ee486e3` touches none of this package's files; `origin/hands-finish` `bca15854`, with no diff from `0ee486e3` in `adapters/hands.ts` or any file here; `origin/review-fixes` `1433d105`, which overlaps only in runtime-flags.ts and touches neither model-evidence.ts nor any of the 59 `REVIEW_PIPELINE_FILES`. The commit gate repeats these fetches; a failed fetch is recorded, never read as "no change".
  - *Review pipeline pin.* hub.ts is in `REVIEW_PIPELINE_FILES`. `REVIEW_PIPELINE_SOURCES` is now `76ac36bb67f9ca9d` (was `7bb35e278bedcc0b`), computed after prettier and with every other edit in place. `REVIEW_PIPELINE_REVISION` stays 4: what a review reads, finds or posts is unchanged; with the marker set only whether Vector Local lets it start changes. CN-01 says VectorScope's core `src/review/**` is not edited; this one-constant touch is the same recorded exception as CN-04, CN-06 to CN-08, CN-13 and CN-14 (the lead's acceptance, VectorScope's acknowledgement at PR review). Repin after the pre-push merge and after any later hub.ts edit; never merge a conflicting pin by hand. At the commit gate the repin goes either in its own `fix(core): repin review pipeline sources after the CN-15 hub change` commit, as `be1eef43` did for CN-08, or in the CN-15 commit with a body that names `packages/core/src/review/model-evidence.ts` and the old and new digests.
  - *PUB-7 merge prediction.* review-fixes' `1433d105` rewrites runtime-flags.ts:46 (`experimentalConnect`) as a comment and `bool("VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL_CONNECT")`; CN-15's hunk starts at :50, with :47 to :49 unchanged between them. `git merge-file` of the working copy, `0ee486e3` and review-fixes' file reports no conflict and keeps both. After the commit, the commit gate re-runs `git merge-tree --write-tree origin/review-fixes HEAD`.

The `/this computer/i` check is narrowed to what it covers (the summaries and reasons connect and its adapters write for the list, with Hands ready and no run). The mixed adapter array is annotated `ConnectAdapter.Factory<ConnectHandsAdapter.Services>[]`, and `bun typecheck` confirms it and the `@ts-expect-error`. runtime-flags.test.ts's all-flags case (umbrella `VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL` on) asserts `cloudWorkspace` false. This agent does not commit (the lane's commit gate does, as for CN-11 to CN-14), so the handoff and status line say "built uncommitted", and the stale "uncommitted" wording of CN-11 to CN-14 now names their commits (`a350a80c`, `21d35b87`, `e4978408`, `4bdedc40`).

Reached only with the marker set and Vector Local off, because the place gate refuses first when it is on: core `connect/frame.ts` `describeSendsTo` ("this computer (local)" :40, "your model server X (local)" :42) and `describeCost` ("no charge (local model)" :53), used by `tool/connect.ts` :314 and :457 and `adapters/vectorscope.ts` :850; policy.ts `place()` (:967) in the `connect_model` consent (:231) and the sendsTo reason (:376); the `connect` permission text (policy.ts:145); the `connect_cloud` consent's "It leaves this computer" (policy.ts:306); the model-facing `tool/connect.txt` ("where the diff went (this computer, …)"); the app's `destinationLabel` "This computer" (app `features/connect/connect-model.ts:65`); `localGate`'s your-cloud reason "not on this computer" (policy.ts:370) under Vector Local; and Hands' own availability reasons, such as `guest`'s "Only the owner of this computer can let Vector use it." (`computer/availability.ts:33`, Hands' file, not edited), which reach the Hands row's reason. Each is a precondition before the owner turns the cloud-workspaces pilot on and before the "Connect inside containers" gate opens. Partly built since 2026-11-27 (entry "CW-24: result wording inside a cloud workspace, unpinned part"): `frame.ts`, `tool/connect.ts`, `tool/connect.txt`, the Tasks pane's `destinationLabel` and Hands' `guest` reason; the policy.ts, vectorscope.ts and hub.ts wording is still held.

The handoff's CN-20 line carries this onto the readiness checklist.

That summary is wording only. Hands needs a loopback desktop bridge, which a container presumably lacks, and nobody has run Hands in one. Whether Hands reports a container-specific reason is for Hands' owner to decide in `computer/availability.ts`, which is Hands' file, not this lane's.
  - A `place` field on `Connect.Run` (public API).

## 2026-11-15 — CW-23d: Playbooks accept read-only connect stages only (CN-16)

Source: `connect-finish` at `ba01e517` (`origin/connect-finish`, clean tree; for these files the same code as `0ee486e3`). This package changes `packages/core/src/playbooks/check.ts` (the schema-failure branch at :126 now returns its issues at once, as codium-finish's copy does, so the nested-workflow check at :127 loses its `else`; one call line after that check, :129; and an unexported `connectIssues` helper at the end of the file, :205-219) and `packages/core/test/playbooks/commands.test.ts` (a `flow` helper and three cases in "the playbook publish check"). The spec (status line, §5 :343, §6 :399, §8 :472, §9 :523, §13 :594) and the handoff's checkpoint are updated (Lane documents, below). It does not change `packages/core/src/playbooks/install.ts`, `script/playbooks-publish.ts`, `api/_lib/playbooks.ts` (the server library), core `orchestration/spec.ts` or `orchestration/plan.ts`, the engine runner, any schema, generated file or SDK, any flag or workflow, or check.ts's header comment (:9-25). No export, type or import is added; `WorkflowSpec` and `Issue` were already in scope.

- **What it closes.** CW-23d, Codium's Playbooks part of CW-23: spec §5's Codium Playbooks row (:343, "the publish check accepts read-only connect stages"), §6's Codium row (:399, "the publish check accepts `connect` stages with read-only actions"), §8 :472 ("CW-23: Remote routes and the Playbooks check"), §9's CW-23 row (:523) and §13's Codium bullet (:594, "Accept read-only connect stages in the Playbooks check"), and deviations :265 (the CN-16 row of the CN-01 table). No review finding or v2-complete-plan row covers it, so neither file is edited.
- **Before → after.** At `ba01e517`, check.ts:125-129 ran `WorkflowSpec.parse(document, { source: "saved" })`, refused nested non-Vector playbooks and returned; a connect stage passed whenever the saved-workflow schema did. A probe through vector-serial (`bun -e`, no file written; `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn16/probe-head.log`, `probe-head-2.log`) passed `{ to: "vectorscope", action: "fix" }` and `action: "review"` with `to` set to `github-agent`, `hands`, `computer`, `VectorScope`, `vectorscope `, ` vectorscope`, `mcp:vectorscope`, `workflow:vector-review`, `cloud-workspace`, `remote` or `tasks`, because `Connect.MemberID` is open text (schema connect.ts:146). Now a connect stage passes only as exactly `to: "vectorscope"`, `action: "review"`; anything else is refused with one issue per stage at `/stages/<i>/connect`: "A Vector Playbook may only review with VectorScope; fixes are separate agent stages." Agent fix stages after the review (`prompt` stages) are unaffected.
- **Decisions.**
  - *Exact literal match,* with no case folding or trimming: `MemberID` is open text, and VectorScope's id is `vectorscope`. `VectorScope` and `vectorscope ` are refused, and mutations pin both.
  - *A static member and action rule.* The publish check has no member manifest to read an action's effects from, so it names the one member and action whose only effect is read: VectorScope's `review` is `["read"]` and its `fix` is `["read", "write"]` (engine connect/adapters/vectorscope.ts:120 and :122). It is the publish-time image of plan.ts's "Workflow connected actions must declare only read effects" (orchestration/plan.ts:274-306). Another read-only member is Codium's line to add later.
  - *An unknown action is refused by the schema, not by this rule (a deviation from the brief's wording).* The brief asks for any other action to be refused "with a plain issue at `/stages/<i>/connect`". An action outside `Connect.ActionID` (`"review" | "fix"`, schema connect.ts:118), such as `deploy`, `task` or `Review`, fails `WorkflowSpec.parse` first, so the new rule never runs. The issues are the union's output: `/stages/1/connect: Unexpected key with value {"to":"vectorscope","action":"deploy"}`, `/stages/1/prompt: Missing key`, the same `/stages/1/connect` line again, `/stages/1/workflow: Missing key`, and `/stages/1/connect/action: Expected Connect.ActionID, got "deploy"`. The test asserts the `/connect/action` issue and that no issue carries the CN-16 message, which pins the order. Cleaning up that union noise is not this package's (spec.ts is outside the Playbooks hunk); it is left as is. The rule's own `action !== "review"` also covers any later `ActionID`, such as CW-22's `task`.
  - *`input` is left to WorkflowSpec,* which already refuses template references in a connected stage's input (spec.ts:207-218).
  - *Issue order:* the text walk (hidden characters, secrets, `!` before a backtick, commands; it returns first, check.ts:124), then the schema, then the nested-workflow check, then the connect rule.
  - *A helper rather than inline code,* mirroring codium-finish's `modelIssues`, so the merge with that branch is one line. A schema failure returns `{ ok: false, issues: workflow.issues }` straight away, which is codium-finish's structure, so the call line needs no `workflow.ok` guard and the nested-workflow check no `else` (review round, below). `issues` is still empty at that point, because the text walk returns first, so the result is the same as before for every playbook whose failed parse carries issues; a failed parse with no issues would now be refused instead of passing. The helper's return type is inferred (AGENTS.md), unlike codium-finish's `modelIssues(...): Issue[]`; the line-level merge is unaffected.
  - *The header comment stays as it is.* codium-finish rewrites check.ts:12-13 to say a playbook "nests only other Vector Playbooks and names a model only as a Codium role". When check.ts is merged, that sentence should gain "and connects only as a VectorScope review". Codium's owner does that, or the lead at merge resolution; it is not edited here, to avoid a second conflict hunk (critique, taken).
  - *Install inherits the rule.* install.ts:33-34 runs the same `PlaybookCheck.validate` on a downloaded playbook before anything is written, so a `fix` connect stage is refused at install with install.ts unchanged. No install-level case is added (the brief puts the tests in commands.test.ts). A workflow saved before this change, or written by hand, is still stopped at run time: plan.ts refuses non-read effects and warns that connected execution is unavailable, and the engine runner refuses every connected stage (engine orchestration/runner.ts:113).
  - *Ownership.* Core `playbooks/**` is Codium's (spec §7.1 :414; deviations :282, Ownership for CN-16 to CN-19). `git fetch origin codium-finish` succeeded at `0d6bfc53`. Its check.ts adds `modelIssues` (Codium roles) and moves `if (!workflow.ok) return { ok: false, issues: workflow.issues }` ahead of the nested-workflow check; it has no connect rule (`git grep -n -i connect origin/codium-finish -- packages/core/src/playbooks packages/core/test/playbooks` prints nothing). It also adds a new `packages/core/test/playbooks/check.test.ts`, "the saved-workflow check", with the model-role and nesting cases, which are the same kind of rule as CN-16. The helper merges cleanly, and so does commands.test.ts (`git merge-file` exit 0 against codium-finish's copy, which adds an import and a re-export case at the top). The commit gate re-runs `git fetch origin codium-finish` and `git merge-tree --write-tree origin/codium-finish HEAD` and records the result.
- **Lane documents.** The spec's status line marks CW-23d built since CN-16 and gives CN-15 its commit (`ba01e517`); §5 :343 names the check as in `release-finish` with the CN-16 rule and splits its owner cell (recipes: Codium's owner; the check's rule: this branch, CN-16); §6 :399 adds the exact rule and replaces "Nothing on main" with the `release-finish` state (Remote is CN-17/CN-18, not built); §8 :472, §9 :523 (with the acceptance "a playbook's `fix` connect stage is refused at publish and install") and §13 :594 say the Playbooks part is built on this branch. The handoff's "Every member is on this base" bullet now says only check.ts has a connect hunk, and Remaining work records CN-16 and leaves CW-23 Remote (CN-17, CN-18) and CW-24's proxy routes (CN-19) to do. This entry and the handoff say "built uncommitted, and the lane's commit gate records its commit", the lane's existing wording, because a commit cannot name its own SHA. The critique asked for CN-09's stale "built uncommitted" in the handoff to become its commit; for the same consistency CN-07 (`7ce17182`), CN-08 (`409f8de3`, pin follow-up `be1eef43`) and CN-15 (`ba01e517`) now name theirs too. Historical deviations entries are left as written.
- **Held.** Connect-stage execution (the private helper-scope capability) stays held at run time, so a playbook may contain the review stage and running it still refuses as today. The "implement, review, fix" recipes and any published playbook are Codium's owner's. Playbooks library activation and Track B are unchanged. Q1 and Q6 stay neutral: no route, ledger or flag default changes.

## 2026-11-15 — CW-23b: Remote lets a paired phone list, cancel and start connect reviews (CN-17)

Source: `connect-finish` at `ac8be435` (`origin/connect-finish`, clean tree). This package changes `packages/desktop/src/main/remote-routes.ts` (Codium's file; every hunk is listed under Ownership), `packages/desktop/src/main/remote-routes.test.ts` (setup gains a capabilities answer and review-start answers, the sorted route list gains five strings, and a new describe "connected tools from a phone (CW-23b)" has 14 cases) and `packages/desktop/src/main/remote-phone-app.test.ts` (the fake engine answers capabilities, runs, the start and cancel, its start can raise a `connect` question and wait until it is answered, and two new end-to-end cases). The spec (status line, §5 :342, §6 :399, §8 :472, §9 :523, §13 :594, :648) and the handoff's checkpoint are updated. It does not change remote-passkey.ts, remote-link.ts, remote-runtime.ts, the phone client or pages (`packages/app/src/phone/**`), the engine, the schema, the SDK, openapi.json, any generated file, flag, workflow or page. No export is added, and `RouteDependencies`, `RemoteTurns` and `ApprovalTarget` are unchanged.

- **What it closes.** CW-23b, Codium's Remote allowlist part of CW-23: spec §5's Codium Remote row (:342, "lists runs and starts a review"), §6's Codium row (:399), §8 :472, §9's CW-23 row (:523, acceptance "The phone starts a review only after a passkey check"), §13's Codium bullet (:594) and the reference row (:648), and deviations :266 (the CN-17 row of the CN-01 table). No review finding or v2-complete-plan row covers it, so neither file is edited.
- **Before → after.** At `ac8be435`, `REMOTE_ROUTES` had 20 entries and no connect route, so `matchRoute` refused every connect path with 403 `REMOTE_ROUTE_REFUSED`. Now it has 25. The five new entries are offered only while the engine's `/experimental/capabilities` reports `connections: true`, and the engine reports that only while `VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL_CONNECT` is on (handlers/experimental.ts:370). With connected tools on, a paired phone may:
  - list the members: `GET /experimental/connect`, with an optional `sessionID`;
  - list a conversation's runs: `GET /experimental/connect/runs`, `sessionID` required;
  - read one run: `GET /experimental/connect/runs/:runID`, `sessionID` required;
  - cancel a run: `POST /experimental/connect/runs/:runID/cancel`, with an empty body and an optional `sessionID` (required since 2026-11-16, entry "connect-finish lane: PR finishing fixes");
  - start one VectorScope review in a conversation: `POST /experimental/session/:sessionID/connect`. The body must be exactly `{to: "vectorscope", action: "review", target, passkey}`, where `target` is `{kind: "uncommitted"}`, `{kind: "staged"}` or `{kind: "branch", base?}`. The start runs only after a passkey check bound to that conversation, folder and target. The engine is sent `{to: "vectorscope", target, background: true}`.

  The phone asks for the start's challenge at the desktop's own `POST /remote/approval`, with the body `{review: {sessionID, target}}`. A permission's challenge still uses `{requestID}`, unchanged. Everything else under connect stays refused before the engine: dismissals, registered findings, the sessionless `/experimental/connect/review`, fixes, budgets, security passes, `since`, a phone-chosen `background`, `task` targets and other members.
- **Decisions.**
  - *The routes (spec drift, recorded).* The spec's §6 row named only the two list reads and the start. The run read and cancel come from the CN-17 brief, and so does cancel's optional `sessionID`. §6 now names all five.
  - *`sessionID` is required on the run list and the run read (critique, taken).* The brief marks it optional only for cancel. With it, the engine returns only the runs that conversation asked for (hub.ts:535-547, `runs` at :183). Without it, the engine would also return the sessionless directory reviews, which the owner starts at the desktop and the phone can neither start nor see in a conversation. A missing `sessionID` is 400 `REMOTE_QUERY_REFUSED` ("The sessionID field is needed."); the mutation "required check dropped" pins it. The member list stays optional because it carries no run data. Cancel stays optional, as the brief says, so a phone that knows a run's id can stop it. Without `sessionID`, a cancel reaches the hub with no scope (owner.ts:341-346, hub.ts:649-655), so it can stop any running connect run in that folder whose id the phone knows, including a sessionless folder review started at the desktop, which the phone can neither start nor list (review round, recorded, and kept as the brief says). A cancel only ends work: it starts nothing and widens no permission. Requiring `sessionID` for cancel too would be one field (`required: ["sessionID"]`) and a deviation from the brief; it is left to the PR review.
  - *The `cr_` rule is in a new `params` check in `handle()`, not in `matchRoute`.* The rule is `cr_` plus 1-61 characters of `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`, i.e. Connect.RunID (schema connect.ts:183) within the id rule. Codium's combined test (`remote-routes-combined.test.ts`, B09) requires `matchRoute(method, path with "id_1")` to find every entry. A non-`cr_` id is therefore matched and then refused with 400 `REMOTE_PATH_REFUSED`, before the engine. The session id rule (`ses` prefix, as SessionID, schema session-id.ts:5) uses the same check on the start path.
  - *The body is reduced.* `to` must be exactly `"vectorscope"` and `action` exactly `"review"`, with no case folding or trimming. The action is checked and not forwarded, because Connect.ReviewInput (schema connect.ts:262-269) has no `action` and openapi.json forbids extra fields. `background: true` is pinned by the desktop, so a phone's start is always the background run (owner.ts:364 defaults to it today), and the engine answers once the review's questions are behind it (hub.ts:153-158). Any other key is refused.
  - *The base rule* is Connect.Ref's: 1-256 characters, not starting with `-`, and no whitespace or control character, the same as `vector connected review --base` (cli/cmd/connected.ts:26-29) plus Ref's length bound. A 7-40 hex commit id passes this rule, and the engine resolves it as a ref or object id (connect/git.ts).
  - *The challenge binding.* A review challenge is issued and verified through the existing `ApprovalTarget`, with `requestID` set to `connect-review:vectorscope:review:` plus the JSON of the reduced target (`reviewKey`). The key contains `:`, which the id rule (`ID`, :28) refuses, so a permission's challenge never starts a review and a review's never answers `/permission/:id/reply`. Both directions are tested. The key also binds the member and action (critique, taken). The mutation that drops them survives, because only one member and action can reach the key at this source; it becomes observable when a second one is added. remote-passkey.ts is unchanged: one phone, one try, two minutes, used up whatever the outcome.
  - *The check order (critique, taken).* For the review challenge: the body, then the phone's credential, then the capability read. For the start: the query (no field is allowed), the folder and `params`, then the body, then the credential, then the passkey verify, saving the new sign count, then the capability read, then the hold, then the engine. For the other four routes: the query, the folder, `params`, the required fields, and for cancel the body, then the capability read. So every refusal except `REMOTE_CONNECT_OFF` is made with no engine call; the tests assert `engineCalls()` for each. Mutations moving the start's capability read before the passkey check, or the challenge's before the credential, are caught.
  - *The capability read is made for each request and fails closed.* It is a loopback `GET /experimental/capabilities?directory=…`. It counts as on only for a 200 answer whose body is an object with `connections === true`. A read error, a non-200 answer, a missing field, `"true"`, `1`, `null` or an array all count as off, 403 `REMOTE_CONNECT_OFF`. Turning connected tools off takes effect at once, including for a challenge issued while they were on.
  - *The hold covers the start's own questions (critique, taken; this replaces the plan's "hold ends with the answer"; reworded and made fail-safe in the review round).* `startReview` marks the conversation in `RemoteTurns` before the engine is asked, unless it is already held. The app's auto-accept (permission-auto-respond.ts, `phoneTurnAllows`) therefore leaves to a person the `connect` and `connect_model` asks the review raises before the engine answers the start; with `background: true` those are the asks before the run detaches (hub.ts:153-158). Those two can be auto-approved; `connect_write` and `connect_cloud` always ask (permission-policy.ts `CONNECT_ALWAYS`). The start never clears the hold itself, whether the engine takes it or not; the conversation's next observed idle releases it through `turns.observe`. owner.ts's `own()` runs the start as the conversation's busy turn and sets it idle right after a background start returns, so in practice the hold ends with that idle. Asks a detached run sends to the requester's session afterwards (hub.ts:803) are not held: after that idle they are treated as those of a review started at the desktop (Known limits). Clearing on success was rejected because it would drop the hold of a phone prompt begun on the same conversation while the start waited. Clearing on failure, as prompt_async's rule does (:255 at `ac8be435`, :382 now), was dropped in the review round for the same reason (Known limits, "A refused start keeps its hold"). Cases cover a refused start and a start the engine never answers (502 `REMOTE_ENGINE_UNAVAILABLE`), each held until its idle; a refused start on a conversation a phone prompt holds (hold kept); a phone prompt that starts while a start waits, for a start that succeeds and one the engine refuses (hold kept both times); and, end to end, a start that waits on its own `connect` ask, which the phone allows once with its passkey while the conversation stays held.
  - *The capability read for connect routes runs before the generic tail, which is otherwise unchanged (critique, taken in part).* The plan's separate `send` for connect routes would have spread the body into the request; it is dropped, and the four non-start routes fall through to Codium's tail at :367-383, which sends cancel's `body: {}` as before. The critique's "for no gain" is declined for one point: a two-line body pre-check is kept before the capability read, so a refused cancel body makes no engine call at all. The mutation "cancel body checked after the capability read" pins it. The tail still computes the body itself, so the pre-check is a duplicate of a pure function call. Computing the body once, by moving the capability read below Codium's body block, was tried in the review round and declined: the moved block sits directly above the line B09 deletes (`const turn = …`, :246 at `ac8be435`), and `git merge-file` against `origin/codium-finish` then conflicts (exit 1, `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn17-fix/merge/`); with the block where it is, it exits 0.
  - *No phone-client change.* The phone reaches these routes through its existing `request()`, and the end-to-end case drives exactly that path. A `startReview` client method, connect tool cards and the pages are CN-18.
- **Ownership.** Desktop main `remote-*.ts` is Codium's (spec §7.1; deviations :282, Ownership for CN-16 to CN-19). `git fetch origin codium-finish` succeeded at `66d0427b`. The desktop main files did not change after `0d6bfc53`, the commit the plan read (`git diff 0d6bfc53 origin/codium-finish -- packages/desktop/src/main/` is empty). Its remote-routes.ts has no connect route: B09 `03266e2e` changes the header at :14, `createRemoteTurns` (where `mark()` returns a restore and `clear()` is removed), prompt_async's tail at :246-255, and adds `recordOf` after `matchRoute`. remote-phone-app.test.ts is untouched there. **CN-17 goes beyond appended entries, which deviates from the brief's "add entries only at the end of `REMOTE_ROUTES`" and from :282's "exact additive hunks".** Every hunk, with its reason:
  - five entries at the end of `REMOTE_ROUTES` (:91-123);
  - a header bullet (:18-23), after Codium's :14 change, with :15-17 between them;
  - three optional fields on `type Route` (:48-53): `params` (the `cr_` rule, kept out of `matchRoute` for the combined test), `required` (the session-scoped reads) and `connect` (the capability gate);
  - two validators (:60-62);
  - three closures between `reply` and `return` (:252-316): `connectionsOff`, `reviewApproval` and `startReview`. The passkey verify and the capability read need `deps`, and a route's `body` function is synchronous and has none;
  - one dispatch line in `/remote/approval` (:329), so that `approval()` stays byte-identical;
  - one block in `handle()` after the permission-reply return (:354-366), so that :367-372 stay unchanged before Codium's :246 hunk;
  - five module helpers and one pattern after `questionBody` (:464-512): `isRecord`, the `REF` pattern, `reviewTarget`, `reviewRequest`, `reviewBody` and `reviewKey`. None is named `recordOf`. After the merge, `isRecord` and Codium's `recordOf` overlap, and folding one into the other is Codium's call.

  This is for Codium's acknowledgement at PR review, as the spec's §6 owner cell now says. Codium's a41 and a42 notes are not edited.
- **Merge prediction.**
  - `git merge-file` of each of the three desktop files against base `0ee486e3` and `origin/codium-finish` `66d0427b` exits 0 (`/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn17/merge/merge-file.log`; re-run after the review round, `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn17-fix/merge/merge-file.log`).
  - Before the review round there was one semantic conflict: B09 removes `RemoteTurns.clear`, which `startReview` called on a refused start. The review round dropped that call (Decisions, the hold), so `startReview` now uses only `has()` and `mark()`, which B09 keeps; B09's `mark()` returns a restore function, and `startReview` ignores it.
  - Run in scratch with the workspace's node_modules (`/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn17-fix/mergerun/run.log`), the merged files with no resolution give remote-routes.test.ts 30 pass, 0 fail (CN-17's 29 plus Codium's memory case), and Codium's full remote-routes-combined.test.ts 13 pass, 0 fail. Desktop typecheck was not run on the merged result.
  - Adopting B09's restore in `startReview` at the merge (`const restore = deps.turns.mark(sessionID)`, and `if (answer.status < 200 || answer.status >= 300) restore()`) is optional. It would release a refused start's own hold at once and still keep a hold another phone turn set meanwhile. In the same scratch it gives 29 pass, 1 fail: the ses_fail/ses_down assertions of "a start the engine refuses or never answers keeps its hold until the conversation is idle", which would then expect no hold for them. The in-flight case passes either way.
  - *Commit gate* (`/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn17-gate/`): `git fetch origin codium-finish` succeeded at `66d0427b`, unchanged since the plan's fetch. `git merge-tree --write-tree --name-only origin/codium-finish` against a temporary commit of this package's tree (made through a scratch index, so the branch and the real index were untouched) auto-merges remote-routes.ts and remote-routes.test.ts with no conflict (`merge-tree-new.log`). It reports four conflicts, the same four it reports against `ac8be435` before this package (`merge-tree-head.log`): `.github/workflows/vector-codium.yml`, `packages/cloud/src/codium-ci-tests.ts` and `packages/cloud/src/workflow-compliance.test.ts` from the lane's earlier `dbfca832` and `aee5584c`, and `packages/core/src/playbooks/check.ts` from CN-16 (`ac8be435`, resolution recorded in its entry). CN-17 adds none.
- **Owner identity and the forwarded refusal (code reading plus receipts; no change).**
  - The desktop reaches the engine as its owner. remote-runtime.ts:37 sends `Basic vector:<engine password>`, and :116-129 set only authorization, accept and content-type. The guest header `x-vector-guest-request` (core free-model-guests.ts:33), which `RequestOwner.forwarded` reads (server request-owner.ts:6-8), is never set, so server auth.ts `identity()` (:86) gives "owner". This is the identity Remote's existing `/permission/:id/reply` already relies on.
  - The desktop engine is the Node sidecar (`utilityProcess.fork(sidecar.js)`, desktop main server.ts:104-106), so the refusal in handlers/connect.ts:20 of a review over Bun's node:http listener does not apply to it.
  - Connect still refuses forwarded owner credentials and scoped guests: packages/server `src/auth.test.ts` gives 17 pass (it includes "every connected-tool route requires the owner for every method", :254), and packages/engine `test/server/httpapi-connect.test.ts` gives 11 pass (it includes "connect routes refuse scoped guests and forwarded owner credentials before any work", :119), on unchanged code.
- **Held.**
  - CN-18 (CW-23c): the phone pages, connect tool cards, a `startReview` client method and the browser spec.
  - Remote's outside security review, any real phone, desktop or relay pairing, and `VECTOR_REMOTE_ENABLED`, which stays the owner's.
  - Q1, the flag default.
  - Q6: the entry follows connect's existing start route, and no route changes. If Q6 moves review starts to VectorScope's route, one entry and the path inside `startReview` move with it.
- **Known limits.**
  - *The shared 30 s request window (critique, taken).* remote-runtime.ts:128 and the phone client's `REQUEST_MS` (client.ts:152) are both 30 s. Until CN-18 the phone has no connect-ask UI. So a phone start whose review raises a `connect` or `connect_model` ask works only if a person answers it within that window, at the desktop or from the phone through `/remote/approval` and the once-only permission reply. Otherwise the request times out, `withRequestAbort` (handlers/connect.ts:14) cancels the review, and the phone sees `REMOTE_ENGINE_UNAVAILABLE`. That fails closed. A longer per-route timeout is Codium's call. The end-to-end case "a review start waits on its own question, which the phone allows once with its passkey" covers a start whose `connect` ask is answered from the phone within the window; the timeout itself is covered only by the unit case where the engine throws.
  - *A missed idle keeps the hold.* remote-link opens its event stream only after the answer is sent (remote-link.ts:437-443), and owner.ts's `own()` sets the conversation idle right after a background start. If that idle event is missed, the hold stays until the conversation's next idle, and more asks go to a person (fail-safe).
  - *A refused start keeps its hold (review round, fail-safe).* A start the engine refuses, or never answers, keeps the conversation held until its next observed idle. `RemoteTurns` at this source cannot tell the start's own mark from one a phone prompt set while the start waited (up to the 30 s window), so releasing it could let that prompt's asks reach auto-accept. A conversation that was already idle stays held until a later turn there ends, so that turn's asks go to a person (fail-safe). The race this closes is the one prompt_async still has between two phone prompts at HEAD; B09's `mark()` restore closes that one at the merge.
  - *Asks after detach are not held (for CN-18).* After the idle that follows a background start, the run's later asks, which go to the requester's session (hub.ts:803), are treated as those of a review started at the desktop: `connect` and `connect_model` may be auto-accepted; `connect_write` and `connect_cloud` always ask. Holding the conversation for the whole detached run would need the run's end in `turns.observe`; CN-18 (connect tool cards) or Codium's B09 helper tracking is where that would go.
  - *Cost.* Each connect request makes one extra loopback read of `/experimental/capabilities`.
- **Safe by default.** Remote is off by default (`VECTOR_REMOTE_ENABLED`), and `VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL_CONNECT` stays off. With the flag off the engine reports `connections: false`, so every connect route and review challenge is refused at the desktop with 403 `REMOTE_CONNECT_OFF`, and the engine's own `enabled` check refuses as well. The change adds allowlist entries and the checks around them only, and touches no engine, schema, generated file, flag, workflow or page.
- **Deferred verification.**
  - vector-codium.yml runs only on pushes to `main`, `codium`, `codium/**`, `codium-legal`, `codium-113` and `codium-113/**`, or on a dispatch (lines 10-19). Its changed-test pick (packages/cloud/src/codium-ci-tests.ts:58) matches `packages/desktop/src/main/remote-*.test.ts`, so it would run these files only there.
  - test.yml runs on pushes to `main` and `v2` and on `pull_request`, so its unit job (`bun turbo test --filter='!@vectordevai/engine'`, test.yml:608) runs desktop's tests only once `connect-finish` has a PR, or after a merge.
  - There is no real phone, relay or desktop run, and the browser spec `remote-phone.spec.ts` is not extended (CN-18).
  - No CI is claimed.

## 2026-11-16 — CW-23c: the phone shows connect cards and runs, and starts a review with a passkey (CN-18)

Source: `connect-finish` at `3f866f30` (`origin/connect-finish`, clean tree). CN-17 is committed in `8387f746`, and nothing under `packages/desktop/src/main` or `packages/app/src/phone` changed after it. This package changes two lane safe-zone files and Codium's phone files (every Codium hunk is listed under Ownership):
- `packages/app/src/features/connect/task-rows.ts`: `goingFirst` is exported and `taskRows` uses it (same order and cap), and `runState` is exported with a narrower parameter type. `connect-model.ts`: `verdictLabel`'s parameter type is widened; its body and its four callers are unchanged. `task-rows.test.ts` gains one case.
- `packages/app/src/phone/remote/model.ts`, `remote/client.ts`, `routes/sessions.tsx`, `routes/session-tasks.tsx`, `routes/remote-parts.tsx`, `routes/approvals.tsx` (one `export`) and `phone.css` (one selector). Tests: `remote/model.test.ts` (one new describe, "connected tools (CW-23c)", 15 cases), `remote/client.test.ts` (one new describe, 4 cases) and `phone.test.ts` (the S6 check also reads session-tasks.tsx, and two new cases).
- `packages/desktop/src/main/remote-phone-app.test.ts`: the phone fixture takes a `requestMs` and counts passkey prompts, and three end-to-end cases are added.

It does not change the engine, the schema, the SDK, openapi.json, any generated file, flag, workflow or page, the desktop main sources (remote-routes.ts, remote-link.ts, remote-passkey.ts, remote-runtime.ts), the phone's api.ts, app.tsx, computer.tsx or status.*, any e2e file, or Codium's a40-a42 notes.

- **What it closes.** CW-23c, Codium's phone part of CW-23: spec §5's Codium Remote row (:342, "The phone sees connect tool cards, answers asks once with a passkey, lists runs and starts a review"), §6's Codium row (:399), §8 :472, §9's CW-23 row (:523), §13's Codium bullet (:594) and a new appendix row after :648, and deviations :267 (the CN-18 row of the CN-01 table). From CN-17's entry it closes the Held item at :1728 (the phone pages, connect tool cards and a `startReview` client method; the browser spec is moved, see Held) and the "no connect-ask UI" part of the Known limit at :1733. No review finding or v2-complete-plan row covers it, so neither file is edited.
- **Before → after.** At `3f866f30` the phone showed a `connect` tool part as the plain line "title · state", reached the connect routes only through a raw `request()`, and described a connected-tools question as "Use connect_model" with its patterns. Now, only while the computer's `/experimental/capabilities` reports `connections: true`:
  - an assistant's `connect` part in the open conversation is a card: title, status and verdict, "Code sent to: …" once ended, the summary of a refused or failed run or the progress of a running one, an open-finding line by severity ("3 open findings: 1 blocking, 1 concern, 1 nit"), and up to five finding lines (severity, title, path and line) as plain text;
  - the session page gains a "Connected tools" section: the conversation's pending `connect`, `connect_model` and `connect_write` questions with Allow once and Deny; a "Review changes" form (uncommitted, staged, or this branch with an optional base) that asks for the passkey tap and posts the restricted start; and the conversation's runs, those still going first, each going run with Cancel;
  - Approvals (and that section) describe the hub's questions with titles ("Hand work to a connected tool", "Send code to a review model", "Change files with a connected tool's fix") and the hub's own sentence, and a fix's question also shows its change as text, each format character in it written as its code point ("[U+202E]").

  With connections off, or for any other part, the old line shows and the section is not mounted. Nothing on the phone offers a fix, a dismissal or a worktree.
- **Decisions.**
  - *Cards show the run the engine answers, never the copy saved in the part (critique, taken; it replaces the plan's card from `state.metadata.connect`).* This is the app's rule at deviations :33 ("Timeline metadata provides only a run pointer…") and connect-model.ts `metadataRunID` ("surfaces fetch the current owned run before showing it"). `partView` reads only the pointer, through `metadataRunID` with the id of the conversation open on the phone (`decodeMessages(body, sessionID)` and `applyMessageEvent` pass it), and only for `tool === "connect"`. The run comes from one `GET /experimental/connect/runs?sessionID=…` read per opened conversation (`useConnectRuns`), which the runs list shares. A pointer whose run is not in that answer keeps the plain line. A part cut off by a stop or a restart still says running in its JSON, while the engine reads it as cancelled (hub.ts `settled()` :225, applied by `stored()` :475); "the card shows the run as the engine answers it, never the copy saved in the part" pins that. The plan's "updates the card live from message.part.updated" case is dropped: an update now causes a re-read (below).
  - *A display projection, failing closed.* `connectRuns` is not a schema decode: the phone bundle carries no Effect schema, and phone.test.ts keeps it free of Node and Bun modules. A run is left out unless its id matches the `cr_` rule, `request.id` is the run's id, `request.requester.sessionID` is the open conversation, the status and action are known, it carries a result exactly when it is done, and the result's state, its verdict when present (critique, taken: `verdict` is optional, schema connect.ts:427) and its `sendsTo` are known. The literal sets are exhaustive `Record<Connect.…, true>` maps, so a literal the schema adds fails typecheck here. `sendsTo` must match schema connect.ts's pattern (:54) within 300 characters, and one that does not drops the run rather than reading "No destination reported", which would understate where the code went (I4). A missing `sendsTo` reads "Code sent to: No destination reported", as task-rows.ts does (critique, taken). *Deviation from the plan:* one finding with an unknown severity or status, or a non-text title or path, drops the whole run, not only that finding, because a shorter list would understate the count and `verdictLabel` reads the findings.
  - *Plain text, one rule (critique, taken).* `plain()` removes `\p{Cf}`, as core connect/frame.ts `escapeUntrusted` does (`FORMAT`, :26), turns each run of control characters and whitespace into one space, trims, and cuts. It never HTML- or entity-encodes, because Solid renders text nodes; the test pins `'Check <img src=x onerror="alert(1)"> & evil line two'` (the test writes its format characters as `\u202e` and `\u200b` escapes, review round). A long `path:line` is cut from the front behind "…", so the file name and line stay (critique, taken). Neither cut splits a surrogate pair: a title's cut steps back over a pair's first half, and a path's steps forward over a pair's second half (review round). The lines are sorted blocking, then concern, then nit, stable within a severity (critique, taken).
  - *Finding titles reach the phone (critique, option two taken).* The brief says "Findings are plain text, never HTML" and asks for a "text escaping" test, so the phone shows up to five titles, where the desktop Tasks pane row shows none (task-rows.ts header). The per-severity line is added as well. Bodies and suggestions never enter the view, which the review case pins by searching the view's JSON.
  - *One order with the Tasks pane (critique, taken).* `connectRunRows` uses `goingFirst` from task-rows.ts, so the list has at most `MAX_TASK_ROWS` rows in all and never cuts a run still going, exactly as task-rows.ts:6 states. The plan's "at most ten done runs" did not match it.
  - *Re-read on this conversation's events (critique, taken; the plan's citation was wrong).* The plan said runs are never re-read on events "keeping A42's rule", but SessionTasks already re-reads on session events (session-tasks.tsx:24-27). Runs are now re-read after this conversation's `session.idle`, `session.status` or `session.error`, a `message.part.updated` of one of its `connect` parts, or a `connect.run.updated` whose `properties.sessionID` is this conversation (which the computer does not stream yet, Known limits). A cut-short part of this conversation also counts, since it arrives without its tool name (remote-link.ts `summary()`, :868, which keeps `sessionID`). Such a read waits 1 s, so the events of that second cost one read, and events that arrive while a read is going ask for one trailing read after its answer, so a chatty run costs about one encrypted round trip a second (review round; it was 250 ms with no spacing). Refresh, a start and a cancel still read at once.
  - *A fix's question shows its change (critique, option a taken).* For `connect_write`, the detail is the hub's sentence, a blank line, then the patch (`connectConsentDiff`, the same text the desktop dock keeps "for an explicit write decision") up to 12,000 characters, then "The rest of this change is shown only in Vector on your computer." when cut. A question with no patch says "The change itself is shown only in Vector on your computer." It lands in Approvals' existing `<pre class="phone-detail">`, a text node that scrolls at 200 px, so approvals.tsx's markup is unchanged. Option a was chosen over a fixed sentence because the phone can allow the question once, and the ask is where a person sees what will change (I3; spec Review notes :663). Each `\p{Cf}` character in the patch (bidirectional controls, zero-width characters, tag characters) is written as its code point, `[U+202E]`, still in text, so a Trojan Source change cannot read on the phone differently from what it applies; the cut is made on the raw patch first, never splits a surrogate pair, and the marking comes after it (review round). This is stricter than the desktop dock, which shows the patch as it is.
  - *The review's own questions can be answered inside the window (critique, option a taken).* A priced or repository-chosen model raises `connect_model` while the start waits (I3), and the start, holding the shared 30 s window (client.ts `REQUEST_MS`, remote-runtime.ts:128), answers only once its questions are behind it. So the section lists the conversation's pending `connect`, `connect_model` and `connect_write` questions: `connectAsks` on one `GET /permission` when the section opens, then `applyConnectAskEvent` over streamed events, with `GET /permission` read again when one of this conversation's asks arrives cut short. Only the hub's asks count (a `cr_` run id and a description, `connectConsentDetails`). Each renders with Approvals' own `PermissionCard`, exported for this (one keyword), so it is Allow once with a passkey or Deny, never "always". phone.test.ts's S6 check now covers session-tasks.tsx. The new end-to-end case "the phone's review start asks for its passkey, then waits on its own question, which the phone allows once" answers the start's own ask through `approve()` while `startReview` waits. A start that still times out says so: `REMOTE_REVIEW_TIMEOUT`, "Your computer did not answer in time. A review still waiting for an approval stops then; answer its approval right away."
  - *The pure gate takes the message (critique, taken).* `connectCard(message, part, runs, connections)` checks connections, the assistant role, the `connect` tool and the pointer; sessions.tsx only calls it, and each refusal is a test.
  - *The request builders.* `reviewTarget` rebuilds `{kind}` or `{kind: "branch", base}` from the person's choice, applies the desktop's `REF` rule to a trimmed base and refuses with "That base is not a branch or commit name." (critique, taken). `reviewChallengeRequest` and `reviewStartRequest` rebuild the target and the passkey's four fields, and the start carries no query and no `background`. `cancelRunRequest` always sends `sessionID`, which closes from the phone's side the sessionless-cancel scope CN-17 recorded; the desktop route still accepts a cancel without one. A cancel answered `200 false` says "That run had already ended." (critique, taken).
  - *`allowOnce` is generalized, not copied.* It now takes the challenge and a `send` callback, and keeps the Safari fresh-tap retry. A new `challenged` helper does the challenge read that `approve` did. `approve` sends the same bytes (`{reply: "once", passkey}` to `/permission/:id/reply`), and `startReview` sends the start. CN-17's cases and "a passkey prompt the browser refuses outside a tap is kept for the next tap" pass unchanged.
  - *The no-fix rule's source (critique, taken).* It is the CN-18 brief and §9 :523's acceptance, which now reads "the phone shows no fix action". I2 ("Data, not instructions", spec :530) holds in that finding text never chooses an action, and the phone has none to choose. phone.test.ts's new case keeps "fix", "dismiss" and "worktree" out of session-tasks.tsx and remote-parts.tsx.
  - *`connect_cloud` is not presented.* Its title and sentence stay held (CN-14, Held). It reads as any unknown permission ("Use connect_cloud" with its patterns), and a test pins that.
  - *Capabilities are read once in `SessionView`.* SessionTasks keeps its own read for `sessionStop`; sharing one read would rewrite Codium's SessionTasks.
- **Ownership.** The phone pages are Codium's (spec §7.1; deviations :282, Ownership for CN-16 to CN-19). `git fetch origin codium-finish` succeeded at `bd3cc7ad`, and `git diff 0ee486e3 origin/codium-finish -- packages/app/src/phone packages/app/src/features/connect packages/desktop/src/main/remote-phone-app.test.ts` has no connect card, `startReview` or connect run code: codium-finish changes only the phone's api.ts, app.tsx, routes/computer.tsx and status.*, plus e2e files. As with CN-17, the hunks go beyond appended lines. For Codium's acknowledgement at PR review, every hunk (new-file line numbers):
  - model.ts: four imports (:1-4); `run?` on the tool `PartView` (:211); `partView` takes the open conversation's id and reads the pointer only for a `connect` part (:215-216, :227, :234); `decodeMessages` and `applyMessageEvent` pass the id on (:257-258, :268, :300); three `TITLES` entries and a comment (:351-354); `permissionView`'s detail takes the hub's sentence first (:368); the new "Connected tools (CW-23c)" section at the end (:472-753).

  - *The shared 30 s window remains.* The inline questions make answering inside it practical, but a person who leaves the page or waits too long still sees `REMOTE_REVIEW_TIMEOUT`, and the desktop's `withRequestAbort` stops the review (deviations :1733). A per-route timeout is Codium's call.

So after the one read that follows the start, its row stays as read (for example "Running") until the person taps Refresh runs, a start or a cancel reads again, or the conversation's turn changes. `connectRunsChanged` already counts `connect.run.updated` for this conversation, so adding that type to `STREAMED` is the whole fix. remote-link.ts is Codium's file, so that line is a follow-up for Codium, not this package.
