## 2026-11-16 — CW-24b: the workspace supervisor allows connect's reads, cancel and a background review start (CN-19)

Source: `connect-finish` at `6cff8020` (`origin/connect-finish`, clean tree; CN-18 is committed there). This package changes one file of cloud-workspaces' (every hunk is listed under Ownership) and two of its test files:
- `packages/cloud-workspaces/src/protocol.ts`: one additive block of 50 lines in `validateEngineCommand`, after the `request` pattern and before the `limit`/`roots` query check. No existing line changes, and no exported signature changes.
- `packages/cloud-workspaces/test/protocol.test.ts`: one new test, "connected tools reach the engine only as a conversation's reads, a run's cancel and a background review (CW-24b)", after the forwarded-turn test.
- `packages/cloud-workspaces/test/service.test.ts`: three fixture lines change and three are added (the changed `requests` element type and the fake `request`'s signature and push keep the workspace id and password of each recorded request; the added `connectOff: false` and a two-line refusal make the fake engine refuse every `/experimental` path with a private detail while it is set), and two new tests follow the first test.

It does not change `service.ts`, `docker.ts`, the gateway (`api/_lib/cloud-workspaces.ts`), the workspace web page, the engine, the schema, the SDK, openapi.json, any generated file, flag, workflow or page, or `docs/vector/build/cloud-workspaces.md`.

- **What it closes.** CW-24b, the proxy half of CW-24: spec §9's CW-24 row (:524, "Connect routes pass the proxy; nothing else does"), §6's cloud-workspaces row (:400, "the supervisor's engine proxy allows the connect GET routes and the review start route", file protocol.ts), §5 (:348), §8 (:474), §13 (:595), the appendix (:650), and the CN-19 row of the CN-01 table (:268, "workspace supervisor allows connect's read, cancel and restricted review routes"). No review finding and no v2-complete-plan row covers CW-24b, so neither of those files is edited.
- **Before → after.** At `6cff8020` `validateEngineCommand` allowed no `/experimental` path: every connect route got the generic 400 `INVALID_WORKSPACE_REQUEST` before ownership was checked. Now it passes exactly five shapes and refuses every other `/experimental` path:
  - `GET /experimental/connect` and `GET /experimental/connect/runs`, each with exactly one query key, `sessionID`, matching the existing `ses_[A-Za-z0-9]{1,80}`, and no body.
  - `GET /experimental/connect/runs/:runID` with no query and no body. The run id is `cr_[A-Za-z0-9]{1,61}`.
  - `POST /experimental/connect/runs/:runID/cancel` with no query, and no body or `{}`.
  - `POST /experimental/session/:sessionID/connect` with no query and a body of exactly `{to: "vectorscope", target, background: true}`. `target` is exactly `{kind: "uncommitted"}`, `{kind: "staged"}`, or `{kind: "branch"}` with an optional `base` matching `^(?!-)[^\s\u0000-\u001f\u007f]{1,256}$`.
  - Still refused: fixes, `POST /experimental/connect/findings`, `.../runs/:runID/dismiss`, the sessionless `POST /experimental/connect/review`, VectorScope's own `POST /experimental/session/:sessionID/review`, every other `/experimental` route (`capabilities`, `session/:id/tasks`, `session/:id/stop`, ...), extra fields, extra or repeated query keys, any method other than the one listed, and a path that reaches one of the five only through dot segments.
  - The supervisor still checks the signature, nonce, closed-account fence and `owned()` (service.ts:114, called at :410 right after `parseCommand` at :409) before `engine()` runs, so a valid shape for another account's workspace answers 404 and reaches no engine. A refused shape answers 400 before ownership, as every refused engine command did before.
- **Decisions.**
  - *Deviation from the brief: the review body is the engine-bound body, with no `action`.* The brief says "the same reduced review-only body as CN-17 (to vectorscope, action review, ...)". CN-17's phone sends `{to, action, target, passkey}` to the desktop, which checks `action` and forwards `{to: "vectorscope", target, background: true}` (remote-routes.ts:278-316, `startReview`, and :498-504, `reviewBody`). The supervisor has no such rebuilding step: service.ts:279 forwards `JSON.stringify(command.body)` as sent, and Connect.ReviewInput (schema connect.ts:262-269) has no `action`. Accepting `action` would send an off-schema field to the engine, or need a hunk outside `validateEngineCommand` (in `parseCommand` or service.ts) to strip it, which this package's scope excludes. The route itself is review-only, so refusing `action` loses nothing. The owner's future workspace page must send exactly this body, and spec §6's cloud-workspaces row now lists every accepted shape as the contract to build against (critique, taken).
  - *`background: true` is required.* service.ts:280 aborts every engine request after 20 s. A foreground review follows its caller (handlers/connect.ts:13-14, `withRequestAbort`), so it would be cancelled at that abort. The engine defaults to background today (connect/owner.ts:364, `input.background ?? true`); requiring it pins that whatever the default becomes. CN-17's desktop always sends `background: true` for the same reason.
  - *`sessionID` is required on both list reads,* as the brief says. CN-17 leaves it optional on the member list, so this is narrower than CN-17 there.
  - *No query on the run read or cancel,* as the brief says ("add sessionID for these two paths only"; "still refused: extra ... queries"). This is a cost, not only a visibility choice (critique, major, taken): without `sessionID`, the engine's `ConnectHub.get` (connect/hub.ts:535-547) finds a run only in the in-memory live ledger, and otherwise falls back to `directoryRuns`, which returns only sessionless directory runs. The ledger keeps `LEDGER_SIZE` (50, hub.ts:82) runs and drops finished ones first (`remember`, hub.ts:1806-1812), and it is emptied when the engine restarts, which every workspace pause and start does. So the run read works only for a running review or a recent one; see Known limits. Cancel is unaffected: `ConnectHub.cancel` acts only on a live, unfinished entry with or without a session (hub.ts:649-656). The CN-17 brief required `sessionID` on the run read for exactly this reason. This package does not widen the brief. Open for the lead: allow an optional, single `sessionID` with the `ses_` pattern on the run read (and on cancel, as CN-17 does), or keep it as is. Either is four lines in this block plus its cases.
  - *Id patterns.* Run ids are `cr_` plus 1-61 letters or digits: Connect.RunID's 4-64 length (schema connect.ts:183) in the supervisor's alphanumeric style. Real ids are `cr_` plus 26 characters from `ascending()` (schema identifier.ts), letters and digits only. Session ids use the existing `ses_[A-Za-z0-9]{1,80}`.
  - *The base rule* is Connect.Ref's (schema connect.ts:213-217), the same as CN-17's `REF` (remote-routes.ts:470). The engine resolves the base only inside ReadOnlyGit (CN-03 to CN-05).
  - *Dot segments are refused under `/experimental/` (critique, taken).* The block matches on `url.pathname`, which service.ts also forwards (:269-275), so `.../runs/cr_x/../../review` and `/session/../experimental/connect/review` already read as the refused sessionless route. The block also refuses any `/experimental/` path whose raw path before `?` differs from the normalized one, so `/experimental/connect/runs/cr_a/../cr_b`, `/experimental/session/ses_a/../ses_b/connect` and `/experimental/connect/./runs` are refused too. This keeps the new routes off the raw-versus-normalized pattern noted for the owner below.
  - *Placement.* The block returns before the `limit`/`roots` check (protocol.ts:242 after the edit), which would otherwise refuse `sessionID`. Mutation M16 pins the placement.
- **D34 reconciliation (critique, taken).** `docs/vector/build/cloud-workspaces.md` on `origin/workspaces-finish` (`098b5a1c`) records D34 (:646): only the CW-24 Connect routes are added ("the three GETs and the session review start"), and cancel stays refused until "an explicit decision widens the allowlist and this invariant together"; this file's CN-15 entry (:1586, :1612) says the lead reconciles D34 before CN-19 edits protocol.ts. The CN-19 brief, issued by the lead on 2026-11-16, allows `POST /experimental/connect/runs/:runID/cancel` with an empty body. This package reads the brief as that explicit decision, but the brief is not a recorded sign-off on D34 itself (review round), so the D34 resolution must go on record before the PR merges: the PR description asks the lead or the cloud-workspaces owner to confirm at PR review that D34's invariant gains a run's cancel, and the handoff's merge line tracks it until then. HEAD's cloud-workspaces.md has no D34, and workspaces-finish rewrites that file (+494 lines), so this lane does not edit it. When workspaces-finish meets this branch, two sentences are for the cloud-workspaces owner or the lead: D34's invariant gains a run's cancel, and cloud-workspaces.md:33 ("Engine routes exposed to the browser are a finite session, message, status, model and approval API") names the connect routes. The handoff's merge line records both. Fallback: if the PR review drops cancel, delete the cancel branch (4 lines) and its cases.

Its protocol.ts diff from HEAD touches only `CloudWorkspace.usage` and `parsePublicWorkspace`, and its test hunks are a new usage test in protocol.test.ts and an import plus two EOF tests in service.test.ts. The other three branches touch neither `packages/cloud-workspaces` nor the three lane documents since `0ee486e3`. This package's hunks: protocol.ts, one additive block (the `run` pattern, a comment and the `/experimental/` branch); protocol.test.ts, one new test; service.test.ts, three changed fixture lines (`requests`' element type, and the fake `request`'s signature and push, which now record `id` and `password`), three added ones (`connectOff: false`, and the two-line refusal of `/experimental` while `connectOff` is set) and two new tests. They are for the cloud-workspaces owner's acknowledgement at PR review.
- **For the cloud-workspaces owner (not fixed here).** service.ts reads the raw `command.path` in places while `validateEngineCommand` and the forwarded request use the normalized path. Example: for `/session/ses_A/../ses_B/prompt_async`, `parseCommand` accepts the command, service.ts:220 takes `ses_A` as the session and checks the message identity against it (:231), and :269-275 forwards to `/session/ses_B/prompt_async` (probe `/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn19/dotprobe.ts`, log `dotprobe.log`; the probe calls only `parseCommand` and the same regex and URL calls, with no service). It is the same account's own workspace and sessions, and it predates this package. So that it is not lost when this log is read only for Connect, the PR description raises it as an item for the cloud-workspaces owner (review round).

- **Held.**
  - The "publish screen shows the last verdict" web page (spec §6), and any workspace page that sends the start: the owner's.
  - Pilot activation and any real supervisor host or container. Only the fake runtime is used.
  - The "Connect inside containers" gate and CN-15's held wording of a result, a consent and a permission inside a container: preconditions before the owner turns the pilot on.
  - Q1 (the default switch). Q6: if review starts move to VectorScope's route and ledger, this allowlist moves with them.
- **Known limits.**
  - *The run read is only as durable as the engine's live ledger.* It works for a running review, or a recent one before eviction or a pause and start; afterwards the engine answers `not_found` and the supervisor gives its generic 409. Durable reads go through `GET /experimental/connect/runs?sessionID=...`, which reads the conversation's stored history. The lead's open choice is under Decisions.
  - *A start is usable only once a page answers its asks within 20 s.* The engine answers a background start only after the review's `connect` and `connect_model` asks are answered (the comments at remote-routes.ts:303-306 and :312), and service.ts:280 aborts the request after 20 s, which cancels the review: it fails closed. The workspace console already loads `/permission` and `/question` every 5 s (20 s while hidden; CloudWorkspaces.tsx:265-266, :279) and renders any pending permission of the open conversation generically as "Allow <name>?" with Allow once (:1026-1055) through the already-allowed reply route. So the critique's "not shown to render connect questions at all" is not quite right, but the conclusion stands: no page sends the start today, and both asks would have to be answered inside the window. A longer per-route timeout and a connect-aware page are the owner's, in service.ts and the web page.
  - *Branch reviews have almost nothing to review in a workspace.* The repository is a depth-1 fetch of one ref, checked out detached (docker.ts:93-94). A base such as `main` does not exist as a local branch, `origin/main` resolves to HEAD itself, `HEAD~2` never exists, and origin/HEAD, which a branch review without a base reads (CN-05), is not set. So only uncommitted and staged reviews are meaningful there until the cloud-workspaces owner changes the clone depth (read from code, not run). The validator still accepts every Connect.Ref so that a deeper clone needs no change here.
  - *Engine refusals collapse into the generic 409* (`ENGINE_REQUEST_FAILED`, service.ts:283-289), as for every engine route. The service test pins that the engine's detail never reaches the caller.
  - *Spend.* A review's model calls go through the workspace's model relay, so they count against its lifetime allowance (`store.reserveModel`, service.ts:545) and its single in-flight slot (a 429 `MODEL_BUSY` against a chat turn, :514-515, :534-535). A blocked model guard refuses them (:508-513), and a review cannot clear it, because only a new `prompt_async` turn resets the guard (:263).
  - *Rate limits.* The gateway counts a start or a cancel as a write (60 a minute, api/_lib/cloud-workspaces.ts:113-121), not under the 12-a-minute prompt limit, which matches only `/prompt_async` (:123-130). The two lists and the run read count as reads (240 a minute).
- **Safe by default.**
  - The gateway refuses before `parseCommand` unless `VECTOR_WORKSPACES_ENABLED` is `"true"` and the account is on `VECTOR_WORKSPACES_ACCOUNTS` (api/_lib/cloud-workspaces.ts:99-111), so nothing new is reachable on vectordev.ai.
  - The container's environment (docker.ts:633-652, the `--env` flags) sets neither `VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL_CONNECT` nor `VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL` (`grep -n VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL` over the package's src, worker and container finds none). Since PUB-7 (`0aa77586`, on this branch since `3f866f30` merged PR #33's `0f034957`), engine runtime-flags.ts:46-47 reads only `VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL_CONNECT` (`bool`), and the umbrella no longer turns Connect on. So every connect route in a workspace answers `disabled`, which the supervisor turns into its generic 409. The spec status line and the handoff's Shipping state said the umbrella still enabled Connect; both are corrected here (critique, taken).
  - Signature, nonce, the closed-account fence and `owned()` run before the engine, and the supervisor's POST fence for a retiring engine (service.ts:201-206) also stops a review start; the service test pins it.
- **Deferred verification.** No real container, supervisor host or browser run was used. The package's `runtime-smoke.ts` (the owner's) does not call connect, and `vector-workspaces.yml`'s Docker smoke and browser steps run only in CI. CI: not run. The package is uncommitted; the lane's commit gate dispatches `vector-workspaces.yml` on `connect-finish` (its trigger is `workflow_dispatch` plus `pull_request` paths, and test.yml does not run this package) and records the run.

## 2026-11-16 — CW-25 prep: release readiness, a draft docs page and owner actions, with Connect pinned off (CN-20)

Source: `connect-finish` at `34cf53cd` (equal to `origin/connect-finish` after `git fetch`, clean tree; CN-19 is committed there). This package is left uncommitted in the working tree, as its brief says; the lane's commit gate records its commit. It changes:
- `docs/vector/build/connected-workspace-release.md` (new): the readiness checklist with evidence (§1), the draft text of the public docs page (§2), the docs-nav line and page path, not applied (§3), and the two flag default lines, marked owner-only (§4).
- `docs/vector/owner-actions/connected-tools.md` (new): the owner's steps for Q1, Q6, CW-22's `task`, publishing the page, the hosted-computer hand-off and Remote's outside security review, plus the cloud-workspace question CN-15 left.
- `packages/engine/test/effect/runtime-flags.test.ts`: one new case, "experimentalConnect is off when VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL_CONNECT is unset or false in the environment" (renamed in review round one), after "turns WebSockets off via a dedicated variable".
- `packages/engine/test/server/httpapi-connect.test.ts`: the `provider` literal becomes a shared `settings` object plus the Connect-on provider (same values); a new `dormant` harness, the production assembly without the flag; a positive control in the existing production case (all three adapters are listed while Connect is on); and one new case, "with VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL_CONNECT unset, connections are off and no connected tool is listed". One named import is added (`Hands` from `@vectordevai/schema/hands`).
- `packages/engine/test/connect/owner.test.ts`: one new case on the existing `off` graph, "with Connect off every owner read and action refuses as disabled" (widened and renamed in review round one), and one named import (`SessionID` from `@/session/schema`).
- The handoff (a new "connect-finish result, 2026-11-16" section and in-place fixes), the spec (status line and §9's CW-25 row) and this entry.

It changes no source file, flag, route, schema, generated file, workflow, `packages/web` file, Terms, Privacy, consent or marketing text, and it does not edit v2-complete-plan.md or the review-findings files.

- **What it closes.** CW-25's prep: spec §9's CW-25 row ("a docs page and a docs-nav line; owner-actions note", minus the flip), the CN-20 row of the CN-01 table, and the handoff's CN-20 line. It closes no review finding.
- **What it does not do.** It does not flip the default (Q1), publish the page or the nav line, write release notes, or decide any owner question; the docs list options only.
- **Before → after.** Before, the default-off state was pinned only by core `test/connect/flag.test.ts` and engine `test/connect/hub.test.ts`:1216, both over the flag reader alone, and nothing pinned what the HTTP API shows with the flag unset. After:
  - the engine flag is pinned through the provider the server uses (`ConfigProvider.fromEnv`): off over an empty environment and with `"false"`, on with `"true"`;
  - the real HTTP assembly with the flag unset answers `/experimental/capabilities` with `connections: false`, and answers `GET /experimental/connect`, the same with a `sessionID` that names no conversation, `GET /experimental/connect/runs` and `GET /experimental/connect/runs/cr_fixture` with 400 and exactly `{code: "disabled", message: "Connected tools are off in this build."}`, with no question asked and no model call;
  - every `ConnectOwner` read and action (`members`, `runs`, `get`, `cancel`, `review`, `directoryReview`, `register`, `dismiss`) fails with that code before any session lookup, input check or ledger write.
- **Decisions.**
  - *Deviation from the brief: the hub-or-owner case is an HTTP case, plus an owner case.* The brief asks for "one hub or owner case to an existing connect test" showing the capability false and no adapter listed. `ConnectHub.members` (`connect/hub.ts`:1715-1756) lists every adapter whatever the flag says, by design (`hub.test.ts`, the members cases); the gates are `ConnectOwner.enabled` (`connect/owner.ts`:110-114, run first by `members`, `runs` and `get` at :321-336) and the capability `connections: flags.experimentalConnect` (`handlers/experimental.ts`:370), which the desktop card, the Tasks pane rows, the directory review and the terminal command read. Only the real HTTP assembly shows the capability and the owner gate together, so the main case is in connect's own HTTP test, `test/server/httpapi-connect.test.ts`, whose production harness already builds the real hub and adapters. The owner case in `test/connect/owner.test.ts` meets the brief's wording too, but it cannot see the capability.
  - *The positive control.* The existing production case now also asserts that `vectorscope`, `computer` (`Hands.ID`) and `github-agent` are listed while Connect is on, so the dormant case's refusal is not vacuous. Hands is listed whenever Connect is on (`connect/adapters/hands.ts`, through members.ts `registered`).
  - *The dormant case uses the node transport.* The fixture's web transport builds its routes over the Connect-on provider, so only the node server reflects the harness's own provider. No start route is asserted: under Bun's node:http, `withRequestAbort` (`handlers/connect.ts`:20-24) answers `disabled` for another reason.
  - *Only the explicit key is asserted* in the runtime-flags case, as the brief says; the umbrella `VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL` is pinned by `hub.test.ts`:1216 and core `test/connect/flag.test.ts` (PUB-7). The all-flags case at :14, which sets the umbrella, is unchanged.
  - *No replacement lines for the flag default (critique, taken).* The plan's two candidate lines would have disagreed (core `=== undefined || truthy(...)` against engine `Config.boolean(...).pipe(Config.withDefault(true))`). A probe (`/tmp/vector-v2/connect/cn20/probe/flag-values.ts`) shows the readers already disagree today: `yes`, `on` and `y` are on in the engine and off in core; `""`, `TRUE`, `True` and `enabled` fail the engine's flag layer while core reads `TRUE` and `True` as on. The release checklist (§1.1 B8(c), §4) gives the exact lines and current text, the measured matrix, and the requirement that the owner's commit give one answer per value in both readers and pin it in both tests. Choosing the new reading is the owner's.
  - *The draft page stays text in docs/vector/build* (PUB-4 precedent): no `packages/web` file. §3 names the repository's existing mechanism for unreleased pages, an unrouted draft in `packages/web/src/drafts/docs/` (Hands' `vector-hands.astro` and `test/docs-hands.test.ts`), and says the routed page and its docs-nav entry land in one commit because `DocsPage.astro`:24 throws for an id with no entry (critique, taken).
  - *The draft text was checked against the code (critique, taken).* The settings defaults come from core `v1/config/connect.ts` `Defaults` (`after_run` off, `before_commit` offer, `max_rounds` 1, `max_cost_usd` 2). The settings page is labelled "Agents" (`settings-registry.ts`), not "Subagents". A project opened through a link works at its real folder since CN-06, so the limit names the exact unavailable reason instead. The base sentence follows `connect/git.ts`:83-90 (HEAD or 7 to 40 hex digits resolve as commit ids, anything else as a name) and core's refusals (a short id that is also a ref name, and a hex-shaped name of 4 to 39 digits with no ref). ## 2026-11-16 — connect-finish lane: phase-review fixes

Source: `connect-finish` at `c60aa2d3` (CN-20), equal to `origin/connect-finish` after `git fetch`, clean tree. A phase review of CN-01 to CN-20 gave thirteen findings, all non-blocking; two of them are the same stale CN-20 note. The change was left uncommitted in the working tree, as its brief says, and the phase-review commit gate committed it as `fix(connect): connect-finish lane review`. It changes:
- `packages/app/src/features/connect/task-rows.ts`, `task-rows-section.tsx` and `task-rows.test.ts` (the Tasks pane finding);
- comments, and one test case each, in `packages/cloud-workspaces/src/protocol.ts` and `test/protocol.test.ts`, `packages/desktop/src/main/remote-routes.ts` and `remote-routes.test.ts`, and `packages/app/src/phone/remote/client.ts`, `client.test.ts`, `model.ts` and `model.test.ts` (the two copy findings);
- the release checklist (Source line, B8, the B6 line under it, the CI paragraph, §1.5), the handoff (the CN-20 row, the CW-25 bullet, the lead's PR items), and in this log the CN-01 ownership paragraph, the CN-07 entry and the CN-12 entry, each with a dated pointer to this entry.

It changes no route, no allowlist rule, schema, generated file, flag, workflow or `packages/web` file, and no review pipeline source, so `REVIEW_PIPELINE_SOURCES` stays `76ac36bb67f9ca9d`.

- **Taken, with a code change.**
  - *The Tasks pane treats a dropped read as a refusal (task-rows-section.tsx:75; CN-12).* Confirmed against the code at `c60aa2d3`: `readTaskRows` turned every runs error into `undefined` (`.catch(() => undefined)`, task-rows.ts:65), and the section then blocked its key, so one timed-out or dropped read hid the conversation's runs, running ones with Cancel included, until a reconnect, an authority change or another root. `readTaskRows` now answers `"refused"` only for a 4xx, read from the `status` the SDK keeps under `cause` for a `throwOnError` call (`packages/sdk/js/src/error-interceptor.ts`), and `"failed"` for anything else: a network error, an abort or a timeout (none has a status), a 5xx, or an answer that does not decode. The section blocks the key only on `"refused"` (and on `undefined`, which a set key never gets). On `"failed"` it keeps that key's last successful rows, or none, and blocks nothing, so the next `connect.run.updated` event, a reopened pane or an authority change reads again. Nothing polls, and rows still come only from a read for the current key, so an authority change still clears them. A guest's 403 still blocks, which `connected-tools.spec.ts`:549 asserts. The CN-12 entry carries a pointer.
  - *Four hand-written copies of the run-id and branch-base rules (protocol.ts:192, remote-routes.ts:469).* Confirmed: schema `Connect.Ref`, desktop `REF`, the phone's `REF` (client.ts) and an inline pattern in protocol.ts share the base rule; desktop `isRun` and the phone's `RUN_ID` allow `cr_[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,61}`, the supervisor only `cr_[A-Za-z0-9]{1,61}`, and schema `Connect.RunID` any characters after `cr_`, 4 to 64 long. For the run id the finding offered two forms, and this takes the second: protocol.ts keeps its narrower rule and its comment now names the reason, `RunID.create()` appending `ascending()`'s letters and digits only (schema identifier.ts). Widening it would loosen a fail-closed allowlist in the cloud-workspaces owner's file, which CN-19's own case pins (`cr_a-b` refused), for no id the engine makes. Each copy now names the copies it mirrors. Importing the schema's pattern was not taken: no exported regex exists, and using the schema at run time would add effect's Schema to the phone's Remote client and widen the owners' hunks. Instead each package gains one case over the same inputs (26 bases and 9 run ids), each checked against the schema rather than against another copy. The inputs include astral characters: `"\u{1f642}".repeat(128)` is 256 UTF-16 code units and passes, `.repeat(129)` does not, so a copy that gained the `u` flag and counted code points would disagree with `Connect.Ref`'s length check. All four copies agree today. The cases:
    - cloud-workspaces `test/protocol.test.ts`, "the connect checks agree with Connect.Ref and admit every Connect.RunID the engine makes": a base passes the review start exactly when `Connect.Ref.makeOption` accepts it; 50 fresh `RunID.create()` ids and the two admitted fixtures pass the run read and the cancel, and each is a `Connect.RunID`; the other seven ids are refused;
    - desktop `remote-routes.test.ts`, "the run and base checks agree with Connect.RunID and Connect.Ref, and admit every id the engine makes": the same bases through the review start (with no passkey answer a start stops at its approval, so only a refused base answers `REMOTE_BODY_REFUSED`), and the same ids through the run read, where the four `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` fixtures and 50 created ids pass and each is a `Connect.RunID`;
    - phone `client.test.ts`, "a base passes exactly when Connect.Ref accepts it" (`reviewTarget`, which trims, so a base it would trim is not sent as written), and `model.test.ts`, "a run is kept for every id the engine makes, and only for a Connect.RunID" (`connectRuns`).
- **Taken, documents only.**
  - *VectorScope's files (checklist §1.5; CN-01 ownership paragraph; model-evidence.ts:62).* Both claims that no VectorScope `src/review/**` file was edited now name the one exception: the `REVIEW_PIPELINE_SOURCES` digest constant in core `review/model-evidence.ts`, which `8e547175`, `539ec394`, `7ce17182`, `be1eef43`, `e4978408`, `4bdedc40` and `ba01e517` repinned because engine `connect/hub.ts`, `connect/adapters/vectorscope.ts` and `agent/agent.ts` and core `connect/policy.ts` and `read-only-git.ts` are pinned pipeline sources. `git diff 0ee486e3 HEAD` under engine and core `src/review/` shows that one line only. §1.5 now asks for what the finding names: the PR description lists every repin, VectorScope's owner acknowledges them and decides whether `REVIEW_PIPELINE_REVISION` goes up before merge, and the digest is recomputed after the lead merges `main`, never resolved by hand. ## 2026-11-16 — connect-finish lane: correction to the hub.test.ts entry above

- **What the entry above got wrong.** The entry above and the message of `549b550b` say `fix.test.ts` and then `hub.test.ts` ran "in the order CI ran them". They did not. `/tmp/vector-v2/connect-finish/pair-fix-hub.log` prints `test/connect/hub.test.ts:` at line 3 and `test/connect/fix.test.ts:` at line 103, so `hub.test.ts` loaded first. `/tmp/vector-v2/connect-finish/pair-sandbox-hub.log` kept only the summary (56 pass, 0 fail, 2 files), so the order of the `github.sandbox-build.test.ts` pair is unknown. Neither pair run is known to have loaded a shard-2 file ahead of `hub.test.ts`. So neither run rules out any file that comes before `hub.test.ts` in shard 2. The two bullets show only that each pair passes in one process, in that run's order.
- **Why the order was not the argument order.** Bun 1.3.14 does not run test files named on the command line in argument order. A probe in `/tmp/vector-v2/connect-finish/order-probe` used three trivial files that log when they load. `bun test test/connect/fix.test.ts test/connect/hub.test.ts` and the reverse both loaded hub, then fix. `bun test hub.test.ts alpha.test.ts fix.test.ts` loaded alpha, hub, fix. Bun treats bare arguments as path filters over its own file scan. Paths starting with `./` kept argument order in the probe (`./hub`, `./alpha`, `./fix` loaded in that order, and `./fix ./hub` loaded fix first). Any bisect should read the load order from its log, not take it from the command line.
- **Where the suspects sit in CI.** The shard-2 log of job 133268762868 lists 166 files. `test/cli/github.sandbox-build.test.ts` is 18th, `test/compliance/runtime-build-config.test.ts` is 44th, `test/connect/fix.test.ts` is 56th and `test/connect/hub.test.ts` is 57th. The error is at line 2084, column 9 (`expect`, the line after the comment at 2083).
- **Root cause not established.** `a320bdbe..HEAD` changes no file under `packages/engine`. So `bun test --shard=2/4` still holds the same 166 files, with `hub.test.ts` 57th after the same 56, and the next push is expected to fail "engine (linux, shard 2/4)" the same way. `hub.test.ts` was not edited or moved. The bisect is the lead's decision: either a local exemption to run shard 2's 56-file prefix plus `hub.test.ts` in one Bun process, halving the prefix each time, or a CI diagnostic run that does the same. Check these two first:
  - `test/cli/github.sandbox-build.test.ts`: an in-process `Bun.build` with plugins (`wrapper.plugin`, `sandboxBunEsprima`).
  - `test/compliance/runtime-build-config.test.ts`: a `Bun.Transpiler` with the `import.meta.env.VITE_VECTOR_CHANNEL` define, against the `import.meta.dir` at `hub.test.ts:2077`.
- **e2e (linux) margin under the 45-minute budget.** In job 133268762928, 388 tests finished in 1,798 s, counted from the first test's start: a 4.63 s mean, or 4.66 s counted from the step's start. At that mean the suite's 477 tests project to about 37 minutes, which leaves 45 minutes about 22% over the projection. The workflow comment's "about 35%" comes from a 4.19 s mean. This lane's specs are not what is slow. Measured from one test's start to the next in the log, the `connected-tools.spec.ts` and `directory-review.spec.ts` tests took 4.5-12.5 s each. `cloud-home-only.spec.ts` took 37.1 s, and twelve tests from other lanes took 9.9-23.5 s. No workflow change was made here. Watch the margin on the next Linux e2e run.
  *2026-11-21 (v2-closeout-ci, connect-finish lane: e2e (linux) margin follow-up):* watched on two later Linux runs, both read through GitHub MCP. Main `f3d7ac74`, test.yml run 44848275007, e2e (linux) job 136673698208: "Run app e2e tests" took 23:08. PR #55 (`v2-final-docs`, `90bd60e1`), run 44860834473, job 136714018803: 30:46. Both are under 45 minutes, with about 49% and 32% headroom; the slower figure is the one to watch. The 45-minute budget stays and no workflow change is made.

## 2026-11-16 — connect-finish lane: root cause and fix for the hub.test.ts load error on Linux shard 2

- **Root cause (this corrects "Root cause not yet established" in `549b550b` and "Root cause not established" in `ec51507b`).** `packages/engine/src/plugin/tui/runtime.ts` registered OpenTUI's runtime module plugin with `ensureRuntimePluginSupport` from `@opentui/solid` 0.3.4. That plugin is process-global. Its catch-all `onResolve({ filter: /.*/ })` installs an `onLoad` for every file outside `node_modules` resolved after it is registered. That loader rewrites `from "..."` specifiers in the file's raw text, strings and regex literals included (`@opentui/core` `index-r49y8kdq.js`:152-153 and :180). A file whose text names a runtime specifier becomes a "resolve parent" (:302-304), and from then on bare specifiers in every later file are replaced by `import.meta.resolve` results from that parent (:236-253, on for every non-`node_modules` file at :296). In job 133268762868 the shard is the sorted test list taken every 4th file, and its 165 files match the log's headers exactly. `test/cli/tui/plugin-install.test.ts` (35th) imports `runtime.ts`, which registers the plugin. `test/cli/tui/plugin-loader.test.ts` (36th) contains `from "@opentui/keymap/extras"` and `from "@opentui/keymap/solid"` in a template string (:211-212), so it becomes a parent. `test/connect/hub.test.ts` (56th, not 57th) then has `from "ai"` inside the regex literal on line 2082 rewritten to `from "file:///…/node_modules/ai/dist/index.mjs"`. The `/` after `file:` ends the regex, the following `//` comments out the closing `)`, and the parser stops at 2084:9 with `Expected ")" but found "expect"`. The file itself was always fine. The lane's `connect/github-agent.test.ts` and `connect/hands.test.ts` sort before it, which moved it into the same residue mod 4 as plugin-install (140) and plugin-loader (144). On `main` it is at 222, so it never shares their process. Windows shard 10/12 has plugin-install and hub but not plugin-loader, so it has no parent there.
- **Fix.** New `packages/engine/src/plugin/tui/runtime-support.ts` `ensureTuiRuntimePluginSupport()` registers the same Solid transform and the same runtime plugin (Solid, solid-js, solid-js/store, core and the keymap modules), under the same global install key. It hands the upstream `setup` a builder whose catch-all resolver skips any file that resolves inside this checkout's `packages/` directory and outside `node_modules`. Vector's own sources and tests are never text-rewritten and never become resolve parents. Plugin files outside the source tree (user plugin directories, temporary fixtures) keep the rewrite. The exact-specifier resolvers that map runtime specifiers to the shared modules are unchanged. A compiled binary has no `packages/engine/src/...` path, so nothing is excluded there. Resolving inside a resolver re-enters the resolvers, so nested calls are left to Bun's own resolution. A first version of this helper had no guard. Its scope check and the upstream resolver each recursed to the stack limit for every import, and loading `src/session/session.ts` after registration never finished: 7.2 million resolver calls for `decimal.js` in 80 s, with `Maximum call stack size exceeded` in the trace. The upstream plugin alone loads it. `runtime.ts` and `test/plugin/local-sdk.test.ts` (which had called `ensureRuntimePluginSupport` directly) now use the helper. `hub.test.ts` is unchanged. No test was skipped, loosened or moved.
- **Regression test.** `test/plugin/tui-runtime-scope.test.ts` imports `src/plugin/tui/runtime`, then dynamically imports `test/fixture/tui-runtime-scope.ts`. That fixture is a parent and a victim at once: a runtime specifier in a string and a regex literal containing `from "ai"`. The test asserts that both are unchanged. A second case asserts that a plugin file in a temporary directory still has its runtime specifier rewritten. Before the fix, run alone, it fails: the marker comes back as `from "opentui:runtime-module:%40opentui%2Fkeymap%2Fsolid"` and the regex source is cut to `from "file:`. After the fix it passed in 5 of 5 runs.
- **Documented two-file check** (`./` paths keep argument order, per the entry above). `bun test ./test/plugin/tui-runtime-scope.test.ts ./test/connect/hub.test.ts` with `runtime.ts` temporarily restored to the old registration printed the CI error exactly: `test/connect/hub.test.ts:` "Unhandled error between tests … error: Expected ")" but found "expect"" at `hub.test.ts:2084:9`. That log is `/tmp/vector-v2/hub-shard2-fix/pair-before.log`. With the fix: 54 pass, 0 fail in 3 of 3 runs. Single-file runs after the fix: plugin-loader (16 pass), plugin-install (1), plugin-add (3), plugin-lifecycle (4), plugin-loader-entrypoint (8), plugin-loader-pure (1), plugin-toggle (4), local-sdk (12), hub (52), all with 0 fail. `bun typecheck` in packages/engine passed. Still needed: the lead re-runs "engine (linux, shard 2/4)" in CI. This branch also merges `main` `2034e1da`, which adds engine test files, so the shard assignment shifts again. The fix does not depend on the order.

## 2026-11-21 — CW-24: the supervisor refuses engine paths with dot segments

- Gap (the handoff's raw-versus-normalized engine path note, CN-19): `validateEngineCommand` checked routes against
  `url.pathname`, the normalized form the supervisor forwards, while `service.ts` keys session identity on the raw
  `command.path`. `/session/ses_A/../ses_B/prompt_async` was checked as a prompt for `ses_B`'s route shape, keyed as
  `ses_A`, and reached `ses_B`. Only the `/experimental` branch compared the two.
- Fix (`packages/cloud-workspaces/src/protocol.ts`): right after `const path = url.pathname`, refuse when
  `command.path.split("?")[0] !== path`; the `/experimental` branch's own copy of the check is gone. The earlier guards
  (no `%`, `\`, `#`, control or space character, a single leading `/`) leave `.` and `..` segments as the only way the
  two forms differ, so no valid route is lost.
- Test (`packages/cloud-workspaces/test/protocol.test.ts`, "closed commands reject URL, ref and engine
  routing/permission substitutions"): `/session/ses_A/../ses_B/prompt_async` and `.../message_id` with valid bodies,
  `/session/ses_A/./abort`, `/session/./status`, `/session/ses_A/message/..`, `/./session?roots=true` and
  `/provider/../session` throw the invalid-request error; the canonical prompt, message id, abort, status, roots list
  and session create still pass. Red on the old source (6 pass, 1 fail: "Received function did not throw"). The
  optional `service.test.ts` case was not added: the refusal is in the parser `service.ts` runs before any runtime call.
- Pilot activation and `VECTOR_WORKSPACES_ENABLED` are unchanged (off).

  `formatter-append.js` fixture) appends "formatted" to each `.fmt` file it formats. For `build`, edit, write and

  the old tools (3 pass, 3 fail: the three `connect_fix` cases). Language servers are not exercised by the test: the
  guard on `touchFile` sits beside the formatter's in the same tools.
- Local note: `test/tool/write.test.ts` "throws error when OS denies write access" fails when run as root, as in this
  container (chmod 0444 does not stop root); unrelated and unchanged.

## 2026-11-21 — C6: a bound key whose receipt is gone answers consumed (part 1 of 2)

- Gap: `owner.ts`'s bound branch answered a key whose original run could not be replayed (its receipt pruned after a
  done run, CN-09) with `invalid`, so the app kept the marker and the key stayed stuck. A binding means the key already
  ran a review, so the answer is now `consumed`, with the same message ("…its original owned run is unavailable. No new
  review was started."). The app (CN-10) already handles a typed 400 consumed by dropping that marker and offering
  "Start a new review", which mints a new key only when clicked.
- Test: `packages/engine/test/connect/directory.test.ts` "a consumed key whose receipt was retired cannot create a
  replacement review" now asserts the code and message, not only a failure; it still checks one job, one anchor and one
  model call. Red on the old code ("Received: invalid"). `test/connect/owner.test.ts` (7 pass) and
  `test/server/httpapi-connect.test.ts` (12 pass) are unchanged.
- Not done (part 2, left for the morning list): returning a bound member-gate refusal as a 200 refused run instead of
  400 invalid. It needs a fixture that reaches the hub's member gate after the binding, which the current tests do not
  build.
- 21 Nov 2026, later the same day: part 2 done, same branch. See "2026-11-21 — C6: a bound key's member-gate refusal
  answers its refused run (part 2 of 2)" below.

## 2026-11-21 — C6: a bound key's member-gate refusal answers its refused run (part 2 of 2)

- Finding first. Through the real hub a bound key's member-gate refusal already answers 200: the gate runs inside the
  job the binding gate released, so `hub.run` hands back the queued run and the refusal lands later, and every retry
  replays that refused run. A new check in the test below pins this (the start answers `queued`, the run ends
  `refused`/`unavailable`). The 400 `invalid` answer is reached only when the hub returns a run that is already done
  for a bound key, which its detach path does when the run ended before it reads it back. That start then said
  `invalid` while every retry of the same key replayed a 200 refused run.
- Fix: `packages/engine/src/connect/owner.ts` maps a done refused run to 400 `invalid` only when the key is not bound
  (`reached.bind !== "bound"`). A refusal before the binding gate still answers `invalid`, tombstones the key, and a
  retry answers `consumed`, as `owner.test.ts` pins. A bound key answers the refused run, which is what its replay
  answers.
- Test: `packages/engine/test/connect/directory.test.ts` "a key bound before the member gate refused keeps the refused
  run, as its replay answers it". The fixture member can now describe itself `unavailable` with a reason. The real hub
  is wrapped (as the file already wraps the real ledger) so that a test can make `run` answer only once the run ended.
  It checks the summary "VectorScope is not ready: the fixture member is offline", that an upper-case retry equals the
  first answer, one job per anchor, no model call and no pending ask. Red on the old owner ("error: VectorScope is not
  ready…", code `invalid`), green after: directory 24 pass, owner 7 pass, httpapi-connect 12 pass, engine typecheck
  clean. Mutation: with the guard inverted (`=== "bound"`), this case and owner.test's "the hub refuses before the
  binding gate tombstones its key" case both go red. No hub, policy or adapter source changed; the review pin is
  untouched.

## 2026-11-21 — CW-21: a non-ready tool-reached member keeps its own reason

- Supersedes the CN-11 review round's "Not taken, by decision" (5) and the "`not-installed` keeps reach `none`"
  follow-up: when the registry does not offer a tool-reached adapter's tool, `members.ts` `registered()` now keeps the
  member's own state and reason (reach `none`, since there is nothing to reach) whenever the adapter reported it not
  ready. Hands' `unavailable` with `ComputerAvailability.message(reason)` therefore says why `computer` is absent, for
  example on a CLI or TUI client without the bridge variables. Only a ready member whose tool is missing reads
  `not-installed`, "Not in this build." The "left out without tool ids" branch is unchanged. Flagged for the lead, since
  it reverses a recorded decision; the fix plan asked for it.
- Tests: `packages/engine/test/connect/hands.test.ts` "Hands keeps its own reason when the registry does not offer
  computer (CW-21)" runs every reason in `REASONS` with tools `["read"]` and expects state `unavailable`, Hands' own
  message and reach `none`, one probe per list; red on the old `registered()` (9 pass, 1 fail). The ready case still
  reads "Not in this build." `test/connect/hub.test.ts`'s compose case adds a non-ready Hands member without its tool
  and expects its state and reason kept (52 pass). Spec §6's Hands row says so.

## 2026-11-27 — CW-23c: the browser spec covers the phone's connect card, a fix's question and connect off

- Closes the browser-spec part of CN-18's Held list (entry "CW-23c: the phone shows connect cards and runs, and
  starts a review with a passkey (CN-18)", Held, now marked done there). PR #105 had already added "a paired phone
  starts and cancels a connected branch review". This adds the three cases it left out to
  `packages/app/e2e/regression/remote-phone.spec.ts`, on the same harness (the desktop's real link and remote-routes,
  the memory relay and Chromium's virtual authenticator):
  - "an agent's connected review shows on the phone as a card read from the engine's run, as text only": the
    session's `connect` tool part keeps a saved copy that still says running, and the card shows the engine's run:
    "Done · Blocking findings", "Code sent to: Your provider: anthropic", "7 open findings: 1 blocking, 5 concerns,
    1 nit", the first five open finding lines by severity (a title holding `<b>` stays text), and "The other findings
    are in Vector on your computer." The sixth open, fixed and dismissed findings are absent; the card has no button and
    no fix, dismiss or worktree text; nothing marked PRIVATE (finding bodies and suggestions, the summary, the tool
    input, the saved progress) reaches the phone. The runs list shows the same run with no Cancel.
  - "a connected tool's fix asks on the phone with its change as text, and is allowed once with a passkey": a
    `connect_write` ask in the hub's shape (engine connect/hub.ts `asker`: description, runID, member, diff, empty
    `always`) shows in the session page's Connected tools section as the sentence and the patch in a `<pre>`, with
    Allow once and Deny and no "always". Allow once raises the passkey's signCount, the fake engine hears exactly
    `{reply: "once"}`, and the card goes.
  - "with connected tools off the phone shows no card and no runs, and a review sent straight through is refused":
    with the capabilities read lacking `connections`, the same part is only its plain line ("Review with VectorScope ·
    running"), there is no card, no Connected tools section and no review form. Those checks run only after the page
    has the capabilities answer: the case sends the same read again through the phone client, and it answers after the
    page's own on the one link (review round, below). A review challenge sent straight through the phone client
    (`remoteFixture.phone`) answers 403 `REMOTE_CONNECT_OFF`, and so does a runs read; the fake engine receives no
    connect request. The challenge is the step a start begins with; the start route itself checks the passkey before
    connect off, so an unsigned start would be refused for its missing signature instead.
- Fixture (`packages/app/e2e/fixtures/remote-phone.jsx`): a new `card=1` page parameter adds the finished run
  `cr_phone_card` to `GET /experimental/connect/runs` (only with `connect=1`) and answers
  `GET /session/ses_1/message` with one assistant message holding a text part and the `connect` tool part. A new
  `remoteFixture.askFix(id)` raises the `connect_write` ask. Both default off, so codium-remote-helpers.spec.ts and
  the three earlier cases see the same engine. `pair()` in the spec now takes page parameters instead of a boolean.
- Deviation: the plan asked to import the shapes from the unit tests. A browser fixture cannot import a `bun:test`
  file, so the run and finding shapes are written in the fixture as phone/remote/model.test.ts builds them. A scratch
  check decoded `cardRun` and the part's saved copy with the real `Connect.Run` schema (packages/schema/src/connect.ts)
  before commit. It caught that model.test.ts's finding uses category `correctness` and bucket `owned`, which
  `Connect.Category` and `Connect.Bucket` refuse; the fixture uses `bug` and `inline`. The phone's reader ignores both
  fields, so model.test.ts is left as it is.
- Workflow: connect-app-validation.yml's Playwright line now also runs `e2e/regression/remote-phone.spec.ts`.
- Not here: streaming `connect.run.updated` (its own item); the cases use Refresh or a fresh read and never expect a
  live run row change. The card reads the runs list; the fixture's one-run read (`GET /experimental/connect/runs/:id`)
  is PR #110's, for its detached review hold, and is kept as it landed.
- Evidence (local only, Chromium 1243 from the machine's preinstalled Playwright browsers through a scratch config
  that sets `executablePath`; no CI run yet): before the fixture change the three new cases failed and the three
  earlier ones passed; after it all six passed, and codium-remote-helpers.spec.ts passed 2 of 2. With
  `SHOWN = 6` in phone/remote/model.ts and the desktop's connect-off check removed, the card case and the connect-off
  case failed.
- Review round (2026-11-27).

    They waited only for the plain tool line, which shows while the capabilities read is still out. Moving the

## 2026-11-27 — CW-24: result wording inside a cloud workspace, unpinned part (CN-15 Held, partly)

- Built from `origin/main` `6349f042`. This is the part of CN-15's held "Wording inside a cloud workspace" that sits
  outside the VectorScope review pin. Without the supervisor's marker every string below is unchanged, byte for byte.
- **Core `connect/frame.ts`.** `describeSendsTo` and `describeCost` take an optional `Where` (`{ cloudWorkspace? }`),
  and `frame()` takes it in its options. In a cloud workspace `this-computer` reads "this Vector cloud workspace (on
  Vector's servers)", a model server reads "your model server X" with no "(local)", and a local-kind cost reads "no
  charge" with no "local model". Omitted or false, the old words.
- **Engine `tool/connect.ts`.** The tool already yields `RuntimeFlags.Service`; it now passes `flags.cloudWorkspace`
  to the review result's frame, the `findings` header and `status`'s `describe()`.
- **Engine `tool/connect.txt`.** The destination list reads "(the machine this engine runs on, the user's model
  server, a model provider they configured, or Vector Codium)" instead of "(this computer, …)".
- **App `features/connect/task-rows.ts`.** The Tasks pane row passes its member's place to `destinationLabel`, so a
  run in a cloud workspace reads "This Vector cloud workspace" (a run's place follows its member, per CN-15). With
  `place: "local"`, or a member the registry does not know, it still reads "This computer", so
  connect-app-validation's Tasks pane case (`connected-tools.spec.ts`, member at `place: "local"`) is unaffected.
- **Hands `computer/availability.ts` (Hands' file, the reword the plan allows).** The guest reason is now
  place-neutral: "A guest is in this conversation, and only the owner can let Vector Hands use their apps." It was
  "Only the owner of this computer can let Vector use it." The Hands spec row (`hands-build-spec.md` :198) follows.
  The desktop app's own copy (`computer.availability.guest` in app `i18n/en.ts`) is shown on the user's computer and
  is not changed.
- **Not changed, and why.**
  - App `findings-card.tsx`, `directory-review.tsx` and the phone's `phone/remote/model.ts` still call
    `destinationLabel` without a place. None of them has the member list (the run carries no place), and none is
    reachable from a cloud workspace today: the supervisor's proxy (cloud-workspaces `protocol.ts`
    `validateEngineCommand`, CN-19) refuses a run read with a query, and the card reads its run with `sessionID`; it
    refuses the sessionless folder review; and the phone talks only to a paired computer's engine. Giving them a
    place would add a member read each for no reachable case. If a cloud-workspace page ever shows them, they need
    the member's place first.
  - Pinned VectorScope review sources, left for the VectorScope owner (`REVIEW_PIPELINE_FILES`; open PRs #162, #163,
    #167, #168 and #170 each change `adapters/vectorscope.ts` and the `REVIEW_PIPELINE_SOURCES` digest, so a repin
    now would collide): core `connect/policy.ts` `place()`, the `connect` permission text, the `connect_cloud`
    consent's "It leaves this computer" and `localGate`'s your-cloud reason; engine `adapters/vectorscope.ts`'s
    triage note ("which sent the diff to …"), which reaches a result's notes; and engine `connect/hub.ts`'s frame of
    a run and its background note (`ConnectNote`), so a background review's note still says "this computer
    (local)". They need `cloudWorkspace` threaded from the hub and one `REVIEW_PIPELINE_SOURCES` repin, after the
    VectorScope owner agrees whether `REVIEW_PIPELINE_REVISION` stays.
  - Engine `connect/owner.ts`'s frame of an owner-started review (it already holds `flags`) is not in the plan and
    has no framed-output test; it is the next unpinned site.
- **Tests.** Core `test/connect/frame.test.ts` "in a cloud workspace nothing says this computer or local, and
  without it nothing changes": 12 pass; red on the old `frame.ts` (11 pass, 1 fail). Engine
  `test/tool/connect.test.ts`: the registry case asserts the description never says "this computer", and "in a cloud
  workspace a loopback review says where it ran without this computer or local" runs review, `findings` and
  `status` with the marker and expects the cloud words and no `/this computer|local/i`: 26 pass; red with the old
  `tool/connect.ts` and `connect.txt` (24 pass, 2 fail). Engine `test/computer/availability.test.ts` pins the new
  guest copy and checks that no reason says "this computer": 32 pass; red on the old copy (30 pass, 2 fail).
  `test/connect/hands.test.ts` 10 pass. App `features/connect/task-rows.test.ts` "in a Vector cloud workspace a run
  on the engine's own machine never reads this computer (CW-24)": 16 pass; red on the old `task-rows.ts` (15 pass,
  1 fail). Every run was one file, `--timeout 30000`.
- The pilot stays off; no flag changes.
